# PR11 — Tokio coordinator, PermitBroker, slots, path leases and closure under concurrency: the working record

The record of the eleventh slice of upstroke v0.2, kept on the branch so that a successor session
inherits what was decided and why. Written before the work and updated as each piece lands, so
that at any commit on this branch the record is true. It is **not** a design document: `DESIGN.md`
and the packet stay the authority, and a sentence here that disagrees with either is a defect in
this file.

**Branch:** `feature/pr11-tokio-coordinator-and-permit-broker`, cut from master at
`79979d2403701a44a2c679947e46eca6c7aa4995` — the merge commit of pull request #326, the tree after
G5 (`reviews/2026-09-25-gate-G5.md`, "G5 passes on the range `d724fb16…`") plus #322, #324, #325
and #326.

**Where this file lives, and why.** As the PR9 and PR10 records (`reviews/2026-09-08-pr9-record.md`,
`reviews/2026-09-12-pr10-record.md`): a slice's working record does not belong at the repository
root, and `docs/internals/` holds only module notes, so it sits beside the gate reports in
`reviews/`, dated by its first commit.

**Who writes it.** The slice is phased by its orchestrator (`orch_pr11`) and implemented by one
fresh session per phase group. Phases 0 and 1 were written by `pr11_impl_a` (`claude-opus-5-5`,
`max`), phase 2 by `pr11_impl_b`, phase 3 by `pr11_impl_c` and phase 4 by `pr11_impl_d` (the same
model and effort); the
handover of each session is `~/orch-pr11/handovers/<agent>.md` on the build box, and every figure
below is in a saved file under `~/orch-pr11/logs/<agent>/` that the sentence names (`pr11_impl_a`
for phases 0 and 1, `pr11_impl_b` for phase 2, `pr11_impl_c` for phase 3, `pr11_impl_d` for phase
4). Round R1, the repair of the early review's findings (§13), is `pr11_repair_r1`'s (the same model
and effort), its figures under `~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_repair_r1/`; round R2, the repair of review round
2's findings (§13), is `pr11_repair_r2`'s (the same model and effort), its figures under
`~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_repair_r2/`; phase 5 is `pr11_impl_e`'s (the same model and effort), its figures under
`~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_impl_e/`; round R3, the repair of review round 3's findings (§13), is `pr11_repair_r3`'s (the
same model and effort), its figures under `~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_repair_r3/`; phase 6 is `pr11_impl_f`'s (the same model and effort), its figures under
`~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_impl_f/`; phase 7, the close-out, with review round 4's one finding repaired first (§13), is
`pr11_impl_g`'s (the same model and effort), its figures under `~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_impl_g/`; round R5, the repair of
the full review's six findings (§13), is `pr11_repair_r5`'s (the same model and effort), its figures under
`~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_repair_r5/`. Phase 3's baseline
design is the orchestrator's research memo `~/orch-pr11/research/phase3-coordinator-design.md`
(written by `pr11_research_c` at `6b7a4836`, read-only; cited below as "memo §n"), with the
orchestrator's dispositions on its open questions; where the code at phase 2's head differs from
what the memo cites, the code won and the reading says so.

## 1. What PR11 is, from the packet

The packet is `~/tactus-artifacts/tactus-parallel-design-neutral-v16.json` (sha256
`8b3991c804d74c91a0fe162304b68249991d4104338bed8647da88ec5da50813`, read with Python; the
sections this record cites are extracted beside their hashes in
`~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_impl_a/phase0/packet-sections/`, `SHA256SUMS`). PR11 is
`.decisions.pr_sequence[]` with `.id == "PR11"`.

- **title:** Tokio coordinator, PermitBroker with InvocationIds, slots (incl. agent probes),
  provisional-reservation ledger, path leases, run-end closure under concurrency
- **scope, verbatim:** "spawned pipelines with typed completions bound to identities incl. retry
  attempts; Runner async surface; PermitBroker (fold-derived entitlements incl. fast prepared and
  retry re-entry, provisional reservations, agent/pool slots incl. agent probes, invocation ledger
  incl. non-slotted gate and shell-probe invocations) with head-reservation FIFO, cancel/complete
  protocol; halt cancels in-flight pipelines and budget stop drains them within the closure
  procedure (a halting drain settlement converts to Halted; deferred items never block either);
  budget_exceeded appended before any budget-driven end under concurrency; an append error under
  concurrency cancels in-flight pipelines, discards completions, and ends the command under the
  append-error protocol; dead-owner and dead-incarnation container reclaim before slot reset under
  concurrency; seam tests; > 1 under TopologyPreview only; no event/merge/recovery/runner-identity
  changes"
- **production_effect:** none. **gating:** "interleaving tests at 3 incl. runtime pool tests;
  deadlock-freedom; ST-01..ST-18; module diff proof". **cumulative_gate_after:** G6.

The **slice contract**, field by field:

| field | text |
|---|---|
| `frozen_range` | recorded at review |
| `invariants_introduced` | INV-18 (broker portion), INV-20 (runtime broker enforcement), INV-21, INV-22 (process-local/broker rows R3, R4, R13, R17, R22, R28 and R19/R26 under concurrency) |
| `invariants_preserved` | fold, queue, merge, repair, recovery, derived outcome, finalization, and runner-identity modules byte-identical to PR10; every INV-01..INV-19 and INV-23 |
| `permitted_transitions` | none new; scheduling only |
| `owned_resources` | R1/R2 entitlements (fold-derived); R13; R3 incl. agent probes; R4 ledger for every Runner process incl. the shell probe; R17, R28 (observed), R19, R22, R26 under concurrency; completions bound to identities |
| `side_effect_vs_event_ordering` | unchanged |
| `cancellation` | shutdown or halt cancels pending requests, cancels/releases granted and non-slotted invocations after termination (processes and containers), cancels provisional reservations, discards completions; halt runs the closure procedure before `run_finished(Halted)`; budget stop drains and converts to Halted on a halting settlement; a returned append error cancels in-flight pipelines and ends the command without folding anything from memory; resume performs the recovery order incl. dead-owner and dead-incarnation container reclaim; coordinator ledgers empty at restart |
| `durable_events` | none new |
| `replay_recovery` | unchanged |
| `expected_failures_refusals` | stale/duplicate/out-of-order completions discarded or refused; partial slot pair impossible; duplicate complete/cancel/release ignored and counted; `max_parallel > 1` still refused in production; any completion applied after the fold is poisoned by an append error |
| `proof_tests` | ST-01..ST-06, ST-08, ST-09 (process-local rows incl. R28 observation), ST-10, ST-13, ST-15, ST-16, ST-17, ST-18 under concurrency; permutation projections; deadlock-freedom under adversarial orders; runtime pool tests incl. agent probes and broker-only tests, gate and shell-probe invocations registered without slots; halt cancellation and budget drain tests incl. halting conversion, `budget_exceeded`-before-end, and deferred items under both closures; `append_error_under_concurrency_cancels_pipelines_and_folds_nothing_from_memory` |
| `non_goals` | production > 1; any authority change; invocation lifecycle events |

The sections the contract names, read in full (`packet-sections/`): `admission_and_leases`
(`ready`, `ready_retry`, `permits.{pipeline, merge, provisional_reservations, agent_pool_slots,

**The named tests and seam rows PR11 is measured against** (the whole list; §7 says where each
will live):

- **Seam rows under concurrency:** ST-01 stale completion; ST-02 duplicate completion and
  duplicate slot/invocation operation; ST-03 out-of-order completions; ST-04 ownership aliasing
  (PR11's portion); ST-05 double release (entitlements/slots/provisional); ST-06
  candidate/generation/attempt mismatch; ST-08 sequential-versus-parallel trace equivalence;
  ST-09 resource accounting (process-local rows R17/R22/R28 and the broker rows); ST-10
  reconstruction; ST-13 provisional reservation lifecycle (ledger under concurrency); ST-15
  same-generation retry under concurrency; ST-16 container crash ownership (PR11's "under
  concurrency" portion); ST-17 run-end closure and derived outcome (concurrent); ST-18 terminal
  finalization.
- **Fault-row tests the contract's rows name for PR11's portions:** T-DISPATCH
  `open_no_attempt_closed_at_run_end`; T-ATTEMPT `halt_cancels_in_flight_attempt`,
  `kill_during_retry_attempt_closes_generation`; T-RETRY `same_generation_retry_regates_cumulative_tree`,
  `retry_refused_after_resume`, `retry_refused_with_stale_incarnation`,
  `retained_worktree_with_residue_closed_not_retried`; T-CONTAINER `orphan_reclaimed_before_slot_reset`,
  `live_owner_untouched_while_dead_orphan_reclaimed`, `concurrent_reclaimers_converge`,
  `schema4_probe_container_owned_during_preflight_untouched_by_foreign_census`,
  `unix_reaper_kills_labeled_containers`, `windows_orphan_window_documented` (the rest of
  T-CONTAINER is PR6/PR7's and G2's); T-APPEND `append_error_never_triggers_cleanup_or_report_from_memory`,
  `poisoned_fold_refuses_every_later_transition`, `resume_after_append_error_follows_surviving_prefix`
  under concurrency, plus the contract's own `append_error_under_concurrency_cancels_pipelines_and_folds_nothing_from_memory`;
  T-FINISH `kill_inside_closure_recovers`, `append_error_inside_closure_ends_command_and_resume_completes_closure`,
  `run_finished_budget_exceeded_refused_after_halting_drain_settlement`,
  `run_finished_halted_and_budget_exceeded_accepted_with_deferred_items`,
  `over_budget_prefix_without_budget_exceeded_is_not_ending` at width 3.
- **Acceptance subset**, item 2: "max_parallel = 3 (post PR11): two independent tasks overlap,
  merge through one queue, dependent starts from a head containing both; disjoint hints permit
  and overlapping/absent hints serialize dispatch"; item 3: "slot tests (runtime-reachable): same
  agent and pool with opposing limits serialize on the binding limit; two agents with own pools
  run in parallel; agent without pool uses the agent slot only; agent probes acquire and release
  their pair; gate and shell-probe invocations register without slots; broker-only shared-pool
  tests labeled".
- **G6's adversarial fault tests** (what the gate after this slice will run against it): the ST
  rows above under interleaving; seeded permutations with identical canonical projections and
  deadlock-freedom under adversarial completion orders and reduced limits; shutdown with in-flight
  pipelines; halt with in-flight pipelines (cancellation, `attempt_interrupted` per attempt,
  `generation_closed{RunEnding(Halted)}`, promotions and authorized publications completed,
  deferred items void, `run_finished(Halted)`); budget stop (`budget_exceeded` before any
  budget-driven end, drain, halting conversion refusing `run_finished(BudgetExceeded)`, a budget
  stop with Deferred tasks ending BudgetExceeded and the resume waking them); an injected append
  error with pipelines in flight; runtime pool tests with opposing limits incl. agent probe
  registration, two agents with own pools, agent without pool, gate and shell-probe invocations
  unslotted, broker-only shared-pool tests labelled; "cancelled head request recomputes head
  reservation; duplicate cancel ignored and counted". Its required artifacts: seam-test outputs
  ST-01..ST-20, the permutation projection equivalence report, slot/invocation/provisional/
  entitlement/container ledger outputs, and "diff proof that fold/queue/merge/repair/recovery
  modules are unchanged from G5".

**The errata** (`~/tactus-artifacts/2026-08-25-g2-pass-errata.md`, sha256
`eabbb6b7db60293e58e850b370030c0921256d7704f312244e00d908e2e5886d`,
`phase0/identity.log`) win over the packet where they conflict. **None of E1–E6 changes PR11's
contract**, read one by one: E1 is the fold's validation of a recorded predicted region (PR7's
`task_dispatched`, a fold rule PR11 consumes unchanged); E2 is the override binding's five fields
(PR9); E3 gives the Process group a probe semantic context (PR4's inventory; PR11 registers probes
in the broker without changing the site); E4 is the attempt allowance rule (engine-side at PR7,
derived not carried — PR11 adds no wire field and no allowance function); E5 extends G2's range;
E6 moves T-CAND-REF's boundary to the settlement (PR7's recovery). One observation, not a
conflict: the errata name the packet copy they were written against as sha256
`02bfed758c72a0ccdd91c11a6aaf9a59683a6e1f914356ab0153ec341a455df6`, and the box's copy the
orchestrator and this record read hashes to `8b3991c8…`; both are "v16", and nothing PR11 reads
from the packet is contradicted by any erratum.

## 2. What the tree has at `79979d24`, measured by reading

**The width-1 ledgers PR11 replaces.** `src/engine/topology/identity.rs`: `SlotAssertion`
(`:113-118`, one held pair, a second `acquire` refused naming "at max_parallel = 1 the substrate
asserts a single slotted invocation rather than queueing", `:150-160`), `is_slotted`
(`:120-127`, attempt and sequence roles slotted except `Gate(_)`, probes slotted only for
`Agent(_)`), `SlotPair` (`:129-133`, `{agent, pool: Option}`), `Reservations` (`:241-247`, at
most one held, `take`/`convert`/`cancel`/`cancel_any`, `entitlements_held` 1 or 2 by kind),
`InvocationLedger` (`:348-352`, `register` refuses a second registration, `complete`/`cancel`
settle once and count a duplicate settlement, `cancel_all_running`, `balances`). The same
predicate is stated on the role in `src/runner/contract.rs:107-113`
(`ExecutionRole::is_slotted`). **Where they are held:** `TopologyRun`
(`src/engine/topology/run.rs:603-615`: `reservations`, `invocations`, `slots`); the attempt
(`attempt.rs:388-389`, `:756-757`); pre-flight (`preflight.rs:27-36`, `RunPreflight`'s `ledger`
and `slots` behind `Mutex`es); run creation's `ProbePair` (`create.rs`, `grant` and the
`ShellProbe`/`AgentProbe` capabilities). **And through a frozen type:** `EmitContext`
(`src/engine/topology/recover.rs:1392-1399`) carries `&mut Reservations` and `&mut
InvocationLedger`, built at `recover.rs:969-970`; `recover.rs` is in the frozen set (§5), so
phase 2 evolves these two types **in place in `identity.rs`** (which is not frozen) and keeps their
names and `new()`, rather than putting new types into `EmitContext`.

**The Runner trait and every implementor.** `pub trait Runner: Send + Sync { fn run(&self,
request: &RunnerRequest) -> Result<ProcessOutput, RunnerError>; }` (`src/runner/contract.rs:243-245`),
object safety held by `the_runner_trait_is_object_safe` (`:450-457`). Implementors at the base
(`phase0/runner-census.log`): production — `HostRunner` (`src/runner/host.rs:202`),
`ContainerRunner` (`src/runner/container/exec.rs:775`), and three decorators that forward to an
inner runner: `Registering` (`src/engine/topology/preflight.rs:173`, register → slot → run →
settle), `ShellProbe` (`src/engine/topology/create.rs:288`) and `AgentProbe` (`:304`); test
doubles — `ScriptedRunner` (`src/gates.rs:794`), `FatedRunner` and `RecordingRunner`
(`src/review.rs:1147`, `:1577`), `Boundary` (`src/agent/adapter.rs:519`), `RecordingRunner`
(`src/agent/codex.rs:1354`), `RecordingRunner`, `FailsTheSecondRequest`, `RefusesEveryRequest`
(`src/engine/topology/create/tests.rs:3255`, `:3280`, `:3944`), `Recording`
(`src/engine/topology/preflight/tests.rs:59`), `RecordingRunner` (`src/engine/topology/scaffold.rs:461`,
the substrate's double), `StubRunner` (`src/runner/host/tests.rs:2182`), `RecordingRunner`
(`src/engine/tests.rs:8551`), and four in `src/engine/topology/recover/tests.rs` (`:852`, `:3952`,
`:8692`, `:16975`).

**Every production caller of `Runner::run`.** The legacy (schema 1–3) engine: the worker
(`src/engine/attempt.rs:110`), the gates (`src/gates.rs:147`, reached from `gates::run_all`), the
reviews (`src/review.rs:547`); the adapters' `probe` and `discover` (`src/agent/claude.rs:53`,
`:87`, `:118`; `src/agent/copilot.rs:51`, `:85`; `src/agent/codex.rs:90`, `:124`, `:131`, `:141`,
`:176`, `:186`, `:387`), which the legacy pre-flight and the topology pre-flight both call
through a `&dyn Runner`; the host shell probe (`src/runner/host/probe.rs:31`). The schema-4
substrate: the attempt's `run_registered` (`src/engine/topology/attempt.rs:986`), which every
worker and gate request goes through; the decorators' forwarding calls
(`preflight.rs:218`, `create.rs:290`, `:318`). **Review passes do not go through
`run_registered`**: `attempt.rs:865` hands the review seam the raw `self.runner`, and the pass's
invocations are registered and completed in the ledger only after it returns
(`attempt.rs:894-899`) — no slot pair is held while a reviewer runs, which is
`PR7-R3-ATTEMPT-002-REVIEWERS-TAKE-NO-SLOT` (§6).

**The hooks lock that serializes a runner.** `HostRunner` holds its observer as
`hooks: Mutex<Box<dyn SpawnHooks + Send>>` (`host.rs:81`) and takes the lock for the whole
supervised run (`host.rs:220-221`); `ContainerRunner` does the same (`exec.rs:781`). Production
installs `NoHooks`, and the lock is still taken, so two invocations on one runner run one after
the other whatever the scheduler asks — the observation `docs/internals/runner/host.md`
("`HostRunner::hooks`") records as PR11's.

**Closure's refusals.** `closure::refuse_unclosable` (`src/engine/topology/closure.rs:37-50`)
refuses, before any append, a closure that finds an in-flight generation, a promoting one or an
unresolved transaction (`unclosable`, `:52`), naming PR11: "Closure under concurrency (in-flight
cancellation, the budget drain, promotion and publication completion inside closure) is PR11's;
nothing was appended". `ending_outcome` (`:11`) reads halt before budget.

**The sequential loop.** `TopologyRun::step` (`run.rs:691-745`) enters the run's cleanup scope
(`:696`), ingests answers, calls `select` (`src/engine/topology/select.rs:217`) and `checkpoint`
(`:272`), and runs exactly one `Admitted` branch — `BudgetExceeded`, `Backoff`, `Integrate`,
`Retry`, `Dispatch`/`RepairDispatch`, `HardBlock`, `Closure` (`run.rs:704-744`) — to completion
before returning. Selection order is eligible integration, then `ready_retry`, then dispatch
(`sequential_substrate.eligibility_order`). The seams are `RunSeams` (`run.rs:466-479`,
`runner: &dyn Runner`, `reviews: &dyn ReviewPasses`, …).

**The integration transaction** (frozen, §5). `integrate()` (`src/engine/topology/integrate.rs:540-554`)
decides the exact base and runs either the fast path (`prepare_fast`, `publish`) or
`integrate_stale` (`:576`), whose clean and already-present arms call `start_and_verify`
(`:672`): append `merge_verification_started` (`:684`) → `journal.verify(..)` (`:696`, the
`Verification` seam, `:47-51`) → append `merge_prepared`/`merge_rejected`/`merge_verification_unavailable`
→ `publish` (the CAS) → `task_merged` — one synchronous chain. The substrate's seam implementation
is `IntegrationCx` (`run.rs:98-190`), whose `verify` maps a Runner error with fate `Gone` to an
infrastructure outage (`run.rs:151-170`).

**The pre-flight probes.** `RunPreflight` (`preflight.rs:27-36`) probes the recorded shell
(non-slotted) and one slotted probe per recorded agent through `Registering`; run creation grants
`ProbePair` capabilities whose `ShellProbe` refuses a slotted invocation and whose `AgentProbe`
refuses an unslotted one (`create.rs:280-321`).

**`max_parallel`.** `DEFAULT_MAX_PARALLEL = 1` (`src/config.rs:282`); a fresh run's
`max_parallel > 1` is refused and a resume's is warned and ignored (`src/config/parse.rs:502-532`),
pinned by `config::tests::max_parallel_above_one_is_refused_rather_than_read_past`
(`src/config.rs:1935`). The fold refuses a `run_started` recording `max_parallel = 0`
(`src/topology/fold/start.rs:41`).

**`WriterSelector::TopologyPreview`.** `src/topology/schema.rs:45-48`; `fresh_writer_schema`
gives production `LATEST_LEGACY_SCHEMA` and the preview `TOPOLOGY_SCHEMA` (`:59-64`), and the
const assertions keep the topology inactive and the readable schema at 3 (`:39-42`). Run creation
takes its `RunStarted4` record from the caller (`create.rs:1525`, the `record` field of `create::Request`); the
scaffold's fixture records `max_parallel: 1` (`scaffold.rs:139`).

**Thread-scoped state on the spawn path** (`phase0/thread-scope.log`): the run's cleanup-lease
registry is a `thread_local!` (`src/rundir/cleanup/scopes.rs:28-29`), entered by
`RunHandle::cleanup_scope` on the stepping thread (`run.rs:696`) and read by the Unix reaper when
the process funnel starts it (`src/agent/proc.rs:2466`, `spawn_reaper` →
`rundir::active_cleanup_lease_paths`); the test observation export keys its record on the
current thread's name (`src/observations.rs:156-157`).

**Measured anew for phase 2** (by reading, at the phase-1 head `6b7a4836`, whose frozen files are
the base's). *R1 and R2 are already the fold's*: `GenerationClass::holds_pipeline`
(`src/topology/fold.rs:360-365`) is `OpenNoAttempt | InFlight | Promoting`, and
`RunState::pipeline_held` (`:606-615`) counts those generations plus one for an open transaction —
exactly `permits.pipeline`'s set, a transaction being unresolved while it is open in any class —
and `TopologyFold::transaction()` is `Some` exactly while one is unresolved (`permits.merge`). *The
slot limits are not recorded*: `TopologyLimits` (`src/topology/events.rs:360`) holds `max_parallel`,
`max_defers` and `max_merge_repairs`; `max_per_agent`/`max_per_pool` are configuration
(`src/config.rs:375-377`, `src/config/parse.rs`, refused at zero, default the configured
`max_parallel`). *A frozen census names every production module that names the fold*:
`FOLD_MENTIONS` in `src/events/log/tests.rs:2799` (21 paths), checked by
`the_stable_prefix_barrier_is_the_only_way_a_log_becomes_a_topology_fold` (R-N). *A frozen test
names the old slot type*: `src/engine/topology/recover/tests.rs:3938`,
`identity::SlotAssertion::new().is_empty()` (R-Q). *The packet settles two test questions in its
alternatives*: `alternatives[74]` rejects strict head-of-line FIFO ("blocks disjoint work; head
reservation adopted") and `alternatives[21]` makes two-agents-one-pool and one-agent-two-pools
"broker-only tests labeled". *The width-1 fast path is already witnessed*: the frozen
`integrate::tests::fast_dual_holding_released_once` (`src/engine/topology/integrate/tests.rs:215`)
takes an integration reservation, converts it at `merge_prepared(fast)` through
`IntegrationJournal::converted` and sees both derived holdings until `task_merged`.

**Measured anew for phase 3** (by reading, at phase 2's head `d4217490`; where a fact bound the
design, the reading that follows it is named). *The manager reads the worktree registry in almost
every funnel*: `revalidate()` runs `git worktree list`, and the removal gate refuses a registration
that is `locked` with no `gitdir` — an add in flight (R-X, measured by the race log there). *The
snapshot constructors have a second production caller*, `runner/container/exec.rs`'s namespace
representative (R-Y). *Three censuses fence the shape of the change*: `run::tests` reads `run.rs`'s
production region and pins one `select(` and one `checkpoint(` call and a `?` after every
`self.emit(` (R-T); `events::log::tests`' `FOLD_MENTIONS` lists the production modules that may
name the fold, and the new coordinator module is not on it (R-N, R-AA); the effect census
classifies every reachable function of `src/workspace_manager.rs` and `src/agent/proc.rs`, so a new
one there would move an instrument (R-X, R-Z). *The frozen test child is `step`'s only driver*
(R-T), and *the Runner's future supervises in its first poll* (R-I), so whatever thread polls it is
occupied for the process's life (R-R).

## 3. Readings

Each reading: the packet sentence, the code fact, the reading, the alternative, and why. A
reading that would need an authority change, contradict the packet's literal text, or edit an
instrument would be a question; none of these is.

### R-A — production effect none, and the legacy engine

**Packet.** `sequential_substrate.runner`: "Runner::run(&RunnerRequest) -> ProcessOutput
synchronous until PR11 (then a boxed Send future)". `design/08_design_trait_surface.md:50-56`:
"every async trait used behind `dyn` returns a boxed `Send` future … The pre-Tokio runner steps use
the same request/output contract through a synchronous `run`; step 5 changes that call shape to
the boxed-future surface while parity tests hold process and parsing semantics fixed". PR11's
`production_effect` is none; schemas 1–3 always run the legacy engine
(`sequential_substrate.selection`).

**Code.** §2's caller list: the legacy engine, the adapters' `probe`/`discover`, the gates and the
reviews call `run` synchronously and consume its `Result` on the spot.

**Reading.** `Runner::run` becomes
`fn run<'a>(&'a self, request: &'a RunnerRequest, call: RunnerCall<'a>) -> RunFuture<'a>`, with
`RunFuture<'a> = Pin<Box<dyn Future<Output = Result<ProcessOutput, RunnerError>> + Send + 'a>>`:
the request stays one borrowed value, the trait stays object-safe behind the same `&dyn Runner`,
and what travels with the call (`RunnerCall`: the cancellation token and optional per-invocation
observers, R-I) is a second argument rather than a field of the request. **Every synchronous
caller meets it through one blocking adapter**, `Runner::run_blocking` (and `run_blocking_with`
for a caller that carries a call), provided methods that drive the future to completion on the
calling thread with a thread-park waker — no Tokio runtime on the legacy path. The host and
container runners' futures do their whole supervision in the poll that first sees them (R-I), so
on the legacy path the process is spawned, supervised and reaped on the same thread, with the same
environment composition, program resolution, hooks, output and errors as before. The adapters'
`probe` and `discover` stay synchronous and call `run_blocking`: design §8 also writes `async fn
probe`, but PR11's scope names the Runner's surface only, and an async adapter surface is not
needed by anything PR11 builds.

**How parity is proved.** (1) The whole existing suite, green, over the migrated call sites — the
suite already holds process and parsing semantics fixed (`runner::host::tests::supervision_parity_tests`
runs the Runner against the direct funnel over the parity fixtures;
`runner::contract::tests::adapter_parse_parity` drives every adapter's parser through a
`&dyn Runner`; the role-grid and environment-composition tests pin what each role's child
receives). (2) New parity tests for the adapter itself: that `run_blocking` drives the host
runner's future on the calling thread (the child is created from the caller's thread, so the
thread-scoped state of §2 is the caller's), and that a call that was never cancelled reports what
the direct funnel reports. (3) No schema-1..3 path changes behaviour: the migration of each call
site is the mechanical `run(` → `run_blocking(` and nothing else, and every test double that
answered `run` synchronously keeps its body and implements the test-only
`contract::tests::InlineRunner` instead (a blanket impl gives it the future); §5's tier-2 check holds
exactly that for the frozen modules' tests.

**Alternatives.** Keep a synchronous `run` and add an asynchronous `run_async` beside it —
rejected: it contradicts the packet's literal "Runner::run … then a boxed Send future" and the
notes' promise that PR11 turns `run` into the future "behind this same `dyn`"
(`docs/internals/runner/contract.md`, "`fn the_runner_trait_is_object_safe()`"). A Tokio runtime
per legacy call — rejected: a runtime per invocation, a panic when a caller is already inside a
runtime, and a Tokio dependency on the legacy path. A private `block_on` at each caller —
rejected: N copies of one executor.

### R-B — no new effect primitive

**Packet.** `tokio_boundary`: "PR11 changes only scheduling"; the runner row keeps every process
under the host runner's supervision; `effect_site_inventory` puts process start behind
`Process.Spawn`/`Process.Terminate`.

**Reading.** Tokio is scheduling only: no `tokio::process`, `tokio::fs`, `tokio::net`,
`tokio::signal` and no `io-*` feature, ever (R-C); no new spawn path. Processes keep spawning
through `agent::proc::run_with_timeout_classified` (`src/agent/proc.rs:135`) →
`ProcessTree::spawn` (`:66`). **Cancellation adds no primitive and no site:** the funnel's
supervision loop gains a stop predicate beside its timeout and output bound, and a cancelled tree
is terminated by the same code a timed-out tree is — `terminate_supervised` on Unix (`:510`,
through the reaper) and `kill_tree` on Windows (`:488`, the private job), each inside
`Process.Terminate`'s hook phases. So `clippy.toml`'s disallowed lists, `effects/*.toml`, the
site inventory and `effect_sites.json` do not change. The census
`every_production_process_start_is_classified` (`src/runner/contract.rs:1163`) keeps its counts:
no new `Command::new(`, `.spawn()` or `run_with_timeout` name; the funnel entry keeps its name and
gains a parameter. The funnel's private entry `run_with_timeout_and_limit` now takes eight parameters and carries
`#[expect(clippy::too_many_arguments, reason = ..)]`, a per-item expectation with its reason, not a
lint-level change. `effects/wrappers.toml` does not change because no new externally reachable
`fn` is added to a `CLASSIFIED_MODULES` file (`src/effects.rs:1365`; the derivation is
`externally_reachable_fns`, `:1423`, which counts `pub`/`pub(crate)`/`pub(super)` fns, every fn of
a trait impl, and default bodies of public traits): the new surface lives in
`src/runner/contract.rs`, which is not a classified module, and the changes to `src/runner/host.rs`
and `src/agent/proc.rs` add private fns only.

**Alternative.** Run each invocation's supervision on a thread the runner spawns — rejected in
R-I, not for effects but for the thread-scoped cleanup lease.

### R-C — the Tokio dependency

**Reading.** `tokio = "=1.53.1"`, the newest non-yanked release when read (crates.io sparse
index, fetched 2026-09-30T00:26:09Z), `rust-version = "1.71"`, below this crate's 1.85; its one
non-optional dependency is `pin-project-lite` (0.2.17, `rust-version = "1.37"`)
(`phase0/tokio-msrv.log`). **Pinned exactly**, for `globset = "=0.4.19"`'s reason: Tokio's MSRV
rolls forward, and the next minor is free to leave the contract. **Features, minimal:** phase 1
uses Tokio only in tests, as a `[dev-dependencies]` entry with `default-features = false,
features = ["rt-multi-thread"]` (which implies `rt`), to drive the Runner surface from a real
multi-thread runtime the way phase 3's pipelines will; phase 3 moves it to `[dependencies]` when
the coordinator first uses it, with `rt`, `rt-multi-thread` and `sync` (the completion channels),
and `time` only if production waits move onto Tokio's clock (the packet's tests use a fake clock).
Never `process`, `fs`, `net`, `signal`, `io-util`, `io-std` (R-B), and not `macros`, which pulls a
proc-macro tree (`syn`, `quote`, `proc-macro2`) for `select!`/`#[tokio::test]` the slice does not
need. `Cargo.lock` is committed with the entry; `cargo +1.85.0 check --locked --all-targets
--all-features` is the MSRV evidence, logged at the phase-1 head (§9). Only
`[dev-dependencies]` is touched in phase 1, never `[lints]`.

**Alternative.** Add Tokio to `[dependencies]` now with the coordinator's features — rejected: a
dependency no production code uses, and a feature set chosen before the code that needs it.

### R-D — the frozen module set, by path

**Packet.** "fold, queue, merge, repair, recovery, derived outcome, finalization, and
runner-identity modules byte-identical to PR10" (`invariants_preserved`); "no event/merge/
recovery/runner-identity changes" (scope); "event vocabulary, fold, queue, merge, repair,
recovery, derived outcome, finalization, and runner identity are unchanged (G6 module diff
proof)" (`tokio_boundary`); G6's artifact "diff proof that fold/queue/merge/repair/recovery
modules are unchanged from G5".

**The set, production files** (each checked to exist, `phase0/frozen-set-history.log`):

| concern | files |
|---|---|
| event vocabulary and the Event funnel | `src/topology/events.rs`, `src/topology/schema.rs`, `src/events/mod.rs`, `src/events/log.rs`, `src/events/log/premove.rs` |
| fold (incl. derived outcome, `fold/outcome.rs`) | `src/topology/fold.rs`, `src/topology/fold/{apply,check_attempt,check_candidate,check_end,check_integration,outcome,parse,predicates,region,start}.rs` |
| queue, and the fold-derived lease table and path policy it reads | `src/topology/queue.rs`, `src/topology/leases.rs`, `src/topology/paths.rs`, `src/topology/registry.rs` |
| merge | `src/engine/topology/integrate.rs` |
| repair | `src/engine/topology/repair.rs` |
| recovery | `src/engine/topology/recover.rs` |
| finalization | `src/engine/topology/finalize.rs` |
| runner identity (the recorded `RunnerPolicy`: resolution, digest, rebuild by inspection) | `src/runner/policy.rs`, `src/runner/container/resolve.rs` (and `recover.rs`'s `rebuilt` module, above) |

**Why these and not more.** `leases.rs`, `paths.rs` and `registry.rs` are named although the
packet does not list them, because each is an input the fold replays (INV-16's lease table,
`path_policy` frozen in `run_started`, the task registry) — a change there is a fold change by
another name. `closure.rs` is **not** frozen: phase 4 replaces its refusal. `identity.rs`,
`attempt.rs`, `candidate.rs`, `settle.rs`, `dispatch.rs`, `select.rs`, `run.rs`, `emit.rs`,
`preflight.rs`, `create.rs` are not frozen: they are the substrate PR11 schedules. The runner's
execution modules (`host.rs`, `container/exec.rs`, `contract.rs`) are not runner *identity*: they
execute under the recorded policy and do not decide it; phase 1 changes their call shape only.
`src/runner/invocation.rs` (the `InvocationId` shape, frozen by `permits.invocation_identity` and
owned by PR4/PR7) is not in the set because nothing in the packet freezes its derives; any change
to it is stated where it is made.

**Test children.** The frozen modules' whole-file test modules — `src/topology/fold/tests.rs`,
`src/topology/fold/tests/{outcome,predicates,questions}.rs`, `src/events/log/tests.rs`,
`src/engine/topology/{integrate,repair,recover}/tests.rs` — are **subjects that assert against
the frozen code**, not the frozen code itself. The Runner's new call shape reaches exactly one of
them: `recover/tests.rs` implements `Runner` four times and calls it (§2), so the call-shape
migration must touch it. The packet's own text requires both — the Runner surface changes in PR11
(`sequential_substrate.runner`) and the recovery module is byte-identical — and the only reading
under which both hold is that "module" means the module's production code; a module's test double
of a trait whose signature the packet itself changes cannot keep its old signature. So the proof
has two tiers: (1) every frozen **production** file is byte-identical to the merge base
(`git diff --quiet`, which also covers their inline `#[cfg(test)]` modules); (2) every frozen
**test child** is either byte-identical or differs only by the mechanical migration, checked by
reversing the migration's substitutions on the head blob and comparing it byte for byte with the
base blob (§5 carries the script).

**"Byte-identical to PR10", measured.** The set is not byte-identical between PR10's merge
(`caf6bed0f9f5f749ed9ecb99fb44bfb3e08fb2ac`) and G5's range (`d724fb16…`): five of its files moved
there, 891 insertions and 4 deletions, through the reviewed G5-period fixes; and between G5's range
and this branch's base two more moved, `src/events/mod.rs` and `src/topology/registry.rs`, in
their inline `mod tests` only (#322's scratch-tree routing, `f3e70039`, `ff25fea4`)
(`phase0/frozen-set-history.log`). So "byte-identical to PR10" cannot mean PR10's own bytes on this
branch; it means **PR11 introduces no change to them**, which is a comparison with the merge base.
**What G6 will need** is the two-step form: G5's range → this branch's merge base, attributed to
the pull requests that made it (all test-only in the set since G5), then merge base → PR11's merged
head, which tier (1) must show empty.

### R-E — where the concurrency seam is cut

**Packet.** `single_writer`: "one coordinator owns EventLog and every ref mutation";
`design/26_design_merge_queue_protocol.md:210-213`: "Only the coordinator owns `EventLog` and Git
ref mutation. Tokio workers return typed results over channels." `completion_identity`:
"verification completions carry (sequence, candidate)". The merge module is frozen (R-D).

**Code.** §2: attempts run in `attempt.rs` (worker, capture, gates through `run_registered`,
reviews through the `ReviewPasses` seam), settle in `settle.rs`/`run.rs`, promote in
`candidate.rs` — none frozen. Integration runs in `integrate.rs` — frozen — as one synchronous
chain from `merge_verification_started` through `journal.verify(..)` to the terminal append and
the CAS (`integrate.rs:672-806`), reached through two seams the coordinator implements:
`IntegrationJournal` (append, fold, hooks, `converted`) and `Verification` (`verify`, `ids`).

**Reading.** *Attempts* are lifted by restructuring the unfrozen substrate: an attempt pipeline
(worker, capture, gates, reviews) runs on a blocking thread and returns a typed completion bound to
`(key, generation, attempt)`; every append (`attempt_started`, `attempt_finished`,
`candidate_prepared`, `task_candidate_created`) and every ref mutation (the candidate-prepared
pin, the candidates ref) stay on the coordinator. *Integration verification* is lifted **at the
`Verification` seam**: the coordinator runs `integrate()` itself, so every append and the CAS stay
on its thread, and its `verify` hands the gates and reviews to a verification pipeline and **keeps
the coordinator loop running while it waits** — processing other pipelines' completions, slot
grants and dispatches — until the pipeline's typed completion for `(sequence, candidate)` arrives,
then returns it to `integrate.rs`, which appends the terminal exactly as today. The seam's
signature already allows it: `verify(&mut self, ..)` holds the whole coordinator, and
`integrate()` holds nothing across the call that another append could invalidate (it re-reads
`journal.fold()` after `verify` returns). A halt, shutdown or append error that arrives while the
verification is in flight cancels the pipeline and makes `verify` return an error, so
`integrate.rs` appends nothing further and the closure (phase 4) settles the verification with
`merge_verification_interrupted`. **Consequences phases 3 and 4 inherit:** a cancelled Runner
error must never be classified as an outage — today `IntegrationCx::verify` maps fate `Gone` to an
infrastructure outage (`run.rs:151-170`) and a review pass reports a `Gone` Runner error as the
review being unavailable (`src/review.rs:547-560`), both of which a cancellation would reach, so the
cancellation is typed (`RunnerError::is_cancelled`, phase 1) and those arms test it first; and `integrate.rs`'s
private reclaim helpers are not reachable from `closure.rs`, so the live closure reclaims staging,
snapshots and the prepared pin through `WorkspaceManager`'s funnels, as recovery does.

**Alternatives.** (a) Split `start_and_verify` at `journal.verify` so the coordinator can resume a
transaction when the pipeline returns — an edit to a frozen file, the owner's to allow. (b) Run
`integrate()` on a pipeline thread behind a journal proxy — rejected: the pin and the CAS
(`publish`) would be ref mutations off the coordinator, against `single_writer`. (c) Block the
coordinator for the verification's duration — rejected: no dispatch, halt or append-error handling
while it waits, and a deadlock the first time a reviewer waits for a slot only the blocked
coordinator can grant (INV-18: "atomic slot pairs granted by the coordinator loop"). The re-entrant
loop is the one option that keeps the merge module byte-identical, the coordinator the single
writer, and INV-18's never-block; it is phase 3's to build and phase 3's review to hold.

### R-F — "the coordinator never awaits"

**Packet.** INV-18: "The coordinator never blocks on an entitlement, provisional reservation, or
slot"; `deadlock_freedom`: "entitlements are never awaited; provisional reservations are taken
only when the derived count permits and are converted or cancelled without waiting".

**Reading.** What awaits: **pipelines** — a pipeline awaits its slot pair (a pending request the
coordinator grants from the head-reservation FIFO when both slots are free) and awaits its Runner
completion (the Runner's future, driven on the pipeline's blocking thread). What never awaits: the
**coordinator** — entitlements are fold-derived counts compared, never waited on; a provisional
reservation is taken only when the count permits and is converted at its first append or
cancelled; a slot grant is a decision in the coordinator loop, never a wait; the coordinator never
polls a Runner future (a host or container future does its supervision inside the poll, R-I, so
polling one would block the coordinator for the life of a process). The coordinator's only wait is
for its next message — a completion or a request — including inside R-E's verification seam.

### R-G — > 1 only under TopologyPreview

**Reading.** Tests reach `max_parallel = 3` by building the `RunStarted4` record themselves, under
`WriterSelector::TopologyPreview`, as the scaffold does for 1 (`scaffold.rs:139`); run creation
takes the record from its caller (`create.rs:1525`) and no config parse is on that path. The
production refusal — `src/config/parse.rs:502-532` and its pin
`max_parallel_above_one_is_refused_rather_than_read_past` (`src/config.rs:1935`) — is untouched,
and production still writes schema 3 (`schema.rs:59-64`, the const assertions `:39-42`), so no
production process reaches the topology engine at any width. PR12 activates.

### R-H — the findings PR11's guard names, per row

(§6 carries the full rows.) `PR7-R3-ATTEMPT-002-REVIEWERS-TAKE-NO-SLOT`: **closed by phases 2/3**
when review passes take their `{agent, pool?}` pair through the broker; witness, planned: a
reviewer and a worker of one agent at `max_per_agent = 1` serialize on the agent slot, and a
review pass's invocation is registered before its process starts rather than after it returns
(the test names land in §7 when written). `PR5-C-FSYNC-UNOBSERVABLE`: **not closable by a test at
the platter**, and not PR11's to change: G5 established the two-crash proof for the simulated loss
and says "the physical sync is outside any test's sight"; PR11 touches no byte of the Event funnel
(R-D). A syscall-level witness (a child process traced for the `fsync` on the log's descriptor)
could make deleting `sync_all()` fail a Linux test, but it would observe the request to the
kernel, not the data on the platter, which is the finding's own claim. Stays deferred; phase 7
re-guards it to the change that adds such a witness, or the owner.
`PR5-CAPACITY-NOT-A-TOPOLOGY-RESOURCE`: **not closed, stays with the owner** — its guard is the
project owner; capacity as a brokered resource is a new row in a frozen packet, capacity-aware
routing is the later G-CAP gate (`cumulative_review_gates.later_gates_not_in_scope`), and PR11's
non-goal is "any authority change". The broker PR11 builds brokers R1–R4 and R13 only; capacity
stays ambient, surfacing as outage settlements.

### R-I — where the Runner's future does its work

**Code.** The Unix reaper takes its shared cleanup-lease holds on the run directories the
*spawning thread* has entered (`rundir::active_cleanup_lease_paths`, a `thread_local!`,
`src/rundir/cleanup/scopes.rs:28-29`, read at `src/agent/proc.rs:2466`), and the substrate enters
the run's scope on the thread that steps the loop (`run.rs:696`); the observation export attributes
a record to the current thread's name (`src/observations.rs:156-157`).

**Reading.** The host and container runners' futures run their whole supervision — environment,
program resolution, spawn, supervision loop, reap — in the poll that first sees them, on the
polling thread. A Runner future is therefore driven from a thread that may block: the blocking
adapter's caller today, a pipeline's blocking thread in phase 3 (which cannot enter the run's
cleanup scope, a thread-local the coordinator's thread holds, and carries the scope's lease paths
into every call instead — R-Z). That is the explicit blocking boundary §10 of the standards asks
for, and it keeps R28's hold and the observation attribution exactly where they are. The
cancellation carried with the call is observed by the supervision loop (host) and the container
supervision loop, so a cancel from another thread terminates the tree through the existing
terminate path and the future then resolves as cancelled with fate `Gone` (`NeverStarted` when
the call was cancelled before anything started). A future dropped before its first poll started
nothing; a future of these two runners cannot be dropped mid-flight, because its supervision is
inside one poll — so the protocol's "cancelled after the Runner terminated its process" is carried
by the token, and the broker (phase 2) releases an invocation once, when its future reports.

**Alternative.** Offload the supervision to a thread the runner spawns and make the future a
receiver. Rejected for phase 1: the reaper would take no lease on the run directory (the offload
thread entered no scope) unless the scope were propagated into the reaper's arming — a change to
R28's machinery that belongs with R28 under concurrency (phase 5), not with the call shape — and
every test that observes spawn points would see them on another thread. Nothing in phases 2–6
needs the offload: pipelines are blocking-thread code anyway (capture and snapshots are Git
subprocesses and filesystem work).

### R-J — where `permits.rs` lives

**Packet.** `phase_zero_modules.future_homes` names `src/topology/permits.rs`.

**Reading (phase 0).** The broker is scheduling state — process-local ledgers (R3, R4, R13) beside
fold-derived counts (R1, R2) — and not authority. Putting it in `src/topology/` is possible without
touching a frozen file: `src/topology/mod.rs` is not in §5's set, and adding `pub mod permits;` is
a declaration-only change of the kind PR9/PR10 recorded as Class A ("no vocabulary change"); the
module would read the fold and never write it. Phase 2 decides between that home and
`src/engine/topology/` (where the width-1 ledgers it replaces live) and records the choice here;
either way a new module is an inline-tested file (a new whole-file test module would need
`WHOLE_FILE_TEST_MODULES`, an instrument) with its own notes file.

**Decided (phase 2): `src/engine/topology/permits.rs`**, declared in `src/engine/topology.rs`
(not frozen), inline `#[cfg(test)] mod tests`, notes `docs/internals/engine/topology/permits.md`.
Three facts decide it, each measured. (1) The ledgers the broker composes stay where they are:
`Reservations` and `InvocationLedger` are named through `super::identity` by `EmitContext` in the
frozen `recover.rs` (`:1392-1399`, §2), so they are evolved in place in `identity.rs`, and a broker
under `src/topology/` would have to import `crate::engine::topology::identity` — a dependency from
the model layer up into the engine that no production file under `src/topology/` has today (their
`crate::engine` references are all in test code). (2) Every caller is in `src/engine/topology/`:
the run, the attempt, the pre-flight, run creation, the retry. (3) The packet's `future_homes` is
a phase-0 placement list for the model modules, and the broker is not model: it is process-local
scheduling state the fold never replays. **The fold readers do not live in `permits.rs` either**
(R-N): a frozen census forbids a new production module naming `TopologyFold`, so R1/R2's reading
and a pipeline's standing live in `select.rs`, which already names it, and the broker takes them as
values.

### R-K — where the slot limits come from

**Packet.** `permits.agent_pool_slots`: "per-agent slots (max_per_agent, default max_parallel) and
per-pool slots keyed by pool name (max_per_pool, default max_parallel); process-lifetime ephemeral
scheduler state". `alternatives[8]` rejects "durable invocation grant/release events for slots".
`canonical_trace_projection` drops "scheduling configuration (max_parallel, max_per_agent,
max_per_pool)".

**Code.** `run_started(4)` records `TopologyLimits { max_parallel, max_defers, max_merge_repairs }`
and nothing else (`src/topology/events.rs:360-364`); the event vocabulary is frozen and PR11 adds no
durable field. `[engine] max_per_agent`/`max_per_pool` are parsed by `src/config/parse.rs`
(`parse_engine`), refused at zero, defaulting to the **configured** `max_parallel`, and carried on
`Config` (`src/config.rs:375-377`, "acted on by the topology engine").

**Reading.** `max_parallel` is the recorded one: the fold's `pipeline_reservable` compares against
`run_started.limits.max_parallel`, and the broker reads it from the fold at every reservation
(`Entitlements::max_parallel`). The two slot limits are the command's configuration — scheduling
state of this process, not of the run — held by the broker as `SlotLimits`, never zero
(`NonZeroU32`). Until a caller hands the configured values over, the broker defaults both to the
recorded `max_parallel` (`PermitBroker::for_run`, `SlotLimits::defaulted`), which is the packet's
default. At this phase no schema-4 caller holds a `Config` (the substrate is built by tests; PR12
activates production), so `TopologyRun::resumed` uses the default; phase 3's coordinator is handed
the command's limits (`PipelineSeams::slots`) and installs them before it admits anything, replacing
the broker with `PermitBroker::new(limits)` only while nothing is outstanding in it
(`TopologyRun::limit_slots`); a command that parsed `[engine]` is PR12's caller.

**Alternative.** Record the limits in `run_started(4)` — rejected: a new durable field is a
vocabulary change (non-goal), and the packet calls the table "process-lifetime ephemeral scheduler
state".

### R-L — "FIFO with head reservation", defined

**Packet.** `permits.protocol`: "if slotted, wait for the atomic pair grant (FIFO with head
reservation)"; `deadlock_freedom`: "head reservation prevents slot starvation";
`alternatives[74]`: "strict head-of-line FIFO for slots — rejected: blocks disjoint work; head
reservation adopted".

**Reading.** Waiting requests form one queue in arrival order. The **head** — the oldest waiting
request — reserves every slot it waits for: one slot of its agent and, when it has a pool, one of
its pool. A request is granted when (a) it is the head and its pair fits the free capacity
(`held + 1 <= limit` for its agent and its pool), or (b) it is behind the head and its pair fits the
capacity left **after the head's reservation** (`held + 1 + 1 <= limit` on a resource the head also
needs, `held + 1 <= limit` on any other). So a later request is **backfilled** past a waiting head
only when it takes nothing the head waits for. The grant procedure after every registration,
completion and cancellation: grant heads while they fit, then backfill the rest in arrival order
against the new head's reservation; cancelling the head moves the reservation to the next request
(`permits.protocol`'s "head reservation recomputed"), which can grant requests the old head held
back. Shutdown and the append-error protocol withdraw every waiting request together, granting
nothing on the way.

**Why the head cannot starve.** While a request is the head no grant can take a slot it reserved,
so every holder of a resource it is blocked on was granted before it became the head; holders are
running processes that wait for nothing and end, so the head is granted at the first release that
frees its last blocking slot, and every later request becomes the head in turn.
`permits::tests::seeded_adversarial_orders_grant_every_request_in_order_and_exceed_no_limit`
checks that invariant after every step of 256 seeded adversarial orders.

**Alternatives.** Strict FIFO — the packet's rejected one. Backfill with no reservation — a head
needing two slots that free at different times loses the first to every later request that fits
it, which is the starvation the reservation exists to prevent
(`identity::tests::a_waiting_head_reserves_every_slot_it_needs_so_no_later_request_starves_it`).
Reserving for every waiting request rather than the head only — equivalent to strict FIFO on any
shared resource.

### R-M — the selection identity of a provisional reservation

**Packet.** `permits.provisional_reservations`: "process-lifetime bridge between a selection
decision and its first append … conversion at the append; cancellation on any pre-append failure,
run end, shutdown, or a poisoned fold … the sequential substrate asserts at most one" — so above
width 1 several may be outstanding. The G6 matrix: "provisional reservation — owner: selection
interval (dispatch, retry, integration); uniqueness: one per selected task or candidate". INV-18:
"every … provisional conversion is bound to a unique InvocationId or selection identity". ST-02:
"duplicate complete/cancel/release for an InvocationId or provisional reservation is ignored and
counted".

**Reading.** A reservation is bound to `(TaskKey, ReservationKind)`, at most one outstanding per
task. One per task is structural, not a convention: a task can be selected for exactly one of the
three admissions at a time — dispatch requires no open generation and no queued candidate, retry a
retained-idle generation, integration a queued candidate, and the queue holds at most one candidate
per task — and a second reservation for a task with one outstanding is refused
(`Reservations::reserve`). The key identifies a candidate as well, which is what the frozen
`IntegrationJournal::converted(key)` converts by. **Staleness.** Every conversion is made by the
coordinator at the append it bridges and every pre-append cancellation at the failure, in the same
step, so an operation from an earlier interval of the same `(task, kind)` cannot arrive after a
later one began. A conversion or cancellation naming a reservation that was taken and is no longer
outstanding is a duplicate or a stale operation: ignored and counted (ST-02). One naming a
reservation never taken, or naming the task's outstanding one under another kind, is refused.

**Alternative.** Bind to `(task, generation, attempt)` or `(sequence, candidate)` — the frozen
`converted(key)` carries only the key, and a sequence is not allocated until the append the
reservation bridges.

### R-N — the acquisition order as an API rule, and where the fold is read

**Packet.** `deadlock_freedom`: "acquisition order pipeline -> merge -> {agent, pool} (atomic
pair); slot holders are running processes that wait for nothing; entitlements are never awaited;
provisional reservations are taken only when the derived count permits … therefore no hold-and-wait
cycle". INV-18: "acquisition order pipeline -> merge -> {agent, pool} is acyclic".

**Reading.** The order is a precondition of the slotted registration, not a convention:
`InvocationLedger::register_slotted` takes a `Standing`, read from the fold for the pipeline the
invocation belongs to, and refuses a pair to a pipeline the fold shows holding nothing. An attempt
stands when its generation is `InFlight` with that attempt number (its processes run between
`attempt_started` and its settlement; an earlier attempt's identity is stale, INV-20); a sequence
stands when the fold's open transaction is that sequence and its verification has started; a
pre-flight probe always stands (probes run before admission and hold no entitlement); a poisoned
fold admits nothing. With entitlements never awaited (a refused reservation is an error, not a
wait) and holders waiting for nothing, no process holds a slot while waiting for an entitlement,
and none holds an entitlement while waiting for anything but a slot: no hold-and-wait cycle.

**Where the fold is read.** `events::log::tests::the_stable_prefix_barrier_is_the_only_way_a_log_becomes_a_topology_fold`
— in `src/events/log/tests.rs`, a frozen test child (R-D) — asserts that the production regions
naming `TopologyFold` are exactly a listed 21. The first cut of this phase named it in four more
(`permits.rs`, `attempt.rs`, `preflight.rs`, `dispatch.rs`) and the census failed
(`~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_impl_b/dev/suite-1/test.log`). Rather than edit a frozen test, the fold
readers — `Entitlements::of` (R1, R2) and `Standing::of` — live in `src/engine/topology/select.rs`,
which is on the list and is where selection already reads the fold's admission; the broker, the
judge and the registering boundary take their results as values, and the emitter seam grows
`EventEmitter::standing` so `attempt.rs` asks the emitter rather than naming the fold. After the
move the census passes unchanged.

### R-O — the width-1 refusal, worded to the packet

**Packet.** INV-18: "The coordinator never blocks on an entitlement, provisional reservation, or
slot". `provisional_reservations`: "the sequential substrate asserts at most one".

**Code.** `SlotAssertion::acquire` refused a second pair "at max_parallel = 1 the substrate asserts
a single slotted invocation rather than queueing" (`identity.rs:150-160` at the base), and
`a_second_slot_pair_is_refused_rather_than_queued` and
`a_refused_slot_acquisition_settles_the_registration_it_took` pinned that wording.

**Reading.** The synchronous substrate is the coordinator running every invocation on its own
thread, so it may not wait for a pair. `InvocationLedger::register_at_once` registers the request,
and when the pair is not granted at once withdraws it — registered and cancelled once, the head
recomputed, nothing spawned — and refuses with INV-18's sentence and the holds it would have waited
behind. The pins are rewritten to that **sentence**, not to a description of the broker:
`identity::tests::the_synchronous_substrate_refuses_a_pair_it_cannot_grant_at_once` replaces the
"rather than queued" test, and the refused-acquisition test now asserts INV-18's words and the
squatter's name. `Reservations::take` keeps the sequential form's own sentence ("the sequential
substrate asserts at most one") for the fixtures that drive the frozen merge and recovery modules;
the width-1 run itself reserves through the broker's check (`PermitBroker::reserve`).

### R-P — review passes take their pair through the broker

**Code (base).** `attempt.rs:865` handed the review seam the raw runner and entered the pass's
invocations in the ledger after it returned (`:894-899`): `PR7-R3-ATTEMPT-002`.

**Reading.** `Judge::judge` hands each review pass a `preflight::Registering` boundary over the
judge's own ledger, in its pipeline mode (`Slots::Pipeline`: the reviewer's pool, the judgement's
standing), so every process of a pass — the pass, a re-ask — is registered with its pair before it
starts and settled after it ends, exactly as the pre-flight's probes already were. The post-hoc
registration is gone. A new check replaces it: the boundary counts the processes it completed, and
a pass that reports more than ran through it ran one outside the broker, and the judgement is
refused — the pattern P4 uses for probes (`used_the_grant`). The check sits after the charge, so
PR8-R2-CHARGE-ORDER holds. **The legacy engine is unchanged**: `src/review.rs` is not touched, and
the schema-1..3 path calls `run_review` with its own runner as before (R-A). Integration
verification goes through the same `Judge` (`IntegrationCx::judge_proposal`, and the scaffold's
`Verification`), so its passes take their pair the same way.

**The lock.** `Runner::run` takes `&self` and a `Runner` is `Send + Sync`, so the boundary reaches
the ledger through a `Mutex` (`Registering<'_, L>` is generic over the ledger's owner: owned by the
pre-flight and run creation, `&mut`-borrowed by a judge for one pass). The lock guards the register
and settle calls only, never the Runner call between them, and one pass runs one process at a time,
so it is uncontended. That is the stated reason CODING_STANDARDS §6 asks for.

### R-Q — the frozen test child the broker's rename reaches

**Code.** `src/engine/topology/recover/tests.rs:3938` (a frozen test child, R-D) asserts
`crate::engine::topology::identity::SlotAssertion::new().is_empty()` — "the slot table is empty at
process start".

**Reading.** The slot table is replaced by `SlotTable` (inside `InvocationLedger`), so the one line
becomes `crate::engine::topology::identity::SlotTable::new().is_empty()`: the same assertion of the
same property, on the type that now holds it. That is a mechanical migration of the kind R-D's tier
2 admits, and §5's check gains its reverse substitution
(`identity::SlotTable::new()` → `identity::SlotAssertion::new()`). No other byte of a frozen file
changes, and `recover.rs` keeps constructing `EmitContext` with `Reservations::new()` and
`InvocationLedger::new()`, whose names and signatures are unchanged.

### Phase 3's readings

R-R..R-AD record the orchestrator's ten dispositions on the research memo (each cites the memo
section it rests on) and the choices the implementation made where neither the packet nor the
memo decides. Line numbers are at phase 2's head `d4217490`, which phase 3 starts from.

### R-R — the threading model: a plain-thread coordinator, pipelines on the blocking pool

**Packet.** `design/26_design_merge_queue_protocol.md:210-213`: "Only the coordinator owns
`EventLog` and Git ref mutation. Tokio workers return typed results over channels."
`tests_acceptance.determinism`: "seeded scheduling permutations under Tokio".

**Code.** The Runner's future does its whole supervision in its first poll (R-I), so whatever
polls it blocks for the life of the process; `integrate()` calls `Verification::verify`
synchronously (`integrate.rs:696`). Measured on Tokio 1.53.1 by the memo's probe (memo §3.1,
`~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_research_c/tokio-blocking-probe.log`): `UnboundedReceiver::blocking_recv`
works on a plain thread, on a `spawn_blocking` thread and nested inside a synchronous call from
its own receive loop, and panics only inside `Runtime::block_on` or a worker task.

**Reading (memo §3, model M1).** The coordinator is the caller's own thread — the one that calls
`TopologyRun::step` at width 1 — and never runs inside a runtime context: it waits only in
`blocking_recv` on its inbox, in its main loop and inside `verify` (R-V). Each pipeline runs on the
runtime's blocking pool (`spawn_blocking`) and drives its Runner futures there with
`run_blocking_with` (R-I), waiting for coordinator replies (a slot grant, a snapshot grant) on
`oneshot` receivers. The runtime is built per coordinator, multi-thread with one worker thread (no
task is ever spawned onto it), its blocking pool bounded by the run's `max_parallel`, and every
pool thread named after the calling thread, because the observation export keys its record on
the current thread's name (`src/observations.rs:156-157`). Tokio moves to `[dependencies]`, still
`=1.53.1`, `default-features = false`, features `rt`, `rt-multi-thread` and `sync` — no `process`,
`fs`, `net`, `signal`, `io-*`, `macros` or `time` (R-C). A pipeline's body runs under
`catch_unwind`, so a panicking pipeline sends a defined completion (an error) rather than leaving
the coordinator waiting on an inbox nothing will write, and every pipeline's `JoinHandle` is
joined before the entry returns (standards §10).

**Alternatives.** (M2) the coordinator as an async task inside `block_on`: `verify` is synchronous
and called from the frozen `integrate()`, so it could wait only through `block_in_place`, which
panics on a current-thread runtime, and every other synchronous wait would need the same wrapping.
(M3) pipelines on `std::thread::scope`: no `'static` cost, but Tokio would shrink to its channels,
against "Tokio workers" (memo §3.2).

### R-S — the cut: what the coordinator keeps and what a pipeline runs

**Packet.** `single_writer`: "one coordinator owns EventLog and every ref mutation";
`completion_identity`: "attempt-level completions carry (key, generation, attempt); verification
completions carry (sequence, candidate); slot grants/releases/cancels and invocation registrations
carry InvocationId"; `permits.protocol`: "a pipeline holds at most one invocation at a time".

**Code.** `TopologyRun::step` (`run.rs:691-746`) runs one branch to completion: the dispatch
(`dispatch.rs:130-177`), `attempt_started`, the worker, capture, the candidate diff, the
assessment, the review inputs and the judgement (`run.rs:1318-1417`), then the settlement or the
promotion (`run.rs:1419-1580`); an integration runs the frozen `integrate()`, whose stale arm calls
`verify` between `merge_verification_started` and its terminal (`integrate.rs:672-806`).

**Reading (memo §2.1–2.2).** Every append and every ref mutation stays in the function that makes
it today, on the coordinator. A pipeline is exactly a span between two appends of one identity:
the **attempt body** `run_worker → capture → candidate_diff → assess → inputs → judge` (between
`attempt_started` and `attempt_finished`/`candidate_prepared`), and the **verification body**
(today's `judge_proposal`, between `merge_verification_started` and the transaction's terminal).
`dispatch()` — the reservation, `task_dispatched`, the intent, the worktree add and a repair's
materialization — and `attempt_started` stay on the coordinator, so the fold never shows a
generation `OpenNoAttempt` while a pipeline is creating its worktree, and `eligible_continuation`
cannot select a generation that is live (memo §2.1). The hand-backs are `Judged(key, generation,
attempt)` and `Verified(sequence, candidate)`, each carrying the body's typed result, and the
per-invocation `Admit`/`Ended` of R-U, bound to `InvocationId`; R-W adds the snapshot gate's two.
The coordinator's only waits are its inbox and the synchronous work it already did at width 1
(its own Git, filesystem and appends).

**Alternative.** Create the worktree inside the pipeline — rejected: the fold would show the
generation `OpenNoAttempt` until a later hand-back, and selection would dispatch it a second time
unless a process-local mask shadowed `eligible_continuation`.

### R-T — one implementation of every transition; `step` is the degenerate schedule

**Packet.** `tokio_boundary`: "PR11 changes only scheduling"; `permitted_transitions`: "none new;
scheduling only".

**Code.** The frozen test child `src/engine/topology/recover/tests.rs` is the only driver of
`TopologyRun::step` (55 calls), builds `RunSeams` as a twelve-field literal twenty times and calls
`resumed`, `fold`, `events`, `spend`, `entitlements_held`, `holds_entitlement`,
`invocations_balance`, `reservations_cancelled` and `commitment_digest`
(`~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_research_c/frozen-test-child-api-census.log`, memo §7); tier 2 of the
module diff proof (§5) allows that file the Runner migration and R-Q's rename only.
`run/tests.rs` reads `run.rs`'s own production region and pins that every `self.emit(` is followed
by `?` and that the loop reaches `select(` and `checkpoint(` through exactly one call each.

**Reading (memo §7).** `RunSeams`, `TopologyRun::{resumed, step}` and every accessor the frozen
file uses keep their signatures and meanings. Each transition becomes one function that both
drivers call: selection (`select` then `checkpoint`, one call site), the dispatch and first-attempt
start, the retry start, the settlement and the promotion, the integration's pre- and post-steps
around the frozen `integrate()`, and the idle arms (backoff, hard block, closure). The pipeline
bodies are free functions over the pieces a body touches — no emitter, no fold — so `step` runs
them inline on its own thread with its own hooks object, the way it runs them today (every kill
point and hook test keeps its meaning), and the coordinator runs the same functions spawned. The
coordinator is a new, additive entry, `TopologyRun::run_concurrently`, in its own module
(`src/engine/topology/coordinator.rs`, an inline-tested file with its notes file), taking the
width-1 `RunSeams` for its own work and a `PipelineSeams` of owned seams for the pipelines
(`Arc`s of the Runner and the plan, review and adapter seams, a clone of the `WorkspaceManager`
and of the `RunPaths`, a factory minting one hooks object per pipeline, and the command's slot
limits, R-K). Only test-built seams are owned in PR11: production has no schema-4 caller at any
width (R-G), and `engine::assembly::FrozenPlans<'a>`, which borrows configuration, is PR12's to
make owned (memo §3.2).

**Alternative.** Change `RunSeams` or `step` to the owned, spawnable shape — rejected: tier 2 of
the proof would fail on the frozen file's twenty literals.

### R-U — the broker client: one registration path for `step` and for pipelines

**Packet.** `permits.protocol`: "register(invocation_id, slots) -> if slotted, wait for the atomic
pair grant (FIFO with head reservation) else immediately runnable -> … -> complete(invocation_id)
releasing any slots … or cancel(invocation_id) …"; INV-18: "atomic slot pairs granted by the
coordinator loop".

**Code.** The judge registers the worker and each gate through `register_now` and settles through
the ledger it borrows (`attempt.rs:939-990`); a review pass reaches its pair through
`preflight::Registering` over a `Mutex` of the same ledger (`attempt.rs:839-871`,
`preflight.rs:168-275`); the synchronous substrate refuses a pair it cannot be granted at once
(R-O). R-N's rule: a slotted registration presents a `Standing` read from the fold for the
invocation's pipeline.

**Reading (memo §2.2 H3, §10.4).** One trait — the registrar — is the only way an invocation of an
attempt or a verification is registered and settled: `admit` (register; for a slotted invocation,
take its atomic pair) and `ended` (complete or cancel). Two implementations. **Inline**, for `step`,
the pre-flight and run creation: the evolved ledger behind a `Mutex`, registering at once and
refusing with INV-18's sentence exactly as today (R-O). **Channel**, for a spawned pipeline: `admit`
sends `Admit{pipeline, invocation, pair}` and waits on a `oneshot` for the grant — the pipeline
waits, never the coordinator — and `ended` sends `Ended`. The coordinator processes `Admit` by
reading the pipeline's `Standing` from the fold at that moment (R-N) and registering in the
broker; a grant is replied at once or when a later `complete`/`cancel` returns it (phase 2's
`complete`/`cancel` return the requests they newly grant). `preflight::Registering` becomes
generic over the registrar, so the review pass's count check (R-P) keeps its guarantee under both.
Every Runner call a pipeline makes carries the pipeline's `Cancellation` and the cleanup-lease
paths of R-Z, injected where the call is built (the judge's own calls) or rebuilt (the decorator
around a review pass), so the coordinator can cancel a pipeline's process from its own thread
(R-I). A pipeline runs its invocations one after another, so it holds at most one at a time.

**Alternative.** A second, channel-only decorator beside `Registering` — rejected: two
implementations of register/slot/run/settle, which is what R-P removed.

**Corrected in round R2 (§13).** "A grant is replied at once or when a later `complete`/`cancel`
returns it" held for the grant and not for its recipient: the reply went to whichever pipeline waited
for it, without asking whether that pipeline still served an identity the fold holds open. The
standing is read once, at registration — a gate registers with none — so a pair freed later could go
to a pipeline whose identity a decline had closed since: stopping one closed pipeline granted the next
(review round 2's `R2-RECONCILE-GRANT`). As repaired, every place a grant is delivered asks the fold
first, and a grant for a pipeline that is cancelled or serves a closed identity is withdrawn
undelivered and its request refused.

**Corrected in round R3 (§13).** Round R2's "every place a grant is delivered asks the fold first" asked
the fold whether the pipeline's identity is open and the coordinator whether an interrupt was recorded — not
the fold whether a halt was. Between a halting append and the admission pass that acted on it, a withdrawal
could grant an open pipeline the pair it freed (review round 3's `R3-HALT-GRANT`). As repaired, no
grant is delivered while an interrupt is recorded or the fold records a halt (`interrupted`), and the
admission pass acts on a halt before it reconciles anything.

### R-V — the coordinator inside `verify` (R-E, built)

**Packet.** `completion_identity`: "verification completions carry (sequence, candidate)"; R-E's
reading (above) and its three alternatives.

**Code.** `integrate()` holds only owned locals and `&mut J` across `journal.verify(..)`
(`integrate.rs:540-806`), re-reads `journal.fold()` after it returns, and reads nothing an append
of another identity changes (memo §4.3); the fold opens the transaction at
`merge_verification_started`, before `verify`, and refuses a second start
(`check_integration.rs:21-27`), and `eligible_integration_candidate` is `None` while one is open
(`outcome.rs:129-140`).

**Reading (memo §4, orchestrator's disposition 4).** The coordinator runs the frozen `integrate()`
itself, with a journal whose `Verification::verify` spawns the verification body and then **keeps
the coordinator loop running** — slot grants, settlements, promotions, dispatches, retries, answer
ingestion and `budget_exceeded` — until the `Verified(sequence, candidate)` completion arrives,
then returns it mapped exactly as the width-1 `IntegrationCx::verify` maps it. Never a second
integration inside `verify` (asserted before every `integrate()` call), never a backoff, hard block
or closure there (those arms are idle-only, R-AB). A halt, a shutdown or an append error inside
`verify` cancels every pipeline and makes `verify` return an error, so `integrate()` appends
nothing further; what follows is decided by the coordinator's own interrupt state, never by error
text (R-AB, R-AC). The verification's spend is charged when its completion arrives, from the
review records the body returns, before the terminal is appended — at width 1 the same passes are
charged inside the body; the total and its position before the terminal are the same.

**Corrected in round R1 (§13).** As built at phase 3's head, `verify` stored the completion and both
charged its spend and classified its outcome only after the snapshot drain: admissions in between
ignored reported spend (the early review's `R1-CONC-4`, reproduced at `83a387bd`), and a fatal
completion cancelled nothing until the drain (`R1-CONC-2`). Phase 4 made the charge happen at
acceptance; round R1 maps the outcome at acceptance too — the width-1 mapping, `verified`, run as the
completion is received — so an error interrupts at once and only an accepted result waits for the
drain. And when the fold stops holding the transaction open (a decline fails its lineage), `verify`
unwinds instead of waiting for a completion that will not come (`R1-CONC-3`).

**Corrected in round R2 (§13).** "A halt … inside `verify` cancels every pipeline and makes `verify`
return an error" held while the verification's pipeline was live, and not after its result had been
accepted: a halt ingested then — the pipeline retired, the transaction still `VerificationStarted` —
was not acted on, and `integrate()` prepared and published the result after the halt (review round
2's `R2-CONC4-1`). And "never a second integration inside `verify` (asserted before every
`integrate()` call)" was the assertion that ended the command when a decline had removed the
transaction and another candidate was already queued (`R2-DECLINE-QUEUED`). As repaired, a halt
interrupts every in-flight identity the fold shows, and while `verify`'s transaction is gone an
admission pass selects nothing until `verify` has unwound.

### R-W — the snapshot gate (the memo's Q-A, option B)

**Packet.** R-D freezes the merge module; G6's pass rule requires a clean module diff proof.

**Code.** `integrate.rs::reclaim_snapshots` (`:1017-1028`) removes **every** snapshot intent and
worktree of the execution root, on every stale terminal (`:752`, and through `reclaim_staging`,
`:1036`); its notes rest that on "this sequential coordinator runs one judgement at a time"
(`docs/internals/engine/topology/integrate.md`). At width > 1 it would delete a concurrent
attempt's live gate or review snapshot mid-run (memo §4.6).

**Reading (disposition 5; the owner's option A — a three-line edit of the frozen file — is
escalated, not taken).** A coordinator snapshot gate, isolated in one type so that option A could
delete it: an attempt pipeline sends `SnapshotBegin` before each snapshot it adds and waits for the
grant, and `SnapshotEnd` after removing it; a pipeline that terminates releases whatever it held.
When selection returns an integration whose candidate is predicted **stale** — its recorded base is
not the log's authorized head, which is `decide`'s fast test on a non-foreign ref — while an attempt
snapshot is live, the coordinator takes no reservation, closes the gate, admits nothing else and
keeps processing messages; when no attempt snapshot is live it **selects afresh** (an answer
ingested meanwhile can put another candidate at the head of the queue). Outside `verify` the
coordinator is running `integrate()` synchronously and grants nothing; inside `verify` the gate
grants again, and once the verification's completion has arrived it stops granting and `verify`
returns only when no attempt snapshot is live — so every snapshot the frozen reclaim removes is the
integration's own. A fast integration never reclaims and needs no gate. No wait cycle: a pipeline
asks for a snapshot grant holding no slot (the worker's pair is released before the capture), and
a pipeline holding a snapshot takes its review slots from the loop that is still running.

### R-X — the Git registry lock (the memo's R1), readers included

**Packet.** `side_effect_vs_event_ordering`: "unchanged"; the memo's measurement (memo §2.3,
`git-worktree-add-prune-race-*.log`): `git worktree add` concurrent with `git worktree prune` failed
2–3 adds in 600 with four loops, none serially.

**Code — where the memo is incomplete.** The memo lists the three prunes of
`remove_worktree_proving` (`workspace_manager.rs:2972`, `:3011`, `:3034`), the direct admin-directory
removal (`:3015`) and `git worktree add` (`:2651`), and says the manager's production code does not
run `git worktree list`. It does: `revalidate()` (`:1659-1680`), at the head of nearly every
funnel — the intent writes and removals, the worktree and snapshot adds, the removals' repair path
— runs `worktree_records()` (`:4964-4975`), which is `git worktree list --porcelain -z`, and so do
`quiescence` and `assert_publishable`. And the removal gate `revalidate_removal_proving`
(`:5026-5157`) reads every registration's `gitdir` directly and **refuses** an entry with `locked`
and no `gitdir` or an empty `gitdir` (`:5100`, `:5116`) — which is exactly an add in flight, as its
own notes say. Measured anew (`~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_impl_c/git-worktree-list-race.log`, script
`race-src/race-list.sh`, Git 2.43.0): with four add/remove/prune loops of 300 and four `git worktree
list` loops, 8, 7 and 10 lists failed in three runs of about 7,500 each ("failed to read
…/commondir: Success", "failed to read '…/locked': No such file or directory"); with adds alone
