(no removal, no prune), 1 of 1,979 lists failed in one run and none of 1,962 in the next. A failed `revalidate()` inside `remove_intent` or `verify_worktree` runs
the torn-registration repair (`:2212-2223`, `:5235-5281`), which could then remove a registration
another thread is creating.

**Reading (disposition 7, extended to the registry's readers).** Every access to one repository's
worktree registry is serialized by a private, process-local lock keyed by the common git
directory, taken inside the `WorkspaceManager` funnels: the `git worktree add` child; the whole of
`remove_worktree_proving` from its registration scan to its prune (scan, admin-directory removal
and prune are one critical section, so the scan never sees an add in flight); `git worktree list`
in `worktree_records`; and the torn-registration plan's scan. The lock guards no data, is never
nested (each holder waits only for its own Git child or filesystem operation), and adds no
externally reachable fn to the classified module (so `effects/wrappers.toml` does not move); the
reason is stated at the lock. Witness: threads adding and removing snapshots through clones of one
`WorkspaceManager` on one repository never fail. **Residual, recorded in §11:** a host-runner agent
that runs `git worktree prune` (or `git worktree add`) in its own worktree is outside this process's
lock and can still race an engine add at width > 1; the container runner's disposable Git view
prevents it there (`design/26:442-447`). Filed in phase 7 as `PR11-HOST-AGENT-PRUNE-RACES-AN-ENGINE-ADD` (§6). The
witness and its mutation run are in §8 (phase 3): with the lock made per call it fails in 7 of 10
runs, and with the lock it passes 20 of 20.

**Corrected in round R1 (§13): "never nested" and "each holder waits only for its own Git child"
were false as built.** The removal's critical section enclosed its funnel's `Before` and `After`
hooks, so that holder waited for whatever its observer did; an observer that listed the worktrees
took the same non-reentrant lock on the same thread and waited on its own caller for ever (the early
review's `R1-REG-1`, executed by the reviewer and reproduced here). **As repaired:** no holder calls
out while it holds the lock. The removal's scan runs under the lock before any hook and binds the
registration the removal acts through — so its refusals come first, and a registration exchanged at
the `Before` hook is still refused inside the funnel; the removal of the checkout, the administrative
directory and the prune take the lock again inside the funnel and release it before the `After` hook.
Every add, list, scan and prune still runs under the lock, so none sees another half done; what the
split admits is a *complete* add of another slot between the scan and the removal, which the prune
leaves alone (its checkout exists). The four holders are the add's Git child, the list, the two scans
and the removal's mutation; none calls a hook or a registry reader.

### R-Y — snapshot identity (the memo's R2)

**Code.** `SnapshotName::gates(generation, attempt)` and `review(generation, attempt, reviewer)`
(`src/workspace_manager/naming.rs:108-116`) name no task, so two tasks at the same generation and
attempt name the same slot; `AttemptContext::discard_residue` removes every snapshot intent of the
execution root (`attempt.rs:639-647`). **Where the memo is incomplete:** the attempt judge
(`attempt.rs:668-694`) is not the constructors' only production caller —
`runner/container/exec.rs:146-167` builds `SnapshotName::gates(0, 0)` as a representative of the
snapshot namespace, to name its directory.

**Reading (disposition 8).** The two existing constructors take the task key as their first
parameter — `k<key>-g<gen>-a<att>-gates`, `k<key>-g<gen>-a<att>-review<n>` — and no constructor is
added (a new reachable fn in a classified module); the judge passes `site.key`; the namespace
representative passes key 0, which changes nothing it computes (the namespace is the first
component, `snapshots`). No instrument pins the name's format (the orchestrator checked
`effects/*`). `discard_residue` removes only the snapshots the attempt's own names produce. The
Windows 220-character budget for a path ending in `/.git` is re-measured with the longer names (§8).

### R-Z — the cleanup-lease paths reach every pipeline's reaper (the memo's Q-B, route 1)

**Code.** The Unix reaper takes its shared R28 hold on the run directories the **spawning thread**
has entered (`spawn_reaper`, `src/agent/proc.rs:2484-2500`, reads the thread-local
`rundir::active_cleanup_lease_paths()`); `step` enters the run's scope on its own thread
(`run.rs:696`); a scope is `!Send` and thread-local, and its only entry points are the run lock and
the frozen `RunHandle` (memo §3.3). A pipeline thread therefore has no scope.

**Reading (disposition 6).** The coordinator enters the run's scope once, as `step` does, reads its
lease paths and carries them to every pipeline, which passes them in every `RunnerCall`; the host
runner hands them to the process funnel, and `spawn_reaper` takes the union of the thread's scope
and the carried paths. The changes are a field and builder on `RunnerCall` (`contract.rs`, not a
classified module) and parameters of existing functions in `host.rs` and `proc.rs` — no new
reachable fn in a classified module, so no instrument moves. Route 2, a `pub(crate)` scope ticket
in `rundir.rs`, would move `effects/wrappers.toml` and is rejected. The container runner spawns no
process through the funnel and ignores the paths. Witness: a process spawned from a thread that
entered no scope, with the paths carried in its call, is supervised by a reaper holding the lease;
the control without them holds nothing.

### R-AA — completions and identity

**Packet.** `completion_identity` (quoted in R-S): "a completion applies only if its identity is the
current open one; otherwise discarded with a non-durable warning; duplicates refused (INV-20); no
completion is applied after the fold is poisoned by an append error". INV-20's enforcement:
"identity check in the coordinator loop before any append".

**Reading (memo §6).** Before any append a completion passes, in order: (1) a poisoned fold —
discard; (2) a `PipelineId` not in the live table — discard, warn, count; (3) an identity other than
the table's — discard, warn, count; (4) an entry marked cancelled — discard and count; (5) the fold
check — an attempt's generation must be `InFlight` with that attempt number, a verification's must
be the open transaction's started verification of that sequence and candidate, read through
`Standing` in `select.rs` (R-N; the coordinator module names no `TopologyFold`); (6) otherwise the
entry is removed and the completion applied. A stale settlement that reached the fold would fail
`plan_transition` and end the command (`emit.rs:237-240`), so the check is the coordinator's, and
the fold's refusal stays the replay-side guard. Warnings join `TopologyRun::warnings`; a discard
counter sits beside them. An `Ended` or `Admit` naming an invocation that is not the sending
pipeline's is discarded and counted; a duplicate `Ended` of the pipeline's own invocation reaches
the ledger, which ignores and counts it (phase 2). The tests' injector (R-AD) is checked like a
pipeline and is never trusted as one: an injected completion, end or request is refused and
counted, and settles or releases nothing.

**Corrected in round R5 (§13).** The injector's refusal is the last check in `accepts`, and the tests sent every forged
completion through it, so (3) and (5) were never under a test: with either removed, or both, every injected completion
still ended discarded and counted (the full review's `FULL-SC-2`, executed by two lenses). Both are now proved on the
channel a pipeline sends on — a completion sent by the pipeline's own client into the coordinator's inbox and taken by
the production intake — and the two injection tests assert which check refused each injected completion. On the
production intake (3) and (5) are defence in depth: a pipeline's identity is fixed when it is spawned, and an identity
closes in a step after which the admission pass stops the pipeline before its completion is applied.

### R-AB — pipeline errors, idle-only arms, the overshoot bound, and cancellation is never an outage

**Reading (disposition 9; memo §5, §6, R4–R6).**
- **A pipeline that returns an error** (a Runner error the body does not settle, a Git error in the
  capture or the judgement, a refused registration) ends the command as `step`'s `Err` does at
  width 1: the coordinator cancels every other pipeline, waits for each to terminate, discards what
  they return, and ends the command resumably; no `attempt_interrupted` is appended live
  ("permitted_transitions: none new"); the next resume settles the open attempts interrupted. A
  coordinator-side error (a settlement's Git failure, a refused dispatch) ends it the same way. A
  panicking pipeline is an error of the same kind (R-R).
- **Backoff, hard block and closure are taken only when no pipeline is live**, by the same
  functions `step` runs; with pipelines live, those arms mean "wait for the next message". The cost
  is latency: a deferred item waits for the in-flight pipelines; `defer_wait_elapsed` is never placed
  among in-flight settlements.
- **The budget overshoot bound** is the unknown spend of at most `max_parallel` live pipelines, each
  running one invocation at a time: `select` checks the ceiling against settled spend only. It is
  stated in the notes and, from phase 4, the report text — never in a durable field ("durable_events:
  none new"). *Corrected in round R5 (§13, `FULL-SC-4`):* "each running one invocation at a time" was read as
  the bound, one invocation per pipeline. The exposure is the **remaining spend** of every pipeline admitted
  before the stop — at most `max_parallel` of them — each running the rest of its body after the stop, its gates,
  review passes and re-asks included; no count of invocations bounds it.
- **A cancelled Runner error is never classified as an outage**: the width-1 verification mapping
  and `review::run_review`'s review-unavailable mapping (`src/review.rs:549`) test
  `RunnerError::is_cancelled` first and return the error. The legacy path never cancels, so its
  behaviour is unchanged.

**Corrected in round R1 (§13).** Two claims above held for attempts and not for everything a pipeline
ends with. *Pipeline errors:* a verification's error completion was held until the snapshot drain
before it ended anything (the early review's `R1-CONC-2`); as repaired, every fatal completion — a
caught panic included — is classified as it is received and interrupts at once, inside `verify` as
outside, before anything the pipeline held is retired or granted on. *Release:* "waits for each to
terminate" was true of the pipelines and not of their invocations — an invocation that ended with its
process unresolved, or was never reported ended, was released by the ledger and its pair granted on
(`R1-CONC-1`). As repaired, such an invocation keeps its registration and pair for the rest of the
process, and is itself an interrupt; the command ends under this reading's error path.

### R-AC — a run ending with pipelines live, in phase 3

**Reading (disposition 10; closure under concurrency is phase 4's).** Phase 3 handles an ending run
minimally: admission stops. A **halt** (a halting settlement appended while other pipelines are
live) cancels every live pipeline and waits for each to terminate, discarding what it returns; a
**budget stop** drains — the live pipelines settle naturally (the fold accepts their settlements
while ending, memo §4.2) — and admits nothing. When nothing is live the closure runs as at width 1.
If it would need closure steps (2)–(4) — an in-flight attempt, a promoting generation, an unresolved
transaction, which is what a halt's cancellation leaves — `closure::refuse_unclosable`'s refusal
stands, nothing is appended, and the command ends resumably; phase 4 replaces that refusal. A halt
inside `verify` leaves the transaction `VerificationStarted` for the same refusal.

### R-AD — deterministic intake and the quiescence observer

**Packet.** `tests_acceptance.determinism`: "deterministic sequential scheduler; seeded scheduling
permutations under Tokio; no sleeps".

**Reading (memo §8).** The coordinator tracks, for every live pipeline, whether it is running on
its own, waiting for a coordinator reply, inside a granted invocation, or finished.
`run_concurrently` takes an optional quiescence observer (a test seam; production passes none).
With none, messages are processed as they arrive. With one, the coordinator buffers what arrives
and processes it only when no pipeline is running on its own, in a canonical order (by
`PipelineId`, then arrival), and
when nothing is buffered it hands the observer the granted invocations — the scheduler completes one
of them in the test runner, chosen by its seed — or lets it inject a forged or recorded message
(ST-01, ST-02, ST-06). So a seed reproduces the same run (true only as repaired in round C1; see
the correction below), and a stuck run (quiescent, nothing to release) is an error rather than a
hang. The test runner records per-invocation results (keyed by
the request), and `durable_at_spawn` parses complete lines only, since the coordinator may be
mid-append when a pipeline's process starts.

**Corrected in round C1 (§13): as built at `aea75a79` this reading claimed more than the code
delivered.** The coordinator marked a pipeline `Invoking` when it *sent* the grant. A pipeline
between its grant and its Runner call was therefore running on its own while the coordinator
applied the next message, appended, cancelled, or asked the observer. "No pipeline is running on
its own" was false for it, and a seed did not reproduce the run. Three things could vary: which
granted calls a cancellation found started, the order processes started in, and the log each saw at
its start. The first two are measured (§13); the third is read from the code. The order in which
the coordinator applied messages did not vary. CI met it on all three test legs. **The reading as
repaired:** under an observer, every grant is handed to it at once
(`Quiescence::granted`, from `started`), and the coordinator does nothing else until the observer
returns. The observer returns when the invocation is inside the Runner, which only the test's runner
can see. So every invocation `Invoking` names is inside the Runner whenever the coordinator acts.
The test scheduler makes that checkable: its runner starts no call until the scheduler admits it,
and at every quiescent point the scheduler asserts that each granted invocation is inside. A seed
now reproduces the invocations released, the processes started and their order, the log each saw at
its start, and the canonical log (`one_seed_reproduces_one_run_down_to_what_each_process_saw_when_it_started`).
Not reproduced: the order in which one interrupt's simultaneous cancellations are recorded by the
double, which is unordered.

**A further correction, round R1 (§13).** On cancellation the coordinator marked every live pipeline
`Running`, including one `Awaiting` whose request was still in the intake's buffer, unapplied; the
intake then waited on the inbox for a pipeline that was waiting on the intake, for ever — a halt ingested
in the pass after a pipeline's end whose next request was buffered behind it hung the command
(`a_halt_answers_a_request_its_intake_still_buffers`, red at `898e7338` by its watchdog). As repaired, a
cancelled pipeline inside an invocation is `Running`, and one waiting for a reply only once its reply is
refused; a buffered request is answered when the intake applies it. Production has no buffer.

### R-AE — a granted invocation cancelled before its process starts (round C1)

**Packet.** `cancellation`: "shutdown or halt cancels pending requests, cancels/releases granted and
non-slotted invocations after termination (processes and containers)"; `permits.protocol`:
"… complete(invocation_id) releasing any slots … or cancel(invocation_id) …".

**Code.** A cancellation that lands between a grant and the pipeline's Runner call reaches the
Runner as an already-cancelled call. The host runner starts nothing and reports
`RunnerError::cancelled(.., NeverStarted)` (`a_call_cancelled_before_it_starts_spawns_nothing`), and
so did the test double. The pipeline reports `Ended{completed: false}` after the call returns
(`attempt.rs`, `execute_typed`; `preflight.rs`, `Registering::settle`), and the coordinator cancels
the registration in the ledger once (`end_invocation`). The double recorded **no ending** for such a
call: no `Ran`, nothing in `endings()`.

**Reading.** A granted invocation cancelled before its process starts **is cancelled and released
once**. It is cancelled, because its call returns a cancellation and no process runs. It is released
once, as a cancellation, by its pipeline's end report after the call returned — "after
termination", where nothing had to be terminated. The ledger shows it cancelled, not completed and
not twice. The test double therefore records it, as `Ending::CancelledBeforeStart`, distinct from
`Cancelled` (started, then terminated), so an oracle can tell whether a process ran.
`RecordingRunner::ran` still lists only processes that started. Under the deterministic intake as
repaired (R-AD) the coordinator never produces this position by itself. Production's arrival-order
intake can, and a test reaches it with the double's barred door (`RecordingRunner::bar`):
`a_shutdown_releases_each_invocation_once_whether_pending_unstarted_running_or_finished`.

**Alternative.** Leave the double silent and count cancellations through the ledger only. Rejected:
an oracle reading the double's endings is then blind to exactly the cancellations that race a
grant. That blindness is what let phase 3's shutdown test encode a thread-timing assumption.

### Phase 4's readings

R-AF..R-AL are `pr11_impl_d`'s, written before the code and read against the packet's own sentences
(`~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_impl_d/packet/phase4-sections.json`: `run_end_policy` whole,
`coordinator_integration.append_error_protocol` and `stable_prefix_barrier`,
`sequential_substrate.loop`, PR11's slice contract; `transaction_fault_matrix.json` beside it).
Line numbers are at `83a387bd`, which phase 4 starts from.

### R-AF — closure step (2) under concurrency: what is settled interrupted, and by whom

**Packet.** `closure_procedure` (2): "Halted -> cancel every in-flight attempt and verification (Runner
terminates processes/containers; attempt_interrupted per in-flight attempt;
merge_verification_interrupted per in-flight verification with prepared pin deleted expected-old and
snapshots reclaimed)"; T-ATTEMPT: "at Halted the same terminal is appended by cancellation", its
`resume_action` naming the residue ("snapshot intents and worktrees reclaimed … the task worktree
scrubbed with force"); T-VERIFY: "Halted cancellation appends the interrupted terminal".

**Code.** Phase 3's `Coordinator::halt` (`coordinator.rs:1258`) cancels every live pipeline and
`finish` (`:1301`) drains them before `TopologyRun::close_run` (`run.rs:1445`), which refuses what the
cancellation left (`closure::refuse_unclosable`, `closure.rs:37`, R-AC). Recovery performs the same
terminals on recovery typestate only: `recover::settle_interrupted` (`recover.rs:1401`) and
`finish_integration` (`:1096`) take `&mut PreflightCertified`, and `integrate.rs`'s own reclaim
helpers are private (`:1017`, `:1030`). `closure::refuse_unclosable` and `closure::unclosable` are
pinned by the frozen `recover/tests.rs`
(`closure_refuses_an_in_flight_generation_and_an_unresolved_transaction_before_any_append`: an
in-flight attempt and a started verification each refused, "PR11" and "nothing was appended" in the
text).

**Reading.** The closure settles interrupted **exactly the in-flight identities whose pipelines this
process's coordinator cancelled and saw end** — never an identity it cannot vouch for. The
coordinator records each identity when it cancels its pipeline (`cancel_all`), and the sequence of a
verification it abandons inside `verify` (whose completion may already have arrived and been
discarded); `finish` hands that set to the closure only after every pipeline has ended, so every
process behind it has been terminated by the Runner and every invocation released by its own
pipeline's end report (R-AE). **An identity is vouched only when its pipeline's end established
termination.** A cancelled pipeline's completion is read before it is discarded: when its error carries
`ProcessFate::Unresolved` — the Runner could not establish that the process or container is gone — the
identity is not vouched, the closure is not entered, and the command ends resumably naming the
invocation, so no terminal is appended and no worktree scrubbed over a process that may still run; the
next process's census reclaims the container and its recovery settles the identity. This is phase 4's
side of the early review's `R1-CONC-1` (`~/orch-pr11/reviews/early-review-R1-triage.md`: `Ended` discards
the fate, so the ledger releases an unresolved invocation's pair), whose repair in phase 3's protocol is
round R1's; phase 4 builds nothing on that release, and after an interrupt nothing is granted to anyone
(pending requests withdrawn, every reply refused, every `Admit` refused). *Round R1 (§13):* `Ended` now
carries the fate, an unresolved invocation keeps its registration and pair, and the unresolved fates the
closure reads come from the ends themselves, for cancelled and uncancelled pipelines alike. *Round R2
(§13):* a verification whose result had arrived when the halt was recorded is vouched too — `verify`
records its sequence for every interrupt, and the halt now reaches it (`R2-CONC4-1`); its pipeline had
ended, and each of its invocations had reported an end that established its process gone, or the
command would have ended on the unresolved one. *Round R3 (§13):* "after an interrupt nothing is granted"
held from the step that recorded the halt as the interrupt, not from the halt's append: the admission pass
between them reconciled first, and a withdrawal could grant an open pipeline the pair it freed
(`R3-HALT-GRANT`); the pass now acts on a halt before anything else, and nothing is granted while the fold
records one. `close_run` takes the set: an
in-flight attempt or started verification
outside it is refused with PR10's wording kept ("… PR11 … nothing was appended"); the width-1 loop and
the coordinator's idle closure pass an empty set, so for them PR10's refusal stands unchanged — the
synchronous loop never leaves an attempt or a verification in flight, and a fresh process's recovery
settles a dead coordinator's before the loop runs. A non-empty set outside a Halted ending is refused
too: a budget stop drains, it never cancels. For each vouched identity, in key order and then the
verification (recovery's (d) before (f)): `attempt_interrupted` (lease by kind,
`GenerationLease::expected(false)`, as recovery writes it), then the attempt's residue — the snapshots
its own names produce (`JudgeNames::Attempt{..}.owns`, the scope R-Y gave `discard_residue`) and the
task worktree scrubbed (`dispatch::scrub`); `merge_verification_interrupted`, then the pin deleted
expected-old (`integrate::prune_pin`, public), the staging worktree and intent removed
(`integrate::staging_slot`, public), and **this sequence's** snapshots only
(`JudgeNames::Integration{sequence}.owns`: `s<seq>-integration`, `s<seq>-review<n>`). Every effect
goes through the `WorkspaceManager` funnels recovery uses, in recovery's order (terminal first, then
pin, staging, snapshots); none of the frozen files is edited.

**Alternatives.** (a) Call `recover::settle_interrupted`/`finish_integration` — they take recovery's
typestate, which a live run does not have. (b) Append `attempt_interrupted` for every in-flight
generation the fold shows — the closure would vouch for processes it never cancelled. (c) Reclaim every
snapshot intent (recovery's `reclaim_snapshot_residue`) — at width > 1 an unscoped reclaim is the
defect R-W exists to avoid; the attempts' own discards already cover theirs.

### R-AG — closure steps (3) and (4) at a live run end

**Packet.** (3) "Promoting generations: complete promotion (candidates ref if absent,
task_candidate_created, pin pruned)"; (4) "FastPrepared/Prepared transactions: complete the authorized
publication (assert_publishable, CAS if ref == expected, task_merged); no abort terminal exists in
authority; halting never publishes unverified work"; T-PREPARED: "completed by the closure procedure at
any run end".

**Code.** A promotion (`TopologyRun::promote_candidate`) and a publication (`integrate()` from
`merge_prepared` to `task_merged`) each run synchronously on the coordinator's thread, so no halting
settlement or budget stop can be recorded between their first and last append; an error between them
ends the command, and the next process's recovery step (f) completes them (`finish_promotions`,
`finish_integration`) before its loop runs. The public pieces are `candidate::recovery_for`,
`create_candidates_ref`, `append_candidate_created`, `reclaim_after_creation`, and
`integrate::Authorized::from_fold` and `publish`.

**Reading.** The closure completes a Promoting generation and an authorized (Prepared or FastPrepared)
publication wherever it finds one at a Halted or BudgetExceeded end, through those public functions on
the live run's journal (`RunJournal` gains the `IntegrationJournal` impl `IntegrationCx` already has),
instead of refusing it. A VerificationStarted transaction is step (2)'s, never published. No
coordinator schedule reaches either shape at a live end (above), so the tests construct it: a CAS made
to fail once leaves `merge_prepared` without `task_merged`, and a later halt ends the run
(`prepared_publication_completed_at_run_end`). At a Parked or Complete end neither can exist (the
derivation requires `common`), and an idle run that is not ending with one of them keeps PR10's
refusal with its blockers, which now name the promoting generation or the prepared transaction.

### R-AH — the budget stop under concurrency: `budget_exceeded` first, the drain, and its end

**Packet.** `precedence_consequences`: "the coordinator checks the ceiling before every dispatch, retry,
or integration selection and … appends budget_exceeded before any effect … halt outranks budget: after
budget_exceeded is recorded, a drained in-flight settlement that is halting makes derived_outcome
Halted, remaining in-flight work is cancelled as for Halted, and run_finished(BudgetExceeded) is
refused"; (2) "BudgetExceeded -> drain to natural settlements, and if any drained settlement is halting
the closure continues as Halted from step (2)"; (5b) deferred items never block Halted or
BudgetExceeded.

**Reading.** Every admission pass — outside `verify` and inside it — selects through `admitted()`,
whose ceiling check turns a breach into `budget_exceeded` before anything the admission would have
done, **against spend that is current**: a verification's review records are charged to the run's
spend when its completion is accepted, before the next admission pass, not when `verify` returns after
the snapshot drain (phase 4's side of the early review's `R1-CONC-4`; R-V's own sentence, "charged when
its completion arrives", as it now holds); the run then ends for budget only through the closure, which reads budget only from the durable
stop (`ending_outcome`). **The drain:** while ending, admission and answer ingestion stop (`admit`
returns at `run_is_ending`; `ingest_answers` reads nothing while ending), slot and snapshot grants go on
so live pipelines can finish, and their completions are settled as at any time (the fold accepts
settlements while ending). **Its end condition:** no pipeline is live — every live pipeline has sent its
completion and been retired; inside `verify`, the verification's own completion has arrived and no
attempt snapshot is live (R-W) — after which the next idle pass selects the closure. **The halting
conversion:** a halting settlement drained while other pipelines are live makes the next admission pass
cancel them (the halt interrupt), so the closure runs as Halted from step (2) with them vouched
(R-AF); drained last, it leaves nothing live and the closure reads Halted, since `ending_outcome` reads
halt before budget. `run_finished(BudgetExceeded)` is never attempted after it, and the fold refuses
it (`check_run_finished`). **What this build can drain:** a halting settlement is, live, only a
declined answer (`question_answered` with `decline_halts_run`) — the ladder's terminal failure that
halts (`Next::Fail`: `NoChain`, or no rungs) is never produced on the schema-4 path, where exhaustion
asks a person — and the fold refuses `question_answered` while a budget stop is current
(`check_question_answered`), which is also why ingestion stops. So no halting settlement can be drained
live after `budget_exceeded` at any width: the conversion is the admission pass's halt check, the one a
halt with pipelines live takes (`halt_cancels_in_flight_attempt_at_width_three`), and the closure's
precedence over a halting drain settlement is asserted on a width-three prefix that plants one
(`run_finished_budget_exceeded_refused_after_halting_drain_settlement_at_width_three`). The overshoot bound of R-AB is
unchanged and stays in the notes: the report is `deny_unknown_fields` and carries no width, and a new
report field is a report-schema change this slice does not make (§11).

**Every interrupt cancels at receipt.** A halt, a shutdown and an append error each cancel every live
pipeline in the same coordinator step that records the interrupt (`cancel_all` from `halt`, from the
`Shutdown` message's handling and from `fail`), inside `verify` as outside it; `verify` then returns
without waiting for the snapshot drain. The early review's `R1-CONC-2` (a *fatal verification
completion* held in `verify` until the drain) is a different receipt, repaired by round R1 (§13): a
fatal completion now interrupts as it is received.

**Corrected in round R2 (§13).** The admission pass acted on a recorded halt only while a pipeline was
live (`halted && !live.is_empty()`), so "drained last, it leaves nothing live" also covered the
verification `verify` still held — its result accepted, its pipeline retired, its transaction open —
and that verification was then prepared and published after the halt (review round 2's
`R2-CONC4-1`). As repaired, the pass reads the fold's in-flight work too: the halt interrupts the
verification, and the closure settles it with `merge_verification_interrupted`. A budget stop is
unchanged: it drains, and an arrived verification reaches its natural terminal.

**Corrected in round R3 (§13).** "Every interrupt cancels at receipt" held for the step that records the
interrupt, and a halt is received when its append records it in the fold. The admission pass after that
append reconciled before it recorded the halt, so withdrawing a closed pipeline's request could grant an
open pipeline the pair it freed, and that pipeline's process could start, before the halt cancelled
anything (review round 3's `R3-HALT-GRANT`). As repaired, the pass acts on a halt before it reconciles,
and no grant is delivered while the fold records a halt. The drain is unchanged, and now stated for
grants: under a budget stop a pending request of a live pipeline whose identity is open is still granted,
because step (2)'s natural settlements need the invocations of work already admitted; what stops is
admission — a dispatch, a retry, an integration.

**Corrected in round R5 (§13).** "The conversion is the admission pass's halt check, the one a halt with pipelines live
takes (`halt_cancels_in_flight_attempt_at_width_three`)" named a test with no budget stop, and the planted-prefix test
drained its halting settlement after a shutdown, with nothing left to cancel: disabling the halt branch only while a
budget stop is current left every coordinator test green (the full review's `FULL-SC-3`). The conversion is now
witnessed live: `a_halting_settlement_drained_after_a_budget_stop_cancels_the_live_worker_and_ends_halted_at_width_three`
drains a halting settlement after `budget_exceeded` while another worker is inside the Runner, and that worker is
cancelled, settled `attempt_interrupted`, nothing is published and the run finishes Halted. The settlement is still
planted (`Release::Append`), for the reason above: no halting settlement can be produced live after the stop in this
build. `run_finished_budget_exceeded_refused_after_halting_drain_settlement_at_width_three` proves outcome precedence,
and is described as doing only that. The overshoot of R-AB is restated by round R5 as the admitted pipelines'
remaining spend (`FULL-SC-4`).

### R-AI — an append error under concurrency

**Packet.** `append_error_protocol`: the event is outcome-unknown, the fold is poisoned, the append is
never retried, no report, status, question payload or cleanup is derived from the poisoned fold, the log
is reopened and the stable-prefix barrier established, the command ends naming present, absent or
undetermined, "in-flight invocations are cancelled through the Runner (PR11: pipelines cancelled;
completions discarded)"; the slice contract: "a returned append error cancels in-flight pipelines and
ends the command without folding anything from memory"; T-APPEND's error-return cases (e-w), (e-u),
(e-s).

**Code.** Every coordinator append goes through `TopologyRun`'s emitting paths, which run the protocol
(`emit.rs:264`: poison, the provisional reservation cancelled, the barrier, the report) and discharge its
obligation (3) at once (`EmitFailure::discharging`, `emit.rs:315` → `UncancelledAppend::cancelling`,
`:159` → `InvocationLedger::cancel_all_running`, `identity.rs:867`), then return the report as the
error; the coordinator's `fail` records it as the interrupt and cancels every pipeline, `finish` drains
them, and `check` discards every completion of a poisoned fold (R-AA).

**Reading.** That is the protocol under concurrency, and phase 4 changes none of it; it tests it with
pipelines in flight at each error-return shape. The ledger half of obligation (3) is discharged at the
protocol, as at width 1: each running registration is settled cancelled there, and the pipelines are
cancelled through the Runner by `fail` at once; each pipeline's own end report after its process
terminated is then a counted duplicate that releases nothing (R4: "ignored and counted"). Nothing can
be granted between the two — the fold is poisoned (every standing reads `Holds::Nothing`), the interrupt
refuses every `Admit`, and the pending requests were withdrawn by the same settlement. No closure,
report, status or cleanup follows: `finish` returns the protocol's error, whose text is the report. The
next process follows the surviving prefix through its own barrier (recovery unchanged).
**Alternative, not taken:** release each running registration only after its pipeline ends (as for
shutdown and halt) by deferring the discharge — obligation (3) is discharged inside every emitting call
site (`run.rs`, `attempt.rs`, `dispatch`, recovery's frozen emitter), and deferring it means a second
discharge path through all of them; the early release is observable only as the duplicate count, and it
can grant nothing.

**Corrected in round R5 (§13).** "Observable only as the duplicate count" did not hold. When a cancelled pipeline's
process ended unresolved, its registration had already been settled, so the unresolved end was counted as a
duplicate, and the ledger reported running none, pair holders none and a balance while the Runner had not
established that the process was gone — against `permits.protocol`'s release after termination and round R1's rule
that an unresolved invocation keeps its registration and pair (the full review's `FULL-CONC-1`, executed by its
concurrency lens: `running=[], holders=[], balanced=true, cancelled=2, duplicates=2`). **As repaired**, the coordinator's
broker is built for its pipelines (`PermitBroker::for_pipelines`, installed by `limit_slots` at the coordinator's
entry), and its ledger's half of obligation (3) (`InvocationLedger::cancel_after_append_error`) withdraws the waiting
requests and settles no running registration: each is settled once by its own pipeline's end report after the Runner
call returned, and an unresolved one is kept with its pair. Every other ledger — the synchronous substrate's, the
pre-flight's, recovery's — runs each invocation itself and settles everything at the protocol, as before. No emitting
call site changes: the ledger handed to the discharge decides, so the second discharge path the alternative feared is
not needed. Nothing is granted meanwhile, for the reasons above.

### R-AJ — shutdown: how it is requested, what it cancels

**Packet.** `cancellation`: "shutdown or halt cancels pending requests, cancels/releases granted and
non-slotted invocations after termination (processes and containers), cancels provisional reservations,
discards completions … resume performs the recovery order … coordinator ledgers empty at restart".

**Reading.** A shutdown is `ToCoordinator::Shutdown` on the coordinator's inbox. In PR11 only a test
sends it (the injector); production has no caller of `run_concurrently` (R-G), and PR12 wires the
command's signal handling to the same message — no handler is added now (Tokio's `signal` feature stays
off, R-C, and nothing in the packet needs one before activation). On it the coordinator cancels every
live pipeline, withdraws every pending request (so no head remains to reserve for), refuses every reply
still owed, drains every pipeline (each running invocation released by its own end report after its
process terminated, R-AE), discards every completion, cancels any provisional reservation (none can be
outstanding when a message is applied — each is converted or cancelled within the coordinator step that
took it — so one found is warned about), appends nothing, and ends the command resumably. The next
process's recovery settles the in-flight identities interrupted (steps (d) and (f)) and starts with
empty ledgers (`PermitBroker::for_run`).

### R-AK — an error or a kill inside the closure

**Packet.** `closure_procedure`: "an append error anywhere in this procedure ends the command under the
append-error protocol and the next process repeats the closure from the surviving prefix"; T-FINISH
`kill_inside_closure_recovers`, `append_error_inside_closure_ends_command_and_resume_completes_closure`.

**Reading.** Each closure append propagates (`self.emit(..)?`), so an append error there ends the
command under the protocol like any other. The next process repeats the closure from what survived:
its recovery settles the remaining in-flight identities interrupted (d), closes retained generations
(e), completes promotions and publications (f), recreates open generations (g), and appends
`run_resumed`; `halted_at` is never cleared, so its loop selects the closure at once and appends
`run_finished(Halted)`. Under a budget stop, `run_resumed` clears the epoch's stop, so the resumed run
continues (a raised budget) rather than re-ending — the packet's resumable BudgetExceeded.

### R-AL — a width-3 run that a real resume can follow (tests only)

**Code.** The scaffold's `Wide` fixture (phase 3) writes its log beside a private directory that is not
`<root>/runs/<run id>`, records a run id its manager does not carry, and writes no owner or commit
record, so the frozen recovery order cannot resume it.

**Reading.** Phase 4's resume claims (shutdown, append error, closure error and kill, the budget stop's
deferred items) are made by resuming through the frozen `recover::run_recovery_order` itself, over a
width-3 run laid out as run creation lays one out: `Wide::durable` in `scaffold.rs` (test-only) writes
the log at the public directory's `events.jsonl`, the private half at `<root>/runs/<run id>` with its
owner and commit records, one run id and incarnation throughout, and `Wide::resume` releases the first
process's locks and hands the directory to the recovery order with test seams (a fake container
runtime, a certifying pre-flight, the manager as the ref funnel). The phase-3 fixture is unchanged, so
no phase-3 test moves.

### Phase 5's readings

R-AM..R-AR are `pr11_impl_e`'s, read against the packet's own sentences
(`~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_impl_e/packet/phase5-sections.json`: PR11's `pr_sequence` entry,
`permits.crash_reconstruction` and `os_matrix`, `resource_accounting` R3, R4, R13, R17, R19, R22, R26 and R28 with
`process_local_os`, `granularity_note` and `outcome_equations`, ST-09, ST-10, ST-16, T-CONTAINER, INV-18, INV-21 and
INV-22, G6's matrix rows and its shutdown row; sha256 in `SHA256SUMS` beside it). Line numbers are at `e651379a`, which
phase 5 starts from.

### R-AM — the second coordinator: a real process, and the one container runtime every process reaches

**Packet.** ST-16's `slice`: "PR11 (under concurrency)"; the census tests' notes: "PR11 owns the clause 'under
concurrency' in the sense ST-16 means it, which is **two coordinator processes**"
(`docs/internals/runner/container/census/tests.md`); `crash_reconstruction`, arm (ii): "probe that run's run.lock
non-blocking … held -> live owner -> never touched", and "every step idempotent and tolerant of already-gone so two
concurrent reclaimers converge"; G6's shutdown row: "… dead-owner and earlier-incarnation containers reclaimed while
another live coordinator's containers continue".

**Code.** Production's owner-liveness probe is `LockProbe`, which is `rundir::is_running` (`runtime.rs:288`,
`rundir.rs:2356`): a claim this process holds, else `F_GETLK` on Unix or a `LockFileEx` probe on Windows against the
owner's `run.lock` — a fact about another process that one process cannot produce for itself. The fake runtime
(`fake.rs`) is in-process state. The effect methods of `ContainerRuntime` (`create`, `start`, `stop`, `remove`) are on
`clippy.toml`'s disallowed list, so only the container funnel's allowlisted modules may call them, and
`std::process::Command` is denied to the topology modules' tests, whose spawning primitives are
`workspace_manager/fixture.rs`'s.

**Reading.** A second coordinator is this test binary run again, `--exact` for one ignored child test
(`coordinator::tests::container_coordinator_child`, whose roles are a fresh coordinator at width 3, a resuming one, a
write command's census and a dead owner), spawned by a fixture primitive (`fixture::LinkedChild`) with its stdin and
stdout piped. It is a real process: it takes its own locks, and a kill (`Child::kill`: `SIGKILL`, `TerminateProcess`)
releases them by process death, so every liveness question is answered by the OS through the production probe. What
the processes share is what coordinators on one host share: the repositories and the private root on disk, and **the
container runtime**. The fake stays in the parent, in the daemon's position; a child reaches it through its stdio, one
request line and one reply line per operation (`fake::LinkedRuntime` in the child, `FakeRuntime::serve` in the parent).
So a container any process created is visible to every other process, outlives the process that created it, and
every runtime operation of every process lands in one actor-tagged journal, in one order. The daemon performs a
child's effect through the fake's own bodies (`daemon_create`, `daemon_start`, `daemon_stop`, `daemon_remove`, which the
trait methods now delegate to) and never through the governed trait methods, so no allowance moves. Handshakes are
lines on the same pipes: a child's report is a line its parent reads with a blocking read, and a child's containers
starting are journal entries its parent waits for on a condition every entry signals (`await_journal`). Each such wait is
bounded (`LINK_BOUND`, 120 s), which turns a silent peer into a failure and orders nothing; the one poll is a parent's
wait for a child process to end (`wait_within`, the kill-child pattern's `try_wait` every 10 ms).

**Alternatives.** A file-backed fake shared through the filesystem: std has no blocking cross-process lock at 1.85
(`File::lock` is 1.89), and a kill racing a removal rewrites a removed container back into existence. Real Docker: G7's.

### R-AN — two live coordinators never share a repository

**Packet.** R17: "`tactus-worktree.lock` exclusive hold (the first effect of every write command, taken after its
read-only refusals and held across the startup census and the whole run)"; ST-16 (b): "live coordinator A running while
dead coordinator B's orphan exists in the same private root (**same or different repository**)".

**Code.** The worktree lock is `<git dir>/upstroke-worktree.lock` (`rundir.rs:1857`), one per repository, held by a
run's handle for its whole life.

**Reading.** While A runs, a third write command in A's repository is refused at the worktree lock, before its census.
So "a third write command's census" beside a live A runs in **another repository under the same private root**: the
tests give it a second repository (`repo-y`) beside the first. A dead owner may be in either. The tests give the dead
owner a run directory of its own outside both repositories' run namespaces, because only its `run.lock` and its intents
concern the container census, and a husk in a run namespace would be the run-directory census's business (PR7's).

### R-AO — what "before slot reset" means in the concurrent entry

**Packet.** Scope: "dead-owner and dead-incarnation container reclaim before slot reset under concurrency";
`crash_reconstruction`: "the census completes before slot/reservation state is initialized, before admission, before
any invocation uses an agent's credential volume, and before this incarnation's probes"; `T-CONTAINER.resume_action`:
"… only then initialize slot/reservation state, allow workspace or credential volume reuse, and run this incarnation's
RunnerPreflight probes"; ST-16 (f): "… before slot init, admission, credential use, or its own probes, while containers
it starts afterwards are untouched".

**Code.** A schema-4 process's slot and reservation state is in three places: the pre-flight's ledger (`RunPreflight`,
R4 with R3's table inside, `preflight.rs:69`), which the caller builds before `run_recovery_order` and which stays empty
until `certify`; the run's broker, built by `TopologyRun::resumed` (`run.rs:734`, `PermitBroker::for_run`) from the
handle `run_recovery_order` returns only after step (h); and the command's slot limits, installed by `limit_slots` at
`run_concurrently`'s entry (`coordinator.rs:212`) over an empty broker. The frozen order censuses at step (a), after the
barrier and before (c)'s certify (`recover.rs:940`, `:959`).

**Reading.** "Before slot reset" is before the first registration or grant in any of the three: while the census
reclaims, the pre-flight's ledger holds no registration and no settlement, the broker does not exist, and no limits are
installed; under concurrency, the census's last operation on a dead incarnation's container precedes this incarnation's
first container operation in the shared runtime's order, and the containers this incarnation starts afterwards are a
live owner's to any other census. The frozen chain's typestate holds that order, so **no product change is owed**.
Phase 5 asserts it from the ledgers and the journal rather than from the order of calls: the census's own container
hooks record the pre-flight's ledger at every reclaim site it passes, each record must be empty, the broker must be
empty when it is built, and the journal must put the reclaim first. A fresh run's creation (`create_run`,
`create.rs:1571`) takes no `CensusComplete`; its precondition is stated at `startup.rs`, and no schema-4 production
caller exists (R-G). Noted, not changed.

### R-AP — a coordinator's containers under the deterministic scheduler

**Packet.** R19: "pruned on complete or cancel"; R26: "released on complete (stop/rm, view removed, intent removed),
cancel, or shutdown"; ST-16's container invocation.

**Code.** The scheduler of R-AD holds a granted invocation inside the scaffold double until it releases it. The real
`ContainerRunner` (`exec.rs`) launches each invocation (intent, view, create, start), supervises it by observing the
container, collects, and releases it (rm, view, intent). A runner-level observer would take its `hooks` lock across
every invocation (§2).

**Reading.** A width-3 coordinator's pipelines run the real `ContainerRunner` over the shared fake, with one addition:
the container's process is the double's invocation of the same request. `scaffold::Contained` registers each request,
the fake's start policy `Start::Run` starts a thread that runs the double and returns its output as the container's
execution, and `stop` cancels that invocation and waits for it. So an invocation inside the double is a started
container, the scheduler and its door work unchanged, and the physical inventory — containers running in the runtime,
intents in `<R>/containers`, views in `<R>/views` — can be compared with the invocations inside the runner at every
quiescent point. No runner-level observer is installed. The view is a `DisposableDirView`: R19 as a directory, the role
Git view's content being PR6's and unchanged.

### R-AQ — the process-local rows, observed: R17, R22, R28

**Packet.** `process_local_os`: R17 "empty at every coordinator start; taken only after the command's read-only
refusals", R22 "released within the runner contract", R28 "owned by the reaper, observed by the next coordinator,
refuses the exclusive side until released"; ST-09: "R17/R22 asserted empty at every simulated coordinator start and
balanced at process end (and R17 asserted still empty after every read-only pre-lock refusal), R28 asserted observed
(refusing the exclusive side) while a simulated reaper hold exists and released afterwards"; ST-10 the same for
reconstruction.

**Reading.** *R17* is OS lock state, observed the way another process or a fresh probe sees it: `rundir::is_running(<run
dir>)` (this process's claims, else the OS probe) and a worktree-lock acquisition (the tests' `holds_nothing`). At every
simulated coordinator start in phase 5's tests — a child's start, an in-process resume — nobody holds the run or the
repository; at every process end, killed or finished, nothing it took is held; another process sees a live
coordinator's hold as live; and a write command refused before any lock (an unknown run id, recovery step (a0)) takes
the worktree lock in the same process right afterwards, which a claim kept by the refusal would forbid. *R22* is not a
ledger of the coordinator's: a host process handle, and on Windows a private job, lives inside one `HostRunner` call and
is released when the call returns on exit, timeout or cancel (phase 1's tests), and the ambient job is the process's own
membership. The coordinator holds none between calls and joins every pipeline before `run_concurrently` returns, so
"empty at start, balanced at end" is structural for the coordinator. What can outlive a coordinator is a descendant, and
that is observed on Unix: a coordinator process whose three pipelines each run a real host process through the real
`HostRunner` is killed, and every one of those processes is gone once its reaper settles; the reapers hold the run's
cleanup lease (R28) through the paths the coordinator carried to them (R-Z) and release it afterwards. On Windows the
same death closes the ambient job (every child a member from creation, kill-on-close); this box compiles that path and
only CI's `test (winguest)` leg executes it. *R28* is simulated as the packet says, by a real process holding the shared
cleanup lock (`rundir::tests::cleanup_hold_child`): after a width-3 shutdown, the next coordinator's recovery order is
refused at its lock acquisition while the hold exists, the hold is still there after the refusal, and once it is
released the resume proceeds with empty ledgers.

### R-AR — the OS matrix at the coordinator

**Packet.** `os_matrix`: Unix "the cleanup reaper survives coordinator death, settles the dead coordinator's process
groups while holding R28, and additionally kills the dead coordinator's labeled containers, closing the orphan window";
Windows "no reaper; the ambient coordinator-joined Job Object ends every ordinary host descendant … containers are
reclaimed at the next tactus write-command start (orphan window until then; documented …)".

**Code.** The reaper reclaims containers only under a registered scope (`set_container_reclaim_scope`,
`proc.rs:4735`), and nothing in the topology engine registers one: its callers are tests. A container invocation spawns
no process through the process funnel, so no reaper is alive across it (`docs/internals/runner/container/census.md`:
"PR7's `TopologyRun` registers it … and must ensure a supervisor is live across a container invocation, or the window is
closed only by the next write command's census").

**Reading.** Stated per platform. On Unix the group half is exercised at the coordinator (R-AQ); the container half is
PR6's mechanism, tested at its own level (`agent::proc::tests::unix_reaper_kills_labeled_containers`), and **not wired
at the coordinator**. So on every platform a dead coordinator's containers are reclaimed by the next write command's
census, which phase 5 exercises under concurrency (ST-16 (b), (f), (g), (h)): Windows' documented orphan window, and on
this build Unix's as well. A live supervisor across container invocations is a new mechanism, not something a PR11 test
could ask of the scheduler; §11 names it for the orchestrator.

### Phase 6's readings

R-AS..R-AX are `pr11_impl_f`'s, read against the packet's own sentences
(`~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_impl_f/packet/phase6-sections.json`: PR11's `pr_sequence` entry and its slice contract's
`proof_tests`, `tests_acceptance.determinism`, `canonical_trace_projection`, `measurable` and `acceptance_subset`, the
seam rows ST-03, ST-04, ST-05, ST-08, ST-13, ST-18, ST-19 and ST-20, G6 whole and
`admission_and_leases.permits.deadlock_freedom`; sha256 in `SHA256SUMS` beside it). Line numbers are at `4bb04a65`,
which phase 6 starts from. **Phase 6 moves no production line**: every change is in `coordinator.rs`'s test module and
in `scaffold.rs`, which is `#[cfg(test)]` (`src/engine/topology.rs`).

### R-AS — the interleaving suite: where it lives, how it schedules, how many seeds

**Packet.** `tests_acceptance.determinism`: "deterministic sequential scheduler; seeded scheduling permutations under
Tokio; no sleeps"; PR11's gating: "interleaving tests at 3 incl. runtime pool tests; deadlock-freedom"; G6's
adversarial rows 1 and 2: the seam rows "under interleaving", and "seeded permutations: identical canonical
projections where scheduling must not matter; deadlock-freedom under adverse completion orders and reduced limits";
`measurable`: "each acceptance item and seam test is one named test asserting on events, refs, worktree state,
ledgers, or projections".

**Code.** R-AD's observer is the only scheduler the coordinator has: under it every grant is handed to the observer
and nothing else happens until the invocation is inside the runner, and a seed reproduces the invocations released,
the processes started and their order, the log each saw at its start, and the canonical log
(`one_seed_reproduces_one_run_down_to_what_each_process_saw_when_it_started`). Phase 3's seeded tests used it for
ST-03 (16 seeds), ST-08 (8) and deadlock-freedom (12, one limit configuration, one order), each asserting a final
state or a projection.

**Reading.** The suite is a nested module of the coordinator's inline tests, `mod interleaving` inside
`coordinator.rs`'s `mod tests` — no new file, so `WHOLE_FILE_TEST_MODULES` does not move, and the module reaches the
scheduler's fixtures and the coordinator's private types (`Identity::owns`, `Quiescent`, `ToCoordinator`) as the
parent tests do. Its observer, `Interleaver`, is R-AD's scheduler with what the rows need: a record of every
quiescent point (the invocations held, the provisional entitlements outstanding, the reservation peak, the discard
and duplicate counters), four release orders (seeded; newest-granted-first; oldest-granted-first; gates first and
agents last), an optional injector drawn from a second seeded stream so that an injected run releases exactly what
the uninjected run of the same seed releases, and an optional watch. It releases exactly one held invocation per
point, so a run's points are its released processes.

**The step bound.** `STEPS = 256` quiescent points per run. Every point releases one process or makes one injection,
and the largest run the suite drives starts 41 processes (a four-task plan with a retry, two reviewers per attempt and
per verification, and a re-ask of every first review pass, §8), so a run that needs more than 256 points is not
progressing; beyond the bound the observer releases nothing, which the coordinator turns into its stuck error
("every live pipeline is waiting on the coordinator or its observer and the observer released nothing … the run is
resumable"). A stuck run, and a run the bound cuts, is an error and never a hang
(`a_scheduler_that_stops_releasing_ends_the_run_as_stuck_rather_than_hanging`); every deadlock-freedom run asserts it
finished within the bound. Both tests run their scenarios under the parent module's `bounded` watchdog (120 s, the
round-R1 helper), so a regression that waited for ever instead of erroring fails the test rather than hanging the suite;
the deadlock-freedom test bounds each of its twenty runs on its own, since CI's library suite runs 2.2 to 6.6 times this
box's time (the library's "finished in" at round R3's CI: 270 s on ubuntu, 451 s on the guest, 755 s on macOS,
`~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_repair_r3/ci/job-test_*.log`; about 114 s here, `~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_impl_f/dev/suite-1/test.log`) and one bound
over twenty runs would approach 120 s on the slowest leg.

**Seed counts, and why these.** The cost of a seeded width-3 run on this box is about 0.3 s (0.45 s with two
reviewers and a retry, §8), and each seed is a schedule reproduced exactly, not a sample, so a count buys breadth, not
confidence in a flaky property. Eight seeds for each whole-run row (ST-04, ST-05 twice per seed, ST-13, the chain,
the halt), sixteen for the independent projection (the report G6 asks for, twice phase 3's count), six for the budget
stop, three or four per configuration in the pool tests (each asserts a per-seed fact the scheduler makes structural
at the first quiescent point, R-AV), and, for deadlock-freedom, two seeds and three fixed adverse orders for each of
four limit configurations — twenty runs, against phase 3's twelve seeds of one configuration. These are the counts on
Linux and macOS; on Windows each test runs the fewest schedules its assertions compare (phase 7's R-AY). The whole suite runs in
about 21 s on four test threads on this box (`~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_impl_f/dev/interleaving-6.log`, at `6ff28cc0`; §8).

**Alternatives.** (a) A new whole-file test module (`coordinator/tests/interleaving.rs`) — rejected: it moves
`WHOLE_FILE_TEST_MODULES`, an instrument. (b) Extending the parent module's `Scheduler` in place — rejected: every
earlier scheduler-driven test would change shape; the new observer is additive. (c) Random seeds per run — rejected by
the packet ("seeded").

### R-AT — ST-13 at every append: what is observed where

**Packet.** ST-13: "a provisional dispatch, retry, or integration reservation converts to fold-derived at its first
append or is cancelled on pre-append failure/run end/shutdown/poisoned fold … the fast path converts the pair
atomically at merge_prepared(fast) with no staging effect between selection and the append, both derived holdings
present at PreCAS and PostCAS, both released exactly once at task_merged; the process-local ledger balances at process
end and is empty at start"; its slice for PR11: "ledger under concurrency".

**Code.** Every append runs `emit` (`emit.rs:224-262`), which after a successful append calls
`TopologyHooks::folded(fold, events)` with the fold and the events and nothing else; the reservation ledger lives in
`TopologyRun`'s broker, which the coordinator holds mutably across the append, and its conversion follows the append
in the same function (`run.rs:1037`, `:1431`; the frozen `integrate()` calls `IntegrationJournal::converted`). A
quiescent observer sees `&TopologyRun`, whose public readers include `entitlements_held()` (the entitlements
provisional reservations hold) and `reservations_peak()`.

**Reading.** ST-13 is asserted at three levels, each where the thing it asserts is observable without a production
change. *At every append* (`Tracing::folded`, and the effect and run-directory hooks, on the coordinator's thread):
the fold-derived pipeline and merge holdings stay within `max_parallel` and one; each first append — `task_dispatched`,
a retry's `attempt_started`, `merge_verification_started`, `merge_prepared(fast)` — moves the derived holdings by
exactly its reservation's entitlements, in that one append (an integration's pair from no merge to one); no staging
effect (`Worktree.WriteStagingIntent`, `Worktree.AddStaging`, `Ref.PinPrepared`, `Object.ProposalCherryPick`) falls
between a fast integration's selection — the append before it — and its `merge_prepared`, or between that and its
`task_merged`; both hook phases of `Ref.CompareAndSwapIntegration` inside a fast sequence see the merge held; and
`task_merged` releases both holdings in one append. *At every quiescent point*: no reservation is outstanding
(`entitlements_held() == 0`) and the peak is one. *At the ends*: empty at process start; at process end taken ==
converted, converted == the number of first appends in the log, none cancelled or duplicated, balanced. What this does
not observe is the ledger *inside* the coordinator step between a reservation and its first append; the counts at the
end and the empty ledger at every point bound it (a reservation that converted late, or twice, or never, changes one
of them), and the derived holdings at the append show the conversion's effect. The same runs carry ST-10: the live
fold equals a replay of its own log after every append.

**Alternative, not taken.** Pass the reservation ledger to `folded`, or add a production reader for it — a
production change the test does not owe (the packet's `production_effect: none`), and the conversion is already
pinned at unit level (`permits::tests::each_kind_converts_at_its_first_append_and_the_total_never_moves`,
`the_fast_path_converts_its_pair_at_merge_prepared_and_releases_both_at_task_merged`).

### R-AU — the G6 raw outputs: an exporter the gate turns on

**Packet.** G6 `required_artifacts`: "gate report", "seam-test outputs ST-01..ST-20", "permutation projection
equivalence report", "slot, invocation, provisional, entitlement, and container ledger outputs", "diff proof that
fold/queue/merge/repair/recovery modules are unchanged from G5"; `pass_fail_rule`: "… ledgers balance, projections
equivalent …".

**Code.** PR10's ST-07 export (`src/observations.rs`): when `UPSTROKE_HOOK_OBSERVATIONS` names a directory, every
harness adapter writes what it observed to a file named after the test thread and the process; a separate ignored
merge check reads the directory back (PR10's record, §3 R14).

**Reading.** The same shape, in test code alone. When `UPSTROKE_G6_EXPORT` names a directory, each test of the suite
writes what it measured, as JSON, to `<dir>/<kind>/<test>.json` (`<test>` the test's name, the last segment of the
thread name libtest gives it):

- `seam/ST-04`, `seam/ST-05` (which carries ST-01, ST-02 and ST-06's injections too), `seam/ST-13` (which carries
  ST-10's per-append replay), `seam/ST-17` (the halt and the budget stop), `seam/ST-18`, `seam/deadlock-freedom`,
  `seam/runtime-pool` (one file per runtime pool test): per seed or run, the counts and facts each test asserted;
- `projection/independent` and `projection/chain` — the permutation projection equivalence report: per seed, the
  sha256 of each key's canonical attempt-level projection and the final integration tree OID (independent tasks), and
  the sha256 of the whole canonical projection at width 3 per seed beside width 1's (the chain), each asserted equal;
- `ledgers/slot`, `ledgers/invocation`, `ledgers/provisional`, `ledgers/entitlement`, `ledgers/container` — the five
  ledgers G6 names: per seed, pairs granted and released; registrations, completions, cancellations and duplicates;
  reservations taken, converted, cancelled, duplicated and the peak; the derived holdings after every append; and the
  container, intent and view inventory at every quiescent point with the names created and removed.

Unset, nothing is written and the suite is an ordinary part of `cargo test`. **How the gate invokes it** (the
wrapper's form on this box; CI's form without the wrapper):

```bash
export UPSTROKE_G6_EXPORT="$PWD/g6-artifacts"   # any empty directory outside the tree
upstroke-build cargo test --lib -- engine::topology::coordinator::tests::interleaving --test-threads=4
```

A passing run is the evidence (every assertion held); the files are its measurements. The rows this exporter does not
produce are the gate's to cite from the tests that hold them: ST-07 is G5's own export (PR10); ST-09's process-local
rows, ST-10's reconstruction beyond the replay check, ST-15, ST-16 and ST-17's scripted shapes are phases 3–5's named
tests (§7); ST-11, ST-12, ST-14, ST-19 and ST-20 are other slices' (their `slice` fields), unchanged here. Phase 6
writes no gate report.

**Alternative.** A separate ignored "merge check" that reads the exports back and judges them, as ST-07 has —
rejected for now: each export's facts are asserted by the test that writes it, so a second judge would re-assert the
same facts; the gate reads the files as measurements. Named in §11.

### R-AV — the runtime pool tests: what "runtime" means here

**Packet.** Acceptance item 3: "slot tests (runtime-reachable): same agent and pool with opposing limits serialize on
the binding limit; two agents with own pools run in parallel; agent without pool uses the agent slot only; agent probes
acquire and release their pair; gate and shell-probe invocations register without slots; broker-only shared-pool
tests labeled"; G6's runtime-pool row; phase 2's tests are the broker-only half (§7).

**Code.** A slotted invocation's pair is `{agent, pool}`: the worker's pool is the attempt plan's (`attempt.rs:930`,
`run.rs:1394`), a reviewer's is its profile's (`attempt.rs:1054`). The scaffold's `WidePlans` gave every agent the
one `pool`. The pre-flight registers its probes through its own synchronous registrar (`preflight.rs`, `Slots::Probe`:
`{agent, none}` for an agent probe, no pair for the shell probe), before `run_recovery_order` hands back the handle the
run's broker is built from.

**Reading.** A runtime pool test drives the coordinator at width 3, on its multi-thread runtime and blocking pool,
through the channel registrar and the broker, with the double holding every process until the scheduler releases it:
the invocations held at a quiescent point are the processes running at once, and since a held set only grows between
points, the points see every set that ever ran together. So "serialize on the binding limit" is a ceiling on the
processes of that agent or pool held at any point, and "run in parallel" is two held at one point. `WidePlans` gains
`pools`, a pool per agent overriding `pool` (`None` for an agent that has none), so two agents can have pools of their
own, or one agent none beside a pooled one; the two agents are the fixture's worker and primary reviewer
(`claude-code`) and its second reviewer (`copilot`). The limits are chosen so the fact is structural at the first
quiescent point, where every task's worker asks at once: at two slots per agent and one per pool an unpooled agent
has two processes held (a pool would forbid it) and a pooled one never two. The pre-flight row runs where the packet
puts probes: a width-3 run resumed through the frozen recovery order with a real `RunPreflight` over a double, whose
adapters (`ScaffoldAdapters::probing`) send each agent probe through the runner; the shell probe first, then one probe
per recorded agent, each settled once, the pre-flight's ledger balanced before admission, and the coordinator's broker
empty when it starts. Gates register without slots: at one slot per agent and pool, a gate runs while the only slot is
held, and the invocation ledger registers every process while the slot table grants and releases only the agent
processes' pairs. **The broker-only shared-pool tests stay labelled broker-only** (`permits.rs`, `broker_only_*`):
two agents in one pool, and one agent in two, are unreachable under the one-agent-per-pool model. The deadlock-freedom
test does drive a shared pool through the coordinator (R-AX) — as an adversarial limit configuration, not as a
reachable one, and the record says so where it is claimed.

### R-AW — ST-18 at width 3: a live end, killed at every cell

**Packet.** ST-18: "for Complete and Halted: kills after run_finished, after report write, and between every cleanup
effect converge to the outcome equations; a resume of the run finalizes … and only then refuses continuation;
finalization is idempotent across repeated invocations; answer files and .partial residue … are left untouched, never
ingested …"; slice PR10, gate G5 — and G6's adversarial row 1 names it "under interleaving".

**Code.** The coordinator's closure ends in `TopologyRun::close_run`, which after `run_finished` calls the frozen
`finalize::finalize` in the same process (`run.rs:1518`); a resume of a finished run finalizes in the frozen recovery
order (`recover.rs:1048`, `finalize_if_finished`) and refuses continuation. PR10's matrix (`recover/tests.rs`, frozen)
kills at every cell of a finalization over a *planted* finished run whose residue it chooses.

**Reading.** At width 3 the finished run is a live one: a durable width-3 run driven to Complete, or to Halted by a
declined question, under one seed (13, which halts with two candidates queued, so the Halted end retains and lists two
candidates refs). Its finalization consults, after `run_finished`, the report's four cells and then its cleanup
effects' two phases each — for this Complete run three candidates-ref deletions and the execution root; for this
Halted run the execution root alone, the closure having already scrubbed what it settled (§8). A child process runs
the same seed and dies by a kill at one cell (`Killer`, counting consultations after `run_finished` is folded, having
reported the cell); the parent adopts the directory and resumes it through the frozen recovery order: the first resume
finalizes to what the uninterrupted finalization left (the report's outcome, retained candidates and open questions,
the refs under the run namespace, the worktrees Git lists, the execution root's absence), the second finds the report
current and removes nothing, neither appends to the log, and answer files planted after the death stay byte-identical.
**Every cell is killed**, not a sample: twelve for Complete and six for Halted, one outcome per test so that libtest runs the two matrices side by side (§8: as one test it was the tail of the whole library suite on this box). The residue classes a live width-3 end
does not leave (task worktrees, snapshots, staging, pins) stay PR10's planted matrix, frozen and unchanged.

### R-AX — deadlock-freedom under reduced limits and adverse orders

**Packet.** `permits.deadlock_freedom`: "acquisition order pipeline -> merge -> {agent, pool} (atomic pair); slot
holders are running processes that wait for nothing; entitlements are never awaited; provisional reservations are
taken only when the derived count permits and are converted or cancelled without waiting; therefore no hold-and-wait
cycle; head reservation prevents slot starvation"; G6 row 2's "deadlock-freedom under adverse completion orders and
reduced limits".

**Reading.** One slot per agent and per pool at width 3, over a four-task plan with a dependent, a same-generation
retry and two reviewers per attempt and per verification (so verifications run beside attempts), in four limit
configurations — two agents sharing one pool, each agent in its own pool, no pools, one unpooled agent beside a pooled
one — two of them with a re-ask of every first review pass, so re-asks take pairs too. Each configuration runs under
two seeds and three fixed adverse orders: newest-granted-first (a process granted last completes first),
oldest-granted-first, and gates first with agents last (every slot held as long as possible). Every run must reach
`run_finished` within `STEPS`, never hold a pair over a limit at a quiescent point, and release every pair it granted
exactly once. A run that could not progress would end in the stuck error (R-AS), which fails the test by its message
rather than hanging it.

### Phase 7's readings

R-AY and R-AZ are `pr11_impl_g`'s. Neither is in the packet's phase list: R-AY answers the orchestrator's
measured time budget for the merge queue's Windows leg (`~/orch-pr11/answers/pr11_impl_g-1.md`), R-AZ the
close-out's residue measurement. Line numbers are at `1fe988cd`, which phase 7 starts from.

### R-AY — on Windows, each seeded test runs the fewest schedules its assertions compare

**Packet.** `tests_acceptance.determinism`: "deterministic sequential scheduler; seeded scheduling permutations under
Tokio; no sleeps"; PR11's gating: "interleaving tests at 3 incl. runtime pool tests; deadlock-freedom". The workflow
runs the Windows suite on the guest for a pull request and on hosted `windows-latest` for a merge-queue entry, under a
45-minute timeout there (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:148`, an instrument this slice does not edit).

**Measured.** The orchestrator's numbers (job logs under `~/orch-pr11/logs/wintime/`): the library harness's
"finished in" on the guest went from 348.98 s at phase 1 (`6b7a4836`, about master's suite) to 592.80 s at
`1fe988cd`, and the queue's hosted leg took 1631.09 s at #326's merge, 4.7 times the guest's; at PR11's cost the hosted
leg projects past its timeout. This phase's reading of the same logs (`~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_impl_g/wintime/`,
`tools/wintime-parse.py`, `tools/wintime-durations.py`): libtest starts tests in name order, one as each completes, so a
test's start is the completion that freed its thread and its duration follows from the timeline. The model needs at
least eleven threads on the guest and three on the hosted runner to leave no negative duration, so the reconstruction
uses twelve and four (`threads-scan.txt`). At `1fe988cd` the guest's summed durations are 6,365 s against 3,412 s at `6b7a4836`, of which
the coordinator module's tests are 2,896 s (the interleaving suite 1,904 s), and every completion from
`engine::topology::coverage::tests` on lands 198 to 245 s later than at phase 1 — the coordinator's tests sort just
before it — ending, at both heads, with PR10's `kill_after_report_before_each_cleanup_step`, which starts only once
every test sorted before it has had a thread (`delta-by-module.txt`). The seeded tests cost the guest 37 to 44 times
what they cost run alone on this box (`guest-vs-linux-per-test.txt`); one schedule starts two to three hundred Git
processes, more than a quarter of them `git worktree list` (`gitcount/`), nearly all the manager's own.

**Reading.** On Windows each seeded coordinator test runs the fewest schedules its assertions compare
(`scheduled_here`, `coordinator.rs`'s test module): one per configuration it names; two where it asserts equality
across seeds (the independent projections of phases 3 and 6, phase 3's out-of-order permutations, and R-AD's
reproduction, two runs of its one seed); and, for a claim made of *some* seed, the seed that makes it on Linux — seed 1
of the gate test (two gates beside a held slot), and for deadlock-freedom one order per limit configuration, the first
seed, newest-granted-first, oldest-granted-first and gates-first in turn, three of which run a verification beside
attempts. Linux and macOS run every seed, so the counts R-AS gives, and the G6 exports, are theirs. The reason it
keeps every guarantee the packet asks for: a seed is a schedule of the coordinator's logic, which has no platform
branch, and the scheduler reproduces it exactly on every machine (R-AD); what a Windows run adds is Windows' I/O —
process creation, Git for Windows, its paths — and every schedule exercises that alike. What differs on Windows runs
there in full: the ST-18 and closure kill matrices (a child's death and the resume after it), the two-process container
tests, the production container runner, and every scripted shape. **Checked:** with `scheduled_here` forced to the
Windows subsets on this box, all 78 coordinator tests pass, the subsets' assertions included
(`wintime/windows-schedules-on-linux/`), and the module's serial time falls from 93.38 s to 49.92 s
(`wintime/full-schedules-on-linux/test-1thread.log`, `wintime/windows-schedules-on-linux/test-1thread.log`).
**Projected** (`wintime/projection-ce422049.txt`): 1,720 s of the guest's summed durations go, and the guest's
harness lands at 449 to 452 s; the hosted leg at 35.3 minutes at the 4.7 ratio, 24.0 at the workflow comment's 3.2 and
32.3 at its 4.3. A projection, not a measurement: CI's `test (winguest)` leg at the pushed head is the measurement.

**Alternatives.** `#[ignore]` on the heavy tests — not without an owner decision, and it drops the Windows run of what
they hold. Raising the queue's timeout — `.github/workflows/` is an instrument, the owner's. Fewer seeds everywhere —
the Linux and macOS counts are what the G6 exports and R-AS's claims rest on. Fewer ST-18 cells on Windows — a kill's
convergence there is platform behaviour (process death, file handles, Git for Windows), so the matrices stay whole.

### R-AZ — a child test process's temporary directory is its parent's

**Code.** `fixture::neutral_git_config` is a `static` whose `Drop` never runs, so every process that runs the fixture's
Git leaves one `upstroke-neutral-gitconfig-*` directory in its temporary directory. PR10's kill witnesses hand their
children a temporary directory inside a scratch-tree guard the parent holds
(`scaffold::kill_child_and_adopt_in_a_scratch_tree`); PR11's children — ST-18's eighteen kill children, the closure's
two and the two-process container children — inherited the suite's own.

**Measured.** A whole `cargo test --all-targets --all-features` with `TMPDIR` at a fresh directory, everything left
listed with no name filter (`tools/residue-run.sh`): 44 top-level entries at the merge base and 67 at `f81e7bd8`, the
difference exactly 23 more `upstroke-neutral-gitconfig-*` (`residue/compare-base-head.txt`); per test, the kill matrices
leave one per child and the parent's own (`residue/per-test-f81e7bd8/`).

**Reading.** Each of PR11's children is given `TMPDIR`, `TMP` and `TEMP` inside a `kill_dir` guard its parent binds
before the child is spawned and before the run it adopts from the child, so the run is dropped first and the guard then
reclaims what the child left (`scaffold::child_temporary_of`); a container child's is its test's `logs` directory. The
nesting lengthens every path under a child's fixture by the guard's name, which Git for Windows refuses past 220
