characters for a `.git` path (#292's review round 8 met exactly that), so the guards' names are short: the longest
checkout `.git` path a child can make on the guest is 207 characters (`measure/pathbudget-child-temporary.txt`). After
it, the suite leaves 44 entries at `18577661`, the same classes as the base, and its recursive listing, 154 entries, is
the base's class for class (`residue/compare-base-head-18577661.txt`, `residue/compare-recursive-base-head-18577661.txt`).

**Alternative.** Remove the neutral configuration at process exit — a change to master's fixture, and a killed child
runs no exit path, which is the case at hand.

## 4. The refusals PR10 left naming PR11, and what replaces each

| PR10 | PR11 |
|---|---|
| `closure::refuse_unclosable` refuses an in-flight generation, a promoting one or an unresolved transaction at closure (`closure.rs:37-50`) — closure steps (2)–(4) | **phase 4, done** (R-AF, R-AG, R-AH). `TopologyRun::close_run` takes the in-flight identities its driver vouches for (`closure::Cancelled`): the coordinator's halt hands over those whose pipelines it cancelled and saw end, and the closure appends `attempt_interrupted` per attempt and `merge_verification_interrupted` per verification, each followed by its own residue (the attempt's snapshots and worktree; the verification's pin, staging and this sequence's snapshots); a promoting generation is promoted and an authorized publication completed (steps (3), (4)); a budget stop drains. What stays a refusal: in-flight work nothing vouches for — `step`'s closure (PR10's refusal, unchanged: the synchronous loop never leaves any) and a coordinator that did not cancel it (`a_closure_never_settles_in_flight_work_its_coordinator_did_not_cancel`) — and a halt whose cancelled process the Runner could not establish as ended (`a_halt_whose_cancelled_process_is_unresolved_ends_the_command_without_closing`). `closure::refuse_unclosable` keeps its words for the frozen `recover/tests.rs` test that holds them. Phase 3's `a_halt_inside_verification_cancels_it_and_leaves_the_transaction_for_closure` is replaced by `halt_interrupts_verification` |
| `SlotAssertion` refuses a second slotted invocation "rather than queueing" (`identity.rs:150-160`); `Reservations` asserts at most one (`:255-270`) | **phase 2, done.** `SlotAssertion` is gone: R3 is `identity::SlotTable` inside the evolved `InvocationLedger` (R4), granting atomic pairs by the head-reservation FIFO (R-L), releasing a pair only by settling the registration that holds it. `Reservations` holds several at once, one per selected task (R-M), taken through the broker's check against the fold-derived counts (`Reservations::reserve`, `PermitBroker::reserve`), with `take` kept as the sequential substrate's "at most one". The width-1 substrate — the attempt, the pre-flight, run creation's probe pair and `TopologyRun` — uses the broker's ledgers as its one authority, and refuses a pair it cannot be granted at once with INV-18's sentence (R-O) |
| the Runner's synchronous `run` (`contract.rs:243-245`); the per-runner hooks lock (`host.rs:81`, `:220`; `exec.rs:781`) | phase 1: `Runner::run` returns `RunFuture`, a boxed `Send` future, taking the request and a `RunnerCall` (the `Cancellation` and optional per-invocation `SpawnHooks`/`ContainerHooks` observers); synchronous callers use the provided `run_blocking`/`run_blocking_with`; the host and container runners supervise in the future's first poll and terminate a cancelled invocation through the existing terminate path before reporting `RunnerError::cancelled`; a runner takes its `hooks` lock only when a runner-level observer is installed |

## 5. Module diff proof

The set is R-D's. At every head this record claims, both tiers are run and logged; the logs are
cited in §8.

```bash
BASE=$(git merge-base origin/master HEAD)
FROZEN_PROD="src/topology/events.rs src/topology/schema.rs src/events/mod.rs src/events/log.rs \
  src/events/log/premove.rs src/topology/fold.rs src/topology/fold/apply.rs \
  src/topology/fold/check_attempt.rs src/topology/fold/check_candidate.rs \
  src/topology/fold/check_end.rs src/topology/fold/check_integration.rs \
  src/topology/fold/outcome.rs src/topology/fold/parse.rs src/topology/fold/predicates.rs \
  src/topology/fold/region.rs src/topology/fold/start.rs src/topology/queue.rs \
  src/topology/leases.rs src/topology/paths.rs src/topology/registry.rs \
  src/engine/topology/integrate.rs src/engine/topology/repair.rs \
  src/engine/topology/recover.rs src/engine/topology/finalize.rs src/runner/policy.rs \
  src/runner/container/resolve.rs"
FROZEN_TESTS="src/topology/fold/tests.rs src/topology/fold/tests/outcome.rs \
  src/topology/fold/tests/predicates.rs src/topology/fold/tests/questions.rs \
  src/events/log/tests.rs src/engine/topology/integrate/tests.rs \
  src/engine/topology/repair/tests.rs src/engine/topology/recover/tests.rs"
git diff --quiet "$BASE" HEAD -- $FROZEN_PROD && echo "tier 1: frozen production files byte-identical"
git diff --stat "$BASE" HEAD -- $FROZEN_TESTS
```

Tier 2's migration check for a test child the Runner migration touches reverses the call-shape
substitutions on `git show HEAD:<path>` and compares the result with `git show $BASE:<path>` byte
for byte; the substitutions are listed in §8 with the phase-1 measurement. Phase 2 adds one more,
the slot-table rename in `recover/tests.rs` (R-Q): `identity::SlotTable::new()` →
`identity::SlotAssertion::new()`. The whole proof is
`~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_impl_a/module-diff-proof.sh` on the build box, and phase 2's copy with the
fourth substitution is `~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_impl_b/module-diff-proof.sh`; their results are in
§8, and every later phase re-runs the latest copy at its own head.

**At phase 7's head, the final proof.** The script is `~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_impl_g/module-diff-proof.sh` (phase 6's
with this worktree's path), run against the merge base `79979d24`, which is still `master`
(`measure/ls-remote-0028.txt`). At `18577661`, the last commit of phase 7 that changes code or findings, tier 1 passes —
the 26 frozen production files are byte-identical to the merge base, their inline `#[cfg(test)]` modules included — and
tier 2 finds seven frozen test children identical and `src/engine/topology/recover/tests.rs` migration-only (+16 −16:
phase 1's three call-shape substitutions over fifteen lines and R-Q's slot-table rename), so phase 7 changes none of the
eight (`measure/module-diff-proof-18577661.log`); §9 records it again at the head the gates run on. G6's two-step form
(R-D) is, first, G5's range to this branch's merge base — five files of the set moved through the reviewed G5-period
fixes and two more, in their inline tests only, through #322 (`~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_impl_a/phase0/frozen-set-history.log`)
— and then the merge base to PR11's head, which is tier 1 and empty.

## 6. Findings, per row

| id | guard | decision in this slice |
|---|---|---|
| `PR7-R3-ATTEMPT-002-REVIEWERS-TAKE-NO-SLOT` (P2) | PR11 | **witnessed in phase 2** (R-P): every review pass, the attempt's and the integration verification's, is registered with its `{agent, pool?}` pair through the broker before its process starts. Witness: `attempt::tests::a_review_pass_registers_and_holds_its_pair_while_its_process_runs` (a real `Judge`'s pass held mid-run by the scaffold runner; a second process of the reviewer's agent is refused its pair naming the running pass as the holder) and `permits::tests::a_reviewer_and_a_worker_of_one_agent_never_hold_its_slot_at_once` (at `max_per_agent = 1` the reviewer waits for the worker's release). **The file is not deleted in phase 2**: phase 3 moves the review pass onto a pipeline thread, where its registration is made by the coordinator loop, and phase 6's runtime pool tests re-run the serialization under a real runtime; the file is deleted in phase 7 with both tests named, as §6 planned. **Phase 3 made that move**: a pipeline's review pass registers through its channel registrar (R-U), and the coordinator loop registers it in the broker against the standing it reads from the fold at that moment; at one slot per agent and per pool, with two reviewers per attempt and per verification, no seeded order ever has more than one agent process granted at once, reviewers included (`coordinator::tests::adversarial_orders_with_one_slot_per_agent_and_pool_always_reach_run_finished`, twelve seeds). **Phase 6 re-runs the serialization under the runtime**: at one slot per agent a worker and a review pass of the same agent are never held at once, at any quiescent point of any seed (`coordinator::tests::interleaving::runtime_pool_same_agent_and_pool_with_opposing_limits_serialize_on_the_binding_limit`, whose agent's processes are its workers and review passes), nor under reduced limits with re-asks and verifications beside attempts (`reduced_limits_and_adverse_completion_orders_reach_run_finished_within_the_step_bound`, R-AX); **closed in phase 7: the file is deleted** (`18577661`), its witnesses the two phase-2 tests above, phase 3's
`adversarial_orders_with_one_slot_per_agent_and_pool_always_reach_run_finished` and phase 6's
`runtime_pool_same_agent_and_pool_with_opposing_limits_serialize_on_the_binding_limit` and
`reduced_limits_and_adverse_completion_orders_reach_run_finished_within_the_step_bound`; the body's ledger row names them |

## 7. The packet's tests and where each will live

Complete: every row names the tests that hold it and their file; its seed counts are Linux's and macOS's (on Windows, R-AY). Phase 3's rows name the 21 tests it adds (§8); round C1's three are in the R-AB and R-AD rows and the scaffold row (§13); phase 4's rows name the 23 it adds and the one it replaces (§8); round R1's eight are in its own row (§8, §13), and round R2's seven in its own (§8, §13); phase 5's nine — eight tests and the ignored child they spawn — are in its rows (§8); round R3's four are in its own (§8, §13); phase 6's eighteen — seventeen tests and the ignored child two of them spawn, all in `coordinator.rs`'s nested `mod interleaving` — are in the rows below and its own (§8); phase 7's two — review round 4's witnesses — are in its own row (§8, §13), and phase 7 adds no other test: R-AY changes how many schedules the seeded tests run on Windows, R-AZ where PR11's children keep their temporary files; round R5's seven — the full review's witnesses — are in its own row (§8, §13), with the five existing tests whose assertions it strengthens.

Every figure below is in the file named beside it, under `~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_impl_a/`.

**Phase 1's diff.** `79979d24..6140c4a8`: 46 files, 3,303 insertions and 281 deletions, of
which `src/` is +1,988 −234; 20 `#[test]` attributes added and none removed — nineteen tests and
one ignored subprocess helper (`rendezvous_helper`) — listed by name
(`phase1-test-census.log`).

**The module diff proof at `6140c4a8`** (`module-diff-proof-6140c4a8.log`, the script
`module-diff-proof.sh` beside it): tier 1 PASS — the 26 frozen production files byte-identical to
the merge base `79979d24`; tier 2 — seven of the eight frozen test children identical, and
`src/engine/topology/recover/tests.rs` migration-only (+15 −15): with its three substitutions
reversed on the head blob (`impl crate::runner::contract::tests::InlineRunner for ` → `impl Runner
for `, `fn run_inline(` → `fn run(`, `.run_blocking(` → `.run(`) it is byte-identical to the base
blob.

**Development runs** (not gate evidence; the tree was the committed base plus the uncommitted phase-1
changes, each log's `run.txt` listing the status): the full `cargo test --all-targets
--all-features` through `upstroke-build`, launched through `gates/sigdefault.py` (every catchable
signal at its default, empty mask; `SigIgn 0000000000000000` recorded from a child) — the first run
(`dev/suite-1/`) failed one test, `effects::tests::every_production_region_that_stops_early_stops_at_a_module`,
because the test-only `InlineRunner` first sat above `SPAWN_SITE` in `contract.rs` and cut the file's
production region at a trait (the census is right: every census reading the region would have lost
the production code below it); moved inside the test module, the second run (`dev/suite-2/`)
passed: 2,875 library tests, 0 failed, 110 ignored, and the 10 binary tests. The new and contract
tests alone: 42 passed (`dev/new-tests-2.log`), and after the move 35 of the contract and scaffold
tests with the census (`dev/new-tests-3.log`).

**The two targets this box does not run**, type-checked: `cargo clippy --target
x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` found three `cfg(windows)`
call sites in `src/runner/host/tests.rs` the Linux build had not compiled (`dev/clippy-windows-1.log`)
and passed once they were migrated (`dev/clippy-windows-2.log`); `--target aarch64-apple-darwin`
passed (`dev/clippy-macos-1.log`). Compiled, not run.

(`dev/internals-notes-1.log`). A check that every backticked fragment of a touched module's notes
headings still occurs in its source where it occurred at the base (`dev/stale-headings.py`)

(cargo: "Locking 2 packages to latest Rust 1.85 compatible versions", `dev/check-4.log`); the MSRV leg

| `m8-no-residue` | step (2) appends its terminals and then reclaims nothing: no snapshot, scrub, pin or staging | 2: `halt_cancels_in_flight_attempt_at_width_three`, `halt_interrupts_verification` |

**Under contention** (round C1's method: every test process pinned by `taskset` to CPUs 30 and 31,
four concurrent loops of twenty, one test thread each, a fresh `TMPDIR` per process;
`tools/contend-module.sh`). The whole coordinator module at `e9e3a955` (binary `bins/e9e3a955-lib`
under the private target, sha256 `4939ed4a…` in `measure/bin-e9e3a955.sha256`): **80 of 80 runs exit
0 and each of the module's 38 tests passes in all 80**, the kill child ignored as in every run
(`proof/contention-e9e3a955-module/summary.txt`); libtest's "finished in" has median 35.73 s, min
30.98 s and max 56.83 s (`durations.txt` beside it), against 23.50 s for one uncontended
single-threaded run of the same binary (`measure/residue-module-test.log`). At `593f9ac9`, the code
commit, the same way: 80 of 80, each of its 35 tests passing in all 80, median 35.28 s
(`proof/contention-593f9ac9-module/`, binary sha256 `3ddb9740…`). **No test phase 4 adds sleeps or
waits on a clock for an ordering**: the scheduler, the observer seam and the double's door order every
interleaving (R-AD). The one bounded wait it adds is the durable fixture's, before a resume, for the
previous incarnation's cleanup hold to be released (`await_release_of_the_previous_process`: 20 s,
50 ms rests). That hold is a lock another test's fork can keep for a moment by an inherited descriptor,
and the recovery suites wait for it the same way before theirs; the wait orders nothing.

**The Windows 220-character budget.** Phase 4 adds no path class: its tests' worktrees are the width-N
fixture's task worktrees, attempt and integration snapshots, merge staging and intents, under the
workspace fixture's private root, and the durable fixture keeps that layout. A walk of the coordinator
module at `e9e3a955` under a 41-character `TMPDIR` (`measure/pathbudget/module-walk.txt`, phase 3's
`walk.py`) saw 621 distinct `.git` paths, the longest 193 characters. The bound is arithmetic
(`measure/pathbudget/arithmetic-e9e3a955.txt`): under that `TMPDIR` a snapshot's `$GIT_DIR` is 141
characters plus the test's fixture tag plus the snapshot's name; the longest tag in the module,
`coordinator-append-error-flush-after-full-line` (46), with the longest name, `k0-g0-a1-review0`
(16), bounds it at 203, 17 under.

**Residue.** In a fresh `TMPDIR`, one single-threaded run of the module at `e9e3a955` leaves three
`upstroke-neutral-gitconfig-*` directories (`measure/residue-module-left.txt`); the module at the base
leaves one (`measure/residue-module-base-left.txt`, run on round C1's `87b29387` binary, whose compiled
inputs are `83a387bd`'s: `measure/residue-base-binary.txt`). Every test process leaves one by design
(`workspace_manager/fixture.rs`: the neutral Git configuration is a `static` whose `Drop` never runs),
and the two more are the kill test's two children, the torn and the complete shape, each a test
process of its own.

**Notes.** `tools/new-heading-fragments.py 83a387bd` (round C1's script) checks 833 heading fragments
of the four changed modules' notes at `e9e3a955` and reports 0 problems
(`measure/heading-fragments-e9e3a955.log`); `test-internals-notes.sh` and `test-docs-consistency.sh`
are gates (§9). The notes also correct `scaffold.md`'s section on `RecordingRunner::admit`, which

5.71 s (`durations.txt` beside it). The whole coordinator module on the same binary, four loops of twenty: **80 of 80 runs exit 0 and each of the module's 57 tests passes in all 80**, the two ignored children (`closure_kill_child_at_width_three`, `container_coordinator_child`) spawned only by their tests (`proof/contention-ad1a36b5-module/summary.txt`); libtest's "finished in" has median 43.72 s, min 28.62 s and max 52.06 s (`durations.txt` beside it). **No test phase 5 adds sleeps or waits on a clock for an ordering**: the in-process
coordinator is driven by the scheduler as before (R-AD); a parent learns a child's state from the shared runtime's journal
(`await_journal`, woken by every entry) or from the child's own event line; two reclaimers are ordered by the fake's
barrier (`pace`). The bounded waits phase 5 adds decide failures only: a link's reads (`LINK_BOUND`, 120 s), the barrier
(`PACE_BOUND`, 120 s), a parent's wait for a child it killed or expects to finish (`try_wait` every 10 ms, the kill-child
pattern), and, in the Unix reaper test, the wait for the reapers — other processes — to settle their groups and release
their holds (20 ms rests, bounded by 120 s), which orders nothing the test asserts: it asserts that they happen.

call site under `src/` (`measure/r4-class-search.txt`, 71 sites in `-raw.txt`). Under contention (round C1's method:
`taskset` on CPUs 30 and 31, one test thread, a fresh `TMPDIR` per process; `tools/contend.sh`) on the binary built at
`18577661` (`bins/18577661-lib`, sha256 `e35fe1e5…` in `measure/bin-18577661.sha256`): the two witnesses, four loops of
fifty, **200 of 200 runs exit 0 and each witness passes in all 200** (`proof/contention-18577661-r4-witnesses/summary.txt`).

**The Windows time budget (R-AY).** The guest's and the hosted queue leg's timelines, each completion's offset from the
library harness's start (`wintime/timeline-*.tsv`, from the orchestrator's job logs in `~/orch-pr11/logs/wintime/`),
and the durations reconstructed from them (`wintime/dur-*.tsv`): at `1fe988cd` the guest's summed durations are 6,365 s
against 3,412 s at `6b7a4836`, the coordinator module's 2,896 s of it; per module, completions after the coordinator's
tests land 198 to 245 s late (`wintime/delta-by-module.txt`). Calibrated against this box, the seeded tests run 37 to 44
times slower on the guest (`wintime/guest-vs-linux-per-test.txt`); one schedule starts 231 to 274 Git processes
(`wintime/gitcount/`, a counting `git` on the `PATH`: 1,386 over six budget-stop schedules, 2,466 over the chain's nine).
With `scheduled_here` forced to the Windows subsets on this box all 78 coordinator tests pass
(`wintime/windows-schedules-on-linux/test.log`), and the module's serial time is 49.92 s against 93.38 s with every
seed (`wintime/windows-schedules-on-linux/test-1thread.log`, `wintime/full-schedules-on-linux/test-1thread.log`; two
earlier serial runs that executed another tree's binary from a shared slot are kept, marked invalid, and not counted).
The projection (`wintime/projection-ce422049.txt`): the guest's harness at 449 to 452 s against 592.80 s, and the hosted
leg at 35.3, 24.0 and 32.3 minutes at the 4.7, 3.2 and 4.3 ratios, under its 45-minute timeout. CI's `test (winguest)`
leg after the push is the measurement, and the handover records it.

**Residue (R-AZ)** (`tools/residue-run.sh`: one whole `cargo test --all-targets --all-features` with `TMPDIR` at a fresh
directory, a listing taken 60 s in to show the suite writes there, everything left listed with no name filter). At the
merge base `79979d24`, from a `git archive` built on its own slot pool: 44 top-level entries, 154 in all, every test
passing (`residue/base-79979d24/`; a first attempt under a longer `TMPDIR` failed one real-Docker test on its socket
path's length and is kept, marked invalid). At `f81e7bd8`, before R-AZ: 67, the difference exactly 23
`upstroke-neutral-gitconfig-*` (`residue/head-f81e7bd8/`, `residue/compare-base-head.txt`), one per child process of
PR11's tests (`residue/per-test-f81e7bd8/`). At `18577661`: 44 top-level and 154 in all, class for class the base's
(`residue/head-18577661/`, `residue/compare-base-head-18577661.txt`, `residue/compare-recursive-base-head-18577661.txt`),
and the coordinator module alone leaves only its own process's configuration (`residue/coordinator-module-fixed/`).
R-AZ's tests under contention, the binary above, four loops of five: **20 of 20 runs exit 0 and each of the eight
passes in all 20** (`proof/contention-18577661-child-tmpdir/summary.txt`).

**Full runs.** The residue run at `18577661` is a whole `cargo test --all-targets --all-features` through `upstroke-build`
and `gates/sigdefault.py` (child `SigIgn 0000000000000000`), Rust inputs touched first, `Compiling upstroke v0.1.0
(/srv/worktrees/pr11-impl-g)`: 2,992 library tests passed, 0 failed, 113 ignored, and the 10 binary tests
(`residue/head-18577661/test.log`, read by `tools/results.py`). A development run at `240141d1` failed one census,
`runner::container::tests::the_readiness_allowance_names_the_paths_it_is_written_against`, which `f81e7bd8` answers
(§13; `dev/suite-1-240141d1/`). Gate evidence is §9's.

**The two targets this box does not run**, type-checked at `18577661` (the tree was the commit, clean,
`measure/clippy-targets-18577661-tree.txt`): `cargo clippy --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` and `--target
aarch64-apple-darwin`, `--all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`, both pass
(`measure/clippy-x86_64-pc-windows-msvc-18577661.log`, `measure/clippy-aarch64-apple-darwin-18577661.log`). The Windows
type-check is what found the repair's helper dead on Windows (`240141d1`, §13). Compiled, not run.

**The Windows 220-character budget.** Phase 7 nests the fixtures of PR11's child processes one directory deeper (R-AZ):
the longest checkout `.git` path one can make on the guest is 141 + 24 + 26 + 16 = 207 characters, the ST-18 kill
child's, 13 under (`measure/pathbudget-child-temporary.txt`, on phase 4's measured layout). No other path class moves.

**Notes.** `test-internals-notes.sh`: 155 markers, 155 notes files, all resolve both ways
(`measure/internals-notes-18577661.log`, and the gate). `tools/new-heading-fragments.py 1fe988cd` checks 892 heading
fragments of the changed modules' notes at `18577661` and reports 0 problems (`measure/heading-fragments-18577661.log`).

## 10. Legs this box cannot speak for

The ten gates run on this box (Linux, x86_64). They cannot speak for **Windows** — the job-object
side of the process funnel (the new stop branch in the non-Unix supervision loop is executed only
there), `MAX_PATH` in nested fixture paths, the Windows spelling of the shell (the cancellation
test's child is a `cmd` line there) — nor for **macOS** — the reaper's signal behaviour, a
daemonizing descendant's SIGHUP
(`findings/P2_correctness_202609281056_a-daemonizing-descendant-hangs-up-the-role-on-macos.md`). CI's `test (winguest)`,
hosted Windows and macOS legs are the truth for those; a red leg's log is read before a cause is
named.

**Phase 2** adds no platform-specific code — the broker, its ledgers and the registering boundary
are plain data and one `Mutex` — and no process or path handling, but every test that drives the
substrate (the attempt, run creation, recovery) runs real Git and real fixture trees, so the
Windows path budget and the macOS process behaviour above still apply to them; the local
type-check of both targets (§8) says only that they compile.

**Phase 3** adds a Tokio runtime and its threads, the carried lease paths (read only on Unix: the
read in `run_concurrently` is `cfg(unix)`, and elsewhere a pipeline carries none, as no reaper
holds a lease there) and a process-local registry lock, and its tests drive real Git worktrees at
width 3. This box cannot speak for **Windows**' job-object supervision of a pipeline's process when
the coordinator cancels it, for Git for Windows' worktree registry under concurrent adds and
removals (the race of R-X was measured with Linux's Git 2.43 only), or for the snapshot paths on
the guest itself — §8's budget is measured on Linux with the guest's `TEMP` length — nor for
**macOS**' reaper holding the union of carried and scoped lease paths. CI's legs are the truth for
those.

**Phase 3's CI at `aea75a79`** (run 36670986992) was the truth this section anticipates. Every lint
and msrv leg passed. All three test legs failed, on one test at one assertion, with three different
subsets: `test (ubuntu-latest)` `[1]`, `test (macos-latest)` `[]`, `test (winguest)` `[0, 1]`. Every
red leg's log was read, and §13 attributes all three to one cause, a timing race in the test seam
that this box's local gates never met. Cargo stopped after the library's binary on every leg, so the
binary target's ten tests did not run there. **Round C1** changes the quiescence seam's protocol,
the test double and tests, in portable code with no `cfg`. It type-checks for both targets (§8), and
its tests force the race's interleaving with a controlled fake rather than waiting for a slow machine
to produce it. CI's legs remain the truth for how the suite runs there.

**Phase 4** adds no `cfg` and no process or path handling of its own. The closure reaches the frozen
modules' effects through their public functions and the `WorkspaceManager` funnels recovery uses
(`dispatch::scrub`, the snapshot reclaim, `integrate::staging_slot`, `integrate::prune_pin`), and the
durable fixture resumes through the frozen recovery order. Its tests drive real Git worktrees at width
three, append through a real log, resume, and kill two child test processes by abort inside the
closure. This box cannot speak for **Windows** removing a worktree the closure scrubs just after a
cancelled pipeline ended: the tests' Runner is the in-process double, so no agent process holds a file
there, and with a real agent the scrub follows the job object's termination (phase 1). Nor for the kill
child's abort and the lock handoff between two incarnations in one test process on the guest: the
durable fixture's wait for the previous incarnation's cleanup hold is the recovery suites' wait, run
here on Linux only. Nor for the snapshot and staging paths on the guest itself, whose §8 bound is
arithmetic over the Linux walk's layout with a 41-character `TMPDIR`. On **macOS**, the same code is
compiled and not run (§8). CI's legs are the truth for those.

**Round R1** adds no `cfg` and no process or path handling. What it changes runs on every platform the
same way: the coordinator's handling of an invocation's end, of a fatal completion and of a closed identity,
the invocation ledger, and the registry lock's scope in `remove_worktree_proving`. This box cannot speak for
**Windows** holding the registry lock around one more section of a removal, where Git for Windows' registry
and the job-object termination of a stopped pipeline's process (the reconcile's cancellation reaches it as a
halt's does) are the platform's own, nor for the guest running the two watchdog tests, whose scenarios run on a
thread of their own; on **macOS** the changed code is compiled and not run (§8). CI's legs are the truth for
those.

**Round R2** adds no `cfg` and no process or path handling. What it changes runs on every platform the same way:
which in-flight work a halt interrupts, where an admission pass stops while `verify` unwinds, and to whom a grant is
delivered, all inside the coordinator, and the text of one durable detail. Its four coordinator tests run the
scaffold's in-process double (two of them on a coordinator the test assembles, which starts no process at all), and
its three notes pins read text compiled into the binary; this box cannot speak for **Windows** or **macOS** running
them beyond compiling them (§8). CI's legs are the truth for those.

**Phase 5** adds test code only. Two of its tests are Unix-only by nature (`cfg(unix)`: the simulated surviving reaper
and the real reapers of a killed coordinator's host processes); the other six, and the child they spawn, run on every
platform. This box cannot speak for **Windows** running a child coordinator, killing it with `TerminateProcess` and
releasing its run lock with its handles; for the census child's `LockFileEx` probe of a lock a live parent holds; for the
stdio pipes and the JSON lines on the guest; or for the paths on the guest itself, whose bound is arithmetic (§8). The
ambient job's termination of a killed coordinator's host descendants is executed by no phase-5 test on any platform, since
the reaper test is Unix-only; the `test (winguest)` leg runs the container tests. On **macOS** the reapers settling a
killed coordinator's groups — where the macOS signal findings of §6 apply — and every other phase-5 test are compiled here
and not run. CI's legs are the truth for those.

**Round R3** adds no `cfg` and no process or path handling. What it changes runs on every platform the same way: when an
admission pass acts on a halt, and whether a grant is delivered while the fold records one, both inside the coordinator,
and the text of three refusals. Its four tests run on a coordinator the test assembles, which starts no process; they
create task worktrees and no snapshot, under fixture tags of at most 35 characters against phase 4's 46, so no path they
create comes near §8's Windows budget. This box cannot speak for **Windows** or **macOS** running them beyond compiling
them (§8). CI's legs are the truth for those.

**Phase 6** adds test code only, and no `cfg`. Its tests run the scaffold double in process (no agent process runs),
real Git repositories and worktrees at width three, the production container runner over the fake runtime, and the
frozen recovery order; one test spawns eighteen children of the test binary, each dying by `abort` inside a
finalization, and resumes each child's run twice in the parent. This box cannot speak for **Windows** running those
children and adopting their directories (the phase-4 kill child's pattern, which the `test (winguest)` leg has run since
phase 4), for Git for Windows removing the execution root and deleting the candidates refs of a run whose process was
killed, for the guest's cost of eighteen children, or for the paths on the guest, whose bound is arithmetic (§8); on
**macOS** the suite is compiled here and not run (§8). CI's legs are the truth for those.

**Phase 7** adds `#[cfg(unix)]` to review round 4's two witnesses and their helper, and one `cfg!(windows)` read, in
`scheduled_here`, whose only effect is what the Windows legs run (R-AY): the guest's `test (winguest)` and the queue's
hosted `test (windows-latest)` run the seeded coordinator tests' Windows subsets, whose assertions this box checked by
forcing the subsets on Linux, not by running them on Windows. This box cannot speak for **Windows**' time after the
change — R-AY's figures are a projection from CI's own timelines, the guest's leg after the push is the measurement, and
the hosted leg runs only for a merge-queue entry; for Windows refusing a reader thread a stack no address space can map,
because the witnesses are Unix-only (on Windows the constructors' new order is compiled, and exercised by every test that
spawns through them); or for Windows building PR11's children's fixtures one directory deeper (R-AZ), whose bound, 207
of 220, is arithmetic. On **macOS** the witnesses run in CI only — that its `pthread_create` answers `EAGAIN` for an
unmappable stack as glibc's does here is read from libpthread, not run — and so do the deeper fixtures. CI's legs are the
truth for those.

## 11. What is not claimed

- The limits of phases 5, 6 and 7 are their own bullets below. The coordinator of phases 3 and 4 exists, and **no production path reaches
  it**: `upstroke run` writes schema 3, a fresh run's `max_parallel > 1` is still refused by the
  configuration, and a schema-4 run is reachable only from tests (R-G); PR12 activates it and makes
  `engine::assembly::FrozenPlans` owned (R-T). The closure under concurrency is therefore exercised by
  tests only. The synchronous loop's closure vouches for nothing and still refuses in-flight work in
  PR10's words (`closure::settleable` with an empty set); `closure::refuse_unclosable` is kept, with
  those words, for the frozen `recover/tests.rs` test that holds them (R-AF). No production path
  cancels an invocation yet: cancellation is exercised by tests only.
- **Phase 2's broker waits for nothing, and at width 1 nobody waits on it.** Every slotted request
  of `step` is made on its own thread and must be granted at once or is refused (R-O); phase 3's
  coordinator loop hands grants to pipelines that wait for them (R-U). Queued requests, grants
  on release and the head reservation are exercised by broker-only tests; **no runtime pool test**
  (a real multi-thread Tokio runtime driving pipelines that await grants) exists yet — acceptance
  item 3's runtime half and G6's "runtime pool tests" are phase 6's, which re-runs the slot tests and
  the seeded orders under the runtime. *Phase 6 adds them* (§7, R-AV).
- The configured `max_per_agent`/`max_per_pool` are not wired to any schema-4 run: no caller holds a
  `Config` there yet, so `TopologyRun` uses the packet's default, the recorded `max_parallel`
  (R-K). Phase 3's coordinator takes the limits it is given (`PipelineSeams::slots`) and installs
  them before anything is admitted; its only callers are tests.
- A pre-flight agent probe's pair is `{agent, none}`: the pre-flight holds no pool table. The probes
  run one at a time before admission, so no pool contention is possible there at this width.
- A judgement's standing is read once, when the judge is built: sound at width 1, where nothing is
  appended while a judgement runs. On a pipeline, phase 3 reads it at the coordinator for each
  registration: the channel registrar sends the invocation and its pair, and the coordinator reads
  the invocation's `Standing` from the fold at that moment (R-U).
- `PR7-R3-ATTEMPT-002`'s finding file is not deleted in phase 2; phase 7 deletes it (§6).
- No mutation campaign was run for phase 2. The broker's oracles are structural where they can be:
  held counts are derived from the holders rather than stored, a pair is released only by settling
  the registration that holds it, and the seeded test checks the head-reservation invariant after
  every step rather than a final state.
- The Windows and macOS behaviour of the new code is compiled, not run, on this box (§8, §10).
- Cancellation's latency is the supervision loop's tick (50 ms on the host, the configured poll on
  the container runner) plus the termination; the host test bounds the whole at under 60 seconds
  against a 300-second child and does not measure the tick.
- A Runner future of the host or container runner cannot be dropped mid-flight — its supervision is
  inside one poll — so "dropping an in-flight invocation terminates it" is carried by the token for
  those runners; drop-while-held is exercised on the test double only.
- No mutation campaign was run for phase 1. The oracles that fail on the first-bad shapes are
  structural: `a_runner_level_observer_still_takes_invocations_one_at_a_time` is the concurrency
  test's oracle failing under a shared observer lock (the pre-PR11 shape), and the cancellation
  tests cannot pass without the stop branch (a child that sleeps 300 seconds would return an output,
  not a cancellation).
- The adapters' `probe` and `discover` stay synchronous (R-A); design §8's `async fn probe` is not
  claimed.
- **Phase 3.** Closure under concurrency is phase 4's: a halt with pipelines live cancels them and
  leaves what they held open for `refuse_unclosable`, and a budget stop drains (R-AC). An append
  error at width > 1 cancels every running registration in the ledger (`emit.rs`,
  `cancel_all_running`) before the pipelines holding them have ended; nothing is granted afterwards,
  because the fold is poisoned and admission stops, and the processes end through their
  cancellations — phase 4's `append_error_under_concurrency_cancels_pipelines_and_folds_nothing_from_memory`
  is not written here. (Round R5 removes that early release: R-AI as corrected, §13.) A shutdown is a message a test injects; no signal handler sends it.
  Container ownership and the process-local rows under concurrency are phase 5's, and the container
  runner ignores the carried lease paths. The interleaving suite at width 3 and the runtime pool
  tests are phase 6's; phase 3's coordinator tests run under a real multi-thread runtime but are not
  the ST matrix. A pipeline's Runner calls carry its cancellation and the lease paths and no
  per-invocation observer.
- **The residual host-agent prune race** (R-X): an agent on the host runner that runs `git worktree
  prune` or `add` in its own worktree is outside this process's registry lock and can still race an
  engine add at width > 1; filed in phase 7 as `PR11-HOST-AGENT-PRUNE-RACES-AN-ENGINE-ADD` (§6).
- The budget overshoot bound (R-AB) is stated in `docs/internals/engine/topology/coordinator.md`
  only. R-AB's "from phase 4, the report text" is not done: the report is `deny_unknown_fields` and
  carries no width, and a report field would be a report-schema change this slice does not make
  (R-AH). Filed in phase 7 as `PR11-OVERSHOOT-BOUND-IN-NO-OUTPUT` (§6). *Corrected in round R5* (§13,
  `FULL-SC-4`): what the notes state is the exposure — the remaining spend of every pipeline admitted before the
  stop, review passes and re-asks included — not a count of invocations, which the finding and the body had said.
- The deterministic intake is a test seam: without an observer the coordinator applies messages in
  arrival order, and its output is deterministic per key, not in its interleaving (ST-08's
  projection). Round C1 repaired the seam and changed no production behaviour. In production's
  arrival-order intake a cancellation can still land between a grant and the Runner call. R-AE reads
  that position, and `a_shutdown_releases_each_invocation_once_whether_pending_unstarted_running_or_finished`
  constructs it with the double's barred door. "A seed reproduces the same run" (R-AD) covers the
  invocations released, the processes started and their order, the log each saw at its start, and
  the canonical log. It does not cover the order in which the double records the cancellations one
  interrupt fires together.
- No mutation campaign was run for phase 3 beyond R-X's lock (§8), and for round C1 beyond its own
  two mutation witnesses (§13). The Windows budget walk samples every 50 ms, so the maxima it reports
  are lower bounds; the bound for the class phase 3 lengthens is arithmetic (§8).
- **Phase 4.** A shutdown is `ToCoordinator::Shutdown`, which only a test sends: no signal handler is
  added, and PR12 wires the command's signals to the same message (R-AJ).
- **No halting settlement is drained live** (R-AH). In this build a halting settlement is only a
  declined answer, and the fold refuses `question_answered` while a budget stop is current, so none can
  be drained after `budget_exceeded`. The conversion is exercised as the admission pass's halt check
  with pipelines live (`halt_cancels_in_flight_attempt_at_width_three`); the refusal of
  `run_finished(BudgetExceeded)` after a drained halting settlement is asserted on a planted
  width-three prefix (`run_finished_budget_exceeded_refused_after_halting_drain_settlement_at_width_three`).
  *Corrected in round R5* (§13, `FULL-SC-3`): that test has a budget stop and nothing live, and the halt test has
  pipelines live and no budget stop, so the conversion during a drain was under neither; it is now witnessed with
  a worker live at a halting settlement planted after `budget_exceeded`
  (`a_halting_settlement_drained_after_a_budget_stop_cancels_the_live_worker_and_ends_halted_at_width_three`).
- **Closure steps (3) and (4) at a live end are constructed** (R-AG). No coordinator schedule reaches a
  promoting generation or a prepared transaction at a live end, so each of those tests makes one ref
  write fail once (the fast integration's CAS, the stale-clean proposal's CAS, the candidates ref) and
  ends the run after it.
- **The append error releases the ledger before the pipelines end** (R-AI). Obligation (3) settles
  every running registration cancelled at the protocol, and each pipeline's own end report is then a
  counted duplicate; nothing can be granted in between, because the fold is poisoned and the interrupt
  refuses every `Admit`. At a halt or a shutdown, each running invocation is released by its own
  pipeline's end report after the Runner call returns — when that end established that its process is
  gone; one whose fate is unresolved keeps its registration and pair (round R1, below). The append
  error's early release covers unresolved invocations too: nothing can be granted after it, and each
  pipeline's later end, unresolved included, is a counted duplicate the coordinator still records.
  *No longer so since round R5* (§13, `FULL-CONC-1`; R-AI as corrected): the ledger then reported release and
  balance over an unresolved process. At the coordinator an append error now withdraws only the waiting requests,
  and each running invocation is released by its own end report, an unresolved one kept with its pair, as at a halt
  or a shutdown.
- **The early review's five findings were not repaired in phase 4** (phase 4 implemented its side of the
  three that overlap its requirements, `R1-CONC-1`, `-2` and `-4`, and named them). **Round R1 (§13)
  repairs all five** on phase 4's head: `R1-REG-1`, `R1-CONC-1`, `-2` and `-3` in the code, each with
  witnesses red at `898e7338` and mutations; `R1-CONC-4` verified as phase 4 left it (red at `83a387bd`,
  green at `898e7338`). What round R1 does not claim:
  - `R1-CONC-3`'s sequence cannot be produced by this build's live engine (at most one member of a
    lineage is ever live; §13 says why); its witnesses plant the fold-valid sibling state through a test
    seam, `Release::Append`, that production never passes.
  - An invocation held after an unresolved end stays held until the process ends: nothing in this
    process can release it, and `limit_slots` refuses a second coordinator on the same run while it is
    held. The width-1 substrate holds such an invocation too; since the unresolved error ends the
    command, the only observable change there is the ledger's balance.
  - A decline-abandoned verification's staging worktree, pin and snapshots are not reclaimed live: the
    terminal finalization and the next resume's reclaim remove them (§13).
  - The Windows and macOS behaviour of the changed code is compiled, not run, on this box (§8, §10).
- **Review round 2's five findings are repaired in round R2 (§13)** on round R1's head: `R2-CONC4-1`,
  `R2-DECLINE-QUEUED` and `R2-RECONCILE-GRANT` in the code, each with witnesses red at `e6ecc64d` and mutations,
  and `R2-REG-1` and `R2-REG-2` in the notes, each pinned by a test red against the start head's notes. What round
  R2 does not claim:
  - The decline-with-a-queued-candidate state and the two-closed-siblings state cannot be produced by this build's
    live engine, as round R1's could not (§13): the first witness plants its sibling through `Release::Append`,
    and the two grant witnesses assemble the coordinator themselves. Production passes no observer and assembles
    no coordinator.
  - Of the grant delivery sites round R2 guards, the production intake reaches one with a closed identity:
    `reply_granted` from `stop`, when one append closes two identities. The others — a closed pipeline's
    registration, snapshot request and queued snapshot, and a pair an end frees for it — are reached only
    between an append that closes an identity and the next admission pass, which the production intake never
    leaves: every message applied and every answer ingested is followed by a pass that reconciles first. They
    are guarded for the class, and each is witnessed on the assembled coordinator and has its own mutation.
  - A halt recorded after a verification's result arrived discards the result. Its review spend was charged to
    the live spend at acceptance (R-AH); `merge_verification_interrupted` carries no reviews, so the report and
    `Spend::replay`, which read the log, do not count them — as for an attempt interrupted after its reviews ran.
    The run is ending Halted, so no later selection reads the live figure.
  - The halt check walks the fold's in-flight work (`closure::in_flight`) once per admission pass while the run
    is ending and halted; its cost is not measured.
  - The Windows and macOS behaviour of the changed code is compiled, not run, on this box (§8, §10).
- A kill inside the closure is tested at the closure's first append (`attempt_interrupted`) in the torn
  and the complete shape, and an append error inside the closure at the same append's partial write
  and sync. Other closure appends are not killed or failed at width three; their width-1 namesakes in
  the frozen `recover/tests.rs` are unchanged.
- The width-three tests are driven by the scripted scheduler and ran 80 of 80 under contention (§8).
  Seeded permutations of these shapes are the interleaving suite's, phase 6 (the halt and the budget stop under seeds;
  the planted and constructed shapes stay scripted).

- **Phase 5** (container ownership and the process-local rows under concurrency) changes no production line; it claims
  what its tests show (§7) and no more:
  - Real Docker is G7's. Docker is present on this box and the gated real-Docker tests run here, but every phase-5 test
    uses the fake runtime by design, as its daemon (R-AM).
  - The Unix reaper's container reclaim is **not wired at the coordinator** (R-AR): nothing in the topology engine
    registers a reaper's container scope, and a container invocation has no live supervisor. On this build a dead
    coordinator's containers are therefore reclaimed by the next write command's census on every platform, Unix
    included. Named for the orchestrator; wiring a supervisor across container invocations is a new mechanism, not a
    scheduling change. Filed in phase 7 as `PR11-REAPER-CONTAINER-SCOPE-UNREGISTERED` (§6).
  - A fresh run's creation takes no `CensusComplete` (R-AO): the census-first order of a fresh write command rests on
    `startup.rs`'s stated precondition, and no schema-4 production caller exists (R-G). Noted, not changed; filed in
    phase 7 as `PR11-FRESH-CREATE-TAKES-NO-CENSUS-WITNESS` (§6).
  - A container's process is the scaffold double's invocation (R-AP): no process runs inside a container, and a
    container's environment and mounts are the production runner's, unchanged and unexercised beyond what PR6's tests
    hold. The pre-flight's agent probes are not containers here (the scaffold's adapters never probe), so ST-16 (f)'s probe
    clause is exercised with the shell probe.
  - A child reaches the daemon one request at a time (`LinkedRuntime`'s turn), so two operations of one child never
    interleave inside the daemon; operations of different processes do.
  - The child coordinators run the production intake with no observer; a parent learns their state from the journal and
    their event lines, and kills them. What a killed child had appended is read from its log afterwards.
  - R22 is observed on Unix only, through a killed coordinator's descendants (R-AQ); on Windows the ambient job's
    behaviour is compiled here and executed by no phase-5 test.
  - The contention runs are §8's; seeded permutations of the two-process shapes are not built (phase 6's interleaving suite
    is the permutation suite).

- **Review round 3's findings are repaired in round R3 (§13)** on phase 5's head: `R3-HALT-GRANT` in the code, with
  witnesses red at `8eae559a` and five mutations, and `R3-BODY` in the pull request's body. What round R3 does not claim:
  - The state `R3-HALT-GRANT` names cannot be produced by this build's live engine: a halting decline must close a live
    pipeline's identity whose withdrawn request frees another's pair, and a root whose repair exists awaits that repair
    and is never in flight beside it (§13, `R1-CONC-3`). The witnesses plant the sibling repair and assemble the
    coordinator, as round R2's grant witnesses did; production passes no observer, assembles no coordinator, and does
    not call the coordinator at all yet (R-G).
  - Of the grant sites the class witness reaches, the production intake reaches none with a halt recorded and not acted
    on: every message applied and every answer ingested is followed by an admission pass, which now acts on the halt
    before anything else. They are guarded for the class, and each is witnessed on the assembled coordinator.
  - An append error has no witness of its own in this round: a poisoned fold holds no identity open, so the delivery
    sites refuse through the identity check, at the start head as now (§13, the class search); phase 4's
    `append_error_under_concurrency_cancels_pipelines_and_folds_nothing_from_memory` holds the protocol.
  - A pipeline whose identity the halting append itself closed is now cancelled by the halt with the others and recorded
    in `cancelled_work`, which settles nothing (the closure settles only what the fold shows in flight); the
    non-durable warning reconciliation used to give for such a pipeline under a halt is no longer given.
  - The Windows and macOS behaviour of the changed code is compiled, not run, on this box (§8, §10).

- **Phase 6** (the interleaving suite at width three, the runtime pool tests, the G6 raw outputs) changes no production
  line; it claims what its tests show (§7) and no more:
  - Every permuted run is the coordinator under the deterministic scheduler (R-AD): a seed is one schedule, reproduced
    exactly, and the suite covers the seeds and orders it names (R-AS), not every interleaving. Production's
    arrival-order intake is not permuted; it runs in the phase-3 to phase-5 tests that pass no observer and in the
    two-process children.
  - ST-13's reservation ledger is read at every quiescent point and at the ends, and the fold-derived holdings at every
    append; the ledger inside the coordinator step between a reservation and its first append is not observed, since
    the append hook does not carry it and no production reader was added (R-AT).
  - The G6 exporter writes the measurements of the tests that assert them; no separate check reads them back, and phase
    6 writes no gate report (R-AU). It exports the rows this suite holds; the gate cites the others from the tests that
    hold them.
  - The runtime pool tests observe processes running at once, at quiescent points (R-AV). The two agents are the
    fixture's worker and primary reviewer (`claude-code`) and its second reviewer (`copilot`); production's per-agent
    pools come from configuration no schema-4 run reads yet (R-K). The agent probes' pairs are shown taken and released
    by the pre-flight's ledger balancing with one completion per probe, one at a time, before admission; its slot table
    is not readable from the coordinator's tests, and the pair's shape is phase 2's unit tests'. The broker-only
    shared-pool tests stay broker-only; the deadlock-freedom test drives shared pools as an adversarial configuration,
    not a reachable one.
  - ST-18 at width three kills at every cell a live width-three end leaves to finalization — the report, the candidates
    refs, the execution root; the residue classes such an end does not leave (task worktrees, snapshots, staging, pins)
    stay PR10's planted matrix in the frozen `recover/tests.rs`, unchanged (R-AW).
  - ST-16's two-process shapes and ST-09's process-local rows (R17, R22, R28) are not permuted: phase 5's scripted
    two-process tests stand; the seeded container test is one process over the fake runtime.
  - The Windows and macOS behaviour of the suite is compiled, not run, on this box (§8, §10).

- **Phase 7** (the close-out; review round 4's repair; the Windows schedules, R-AY; the children's temporary directories,
  R-AZ) changes no production line; it claims what its tests and measurements show and no more:
  - The Windows time budget is projected here, not measured (R-AY): the guest's harness at 449 to 452 s, from CI's own
    timelines scaled by each seeded test's share of its schedules. A test's fixed costs do not scale, so the projection
    leans optimistic; CI's `test (winguest)` leg at the pushed head measures it, and the queue's hosted leg runs only for
    an entry.
  - On Windows the seeded coordinator tests run their Windows subsets; the counts R-AS gives, and the G6 exports, are
    Linux's and macOS's.
  - `R4-REG-1`'s witnesses are Unix-only (§13). Its class is the fixture constructors that own a child; test bodies and
    synchronous helpers that hold a raw `Child` across fallible work, and owners of a child that are not guards (the kill
    samplers' `KillableGitChild` and `SampledChild`), are listed and not changed (§13).
  - The residue is measured on Linux with `TMPDIR`; the children are handed `TMP` and `TEMP` the same way for Windows,
    and the suite's residue on the guest is not measured.
  - The four findings phase 7 files are latent defects with guards (§6), not repairs.
  - The merge (§12) is the orchestrator's and not yet made.

- **Round R5** (the full review's findings, §13) changes one production behaviour — the coordinator's invocation
  ledger at an append error (`FULL-CONC-1`) — and claims what its witnesses, mutations and class searches show:
  - `FULL-SC-2`'s witnesses assemble the coordinator, as round R2's and R3's grant witnesses did, and take each
    completion through the production intake (`receive_one` with no observer); a real pipeline's completion never
    carries another identity, and an identity it carries closes only in a step after which the pipeline is stopped
    before its completion is applied, so on the production intake the binding and the fold check are defence in
    depth, now proved. The poisoned-fold check has no test that fails without it: at the coordinator it is redundant
    with the cancelled check and the fold check (§13), and the property holds with any one of the three removed.
  - `FULL-SC-3`'s halting settlement is planted, as R-AH says any halting drain settlement must be in this build.
  - `FULL-CONC-1` changes the coordinator's ledger only. The synchronous substrate's, the pre-flight's and recovery's
    ledgers still settle every running registration at the protocol, which is sound because their owners run each
    invocation themselves; `cancel_in_flight` is unchanged.
  - `FULL-REG-1`'s class is PR11's own helpers. A pre-existing real-Docker test with the same defect is filed
    (`PR11-R5-REAL-DOCKER-GATE-SCRIPT-SPLICES-PATHS`, §6), not repaired; a real-Docker test whose Unix socket path
    outgrows `sun_path` under a long temporary directory is noted (§13), not filed.
  - `FULL-SC-4` corrects sentences: the exposure is still stated in the notes only, and the finding stays deferred.
  - On Windows the seeded ST-05 test runs seed 1 rather than seed 0 (R-AY's rule for a claim made of some seed).
  - The Windows and macOS behaviour of the changed code is type-checked for both targets on this box, not run (§8,
    §10); CI's legs are the measurement.

## 13. Repairs

### Phase 3 CI repair (round C1)

Written by `pr11_repair_c1` (`claude-opus-5-5`, `max`), a fresh session `orch_pr11` spawned after CI
failed at phase 3's pushed head. Every file named here is under `~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_repair_c1/`
unless another directory is named.

**The failure, verbatim.** CI run 36670986992 at `aea75a79`: `lint`, `lint (windows)`, `lint
(macos)` and the three `msrv (Rust 1.85, …)` legs passed. The three test legs failed, and
`upstroke-ci` failed because they did. The job logs are the orchestrator's, under
`~/orch-pr11/logs/ci-aea75a79/`, and their sha256 matches `SHA256SUMS` there:

```

thread 'engine::topology::coordinator::tests::a_shutdown_cancels_every_live_pipeline_and_ends_the_command_resumably' (40403) panicked at src/engine/topology/coordinator.rs:2522:9:

  left: [1]
 right: [0, 1, 2]
test result: FAILED. 2921 passed; 1 failed; 110 ignored; 0 measured; 0 filtered out; finished in 224.73s

thread 'engine::topology::coordinator::tests::a_shutdown_cancels_every_live_pipeline_and_ends_the_command_resumably' (154494) panicked at src/engine/topology/coordinator.rs:2522:9:
assertion `left == right` failed: every live worker was cancelled
  left: []
 right: [0, 1, 2]
test result: FAILED. 2856 passed; 1 failed; 88 ignored; 0 measured; 0 filtered out; finished in 637.33s

test (winguest)  job 109745746648  job-109745746648.log:3078-3087
thread 'engine::topology::coordinator::tests::a_shutdown_cancels_every_live_pipeline_and_ends_the_command_resumably' (5472) panicked at src\engine\topology\coordinator.rs:2522:9:
assertion `left == right` failed: every live worker was cancelled
  left: [0, 1]
 right: [0, 1, 2]
```
