
Each log has exactly one `... FAILED` line, and the library's result line says one failure. The only
other panic, in the ubuntu and macOS logs, is `sigchld_target_setup_failure_helper`'s: the helper
subprocess's expected panic, whose parent test passes. Cargo stops at the first failing test binary
(`ci.yml` runs `cargo test --all-targets --all-features` without `--no-fail-fast`), and no `Running`
line follows the library's on any leg. So the binary target's ten tests did not run on any leg, and
"nothing else failed" speaks for the library's tests alone. Phase 3's local gates passed the test
(`~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_impl_c/gates/at-aea75a79/`).

**The reproduction, at `aea75a79`, before anything changed.**

- **No instrument.** The library's test binary was built from this worktree at `aea75a79`
  (`repro/base/build.log`, whose Compiling line names the worktree; sha256 `42cfa6f5…` in
  `repro/base/binary.sha256`). Alone, the test passes (`repro/base/single-1.log`). It was then run
  200 times, as 8 concurrent loops of 25, with every process pinned by `taskset` to two CPUs, so the
  loops contend for those two alone (`tools/contend.sh`, `repro/contention-aea75a79-a/`). It failed
  188 times at the same assertion, and `left` took every proper subset of `{0, 1, 2}`: `[]` 109,
  `[0]` 26, `[2]` 12, `[0, 2]` 11, `[1, 2]` 11, `[1]` 10, `[0, 1]` 9 (`summary.txt`). CI's three
  shapes are among them. The whole coordinator module the same way, 40 runs as 4 loops of 10
  (`tools/contend-module.sh`, `audit/contention-aea75a79-module/`): the shutdown test failed 36
  times, and every other test of the module passed all 40.
- **A controlled instrument, the exact CI shapes.** A scratch `git archive` copy of `aea75a79`,
  never committed (`repro/instrument-scaffold.diff`, `repro/instrument-shutdown-test.diff`). For the
  worker keys listed, the double's first poll of the call waits until that call is cancelled, and
  the test waits for the unlisted workers to be held before it injects the shutdown. Listing
  `{0, 1, 2}`, `{0, 2}` and `{2}` gives `left: []`, `[1]` and `[0, 1]`, macOS's, ubuntu's and
  winguest's shapes, 10 times out of 10 each. With nothing listed the unchanged test passes 10 of
  10 (`repro/instr/barrier-*.log`). Every run printed all three workers granted at the first
  quiescent point. The subset in `left` is exactly the set of workers inside the runner when the
  shutdown's cancellation landed. A 200 ms sleep at the first poll instead (`pr11_impl_c`'s
  instrument) fails `left: []` 10 times out of 10 (`repro/instr/delay200-*.log`).

**The root cause.** The orchestrator's reading is confirmed.

- **Where `Invoking` came from.** The coordinator marked a pipeline `Busy::Invoking(invocation)`
  when it *sent* the grant: `started`, from `admit_invocation` and `reply_granted`. The
  deterministic intake counts an `Invoking` pipeline as not running on its own. So the observer
  was asked, and the shutdown injected, while granted pipelines were still between their grant and
  the first poll of their Runner call.
- **What the double did with the late ones.** The scaffold runner's first poll checks the
  cancellation before it starts anything. A call cancelled there returned
  `cancelled(NeverStarted)` and recorded no ending. A call past that check was held, and when
  cancelled it recorded `Ending::Cancelled`.
- **Why a slower machine failed.** The first quiescent point follows the third grant by one
  admission pass. On this box, uncontended, the three threads had arrived by then: phase 3's gates
  passed, and so did `repro/base/single-1.log`. On CI's runners, or two contended CPUs, they had
  not.
- **The class is wider than this test.** Under an observer the coordinator could act on anything
  while a granted pipeline was on its way to the Runner: apply the next message, append, cancel, or
  ask the observer. The race shows wherever a cancellation or an observation lands in that window.
  The order in which the coordinator applied messages was unaffected. A pipeline on its way to the
  Runner sends nothing until its call returns, and what a cancelled pipeline sends is discarded
  either way. R-AD's "a seed reproduces the same run" was false as built (§3, R-AD, corrected).

**Production, in the racing runs.** The scratch test printed the ledger before its assertions.
Whichever workers raced, every instrumented run showed three registered, three cancelled, none
completed, no duplicate and `discarded() == 3`, and the ledger balanced (`repro/instr/ledger-*.log`,
`delay200-*.log`). A granted invocation cancelled before its process started was released exactly
once, as a cancellation, by its own pipeline's end report. Only the double's record of it was
missing (R-AE).

**`pr11_impl_c`'s leads** (`~/orch-pr11/handovers/pr11_impl_c.md`, `…/pr11_impl_c/ci/leads/`).

- **Confirmed.**
  - The mechanism.
  - Its 200 ms first-poll instrument reproduces macOS's `[]`.
  - Product behaviour correct. It stated this without a measurement, and the ledger above measures
    it.
  - The halt and pipeline-error tests rest on the same precondition. The audit below measures both
    failing under the instrument.
- **Not taken.** `candidate-scheduler-waits-for-held.patch` has the scheduler wait, at each
  quiescent point, for every granted invocation to be held. That closes the observer's decision
  point only. Between a grant and the next quiescent point the coordinator still applies messages,
  appends and cancels, and a halt or a pipeline error in the same pass cancels a grant made moments
  earlier before the observer is asked. The wait belongs at the grant.
- **Its alternatives.**
  - *Counting cancellations through the ledger only:* rejected (R-AE's alternative).
  - *Recording the double's pre-start cancellation:* adopted, as a distinct ending, beside the
    repair rather than instead of it.
  - *Asserting either kind of cancellation:* rejected. Under the repaired intake the shutdown
    test's workers are all running, and accepting a pre-start cancellation there would hide a
    regression of the intake.

**The repair** (`87b29387`).

- **`coordinator.rs`.** `Quiescence::granted` is the new observer method. Every grant, answered at
  once or freed by a later release, passes through `started`. `started` hands it to the observer
  before doing anything else, and marks the pipeline `Invoking` when the observer returns, which it
  does once the invocation is inside the Runner. Production passes no observer, and the call sits
  inside `if let Some(observer)`, so no production behaviour changes. The production region moves
  by the declaration, those three lines and one sentence of the §10 protocol comment (§8).
- **`scaffold.rs`** (the test double).
  - `Ending::CancelledBeforeStart` (R-AE).
  - Late entry: under `enter_late` a call waits at the runner's door until `admit` lets it in or
    its call is cancelled, and `inside` says whether a call is held or barred at the door.
  - `bar`: a door that opens only on cancellation.
- **The test `Scheduler`.** It turns late entry on, admits each grant in `granted` (bounded by
  `BOUND`), and asserts at every quiescent point that every invocation it is handed is inside the
  runner. The protocol R-AD claims is therefore checked at every quiescent point of every
  scheduler-driven test, on any machine.
- **The shutdown test.** It keeps its scenario: the first quiescent point, three granted workers,
  the CI failure's own interleaving. Its assertion now states the packet's `cancellation` sentence
  in the runner and the ledger. Every granted worker ended `Cancelled` (started, then terminated).
  Each was released exactly once, as a cancellation: registered 3, cancelled 3, completed 0,
  duplicates 0. A release before the termination would make the pipeline's own end report a
  counted duplicate.
- **New tests.**
  - `a_shutdown_releases_each_invocation_once_whether_pending_unstarted_running_or_finished`
    builds the positions production can reach (R-AE, next paragraph).
  - `one_seed_reproduces_one_run_down_to_what_each_process_saw_when_it_started` pins R-AD. Three
    runs of seed 11, with two reviewers per attempt and per verification, release the same
    invocations and start the same processes, in the same order, on the same workspaces, with the
    same log durable at each start. They write the same canonical log.
  - `a_late_call_waits_at_the_door_until_admitted_and_a_barred_one_until_cancelled` tests the
    double's door.
  - `the_scaffold_runner_ends_a_cancelled_or_dropped_invocation_exactly_once` is extended with a
    call cancelled before it starts.

**Production semantics.** The question: can a pipeline cancelled between its grant and its Runner
call be released twice, never released, or reported as something other than cancelled?

- **Released once.** The grant makes the registration `Running` and the invocation the pipeline's
  `running` one.
  - *The call.* It returns `cancelled(NeverStarted)`. The host runner pins this with
    `a_call_cancelled_before_it_starts_spawns_nothing`, the container runner with
    `a_container_call_cancelled_before_it_starts_writes_no_intent`.
  - *The report.* `execute_typed` or `Registering::settle` reports `Ended{completed: false}`.
  - *The release.* `end_invocation` cancels the registration once and clears `running`, so `retire`
    finds nothing to withdraw.
  - *A pipeline that ends without reporting* (a panic `catch_unwind` caught) still holds `running`,
    and `retire` withdraws it once.
- **Never twice.** A second settlement is a counted duplicate that releases no slot
  (`InvocationLedger::settle`). `cancel_all` withdraws pending registrations and never a granted
  one.
- **Reported as a cancellation.** The ledger records it `Cancelled`. The error is `is_cancelled()`,
  which the verification and review mappings test first (R-AB). The completion is discarded as a
  cancelled pipeline's.
- **The one path that releases a granted registration before its process ends** is an append error
  (`emit.rs`, `cancel_all_running`), recorded in §11 as phase 4's. There the pipeline's later end
  report is a counted duplicate and releases nothing twice.
- **The interleavings constructed.**
  - *The racing runs above:* the ledger balanced in each.
  - *At `87b29387`, `a_shutdown_releases_each_invocation_once_whether_pending_unstarted_running_or_finished`
    builds every position at one shutdown.* At width four with three agent slots, the shutdown
    finds a pending request, a granted but unstarted worker (barred), a running worker, and one
    whose result was delivered but not yet reported. Each is released exactly once. The pending
    request is withdrawn and never reaches the runner. The unstarted worker is cancelled before it
    starts, the running one is terminated, and the finished one completes. The ledger ends
    registered 4, completed 1, cancelled 3, duplicates 0, balanced, and `ran()` names the running
    and finished workers only.
  - *`a_panicking_pipeline_ends_the_command_with_a_defined_error`* runs without an observer and
    asserts the balance, which holds in whatever interleaving its threads take.

**The audit.** Every test that uses `Quiescent`, `view.invoking`, `Release::Invocation`, a
`Scheduler` (`first`, `scripted`, `seeded`) or the injector: the module's thirteen scheduler-driven
tests. The module's other two drive no observer, and the double's own tests are listed for
completeness. "Exposed" means its outcome could depend on how far a granted pipeline had got when
the coordinator acted.

- **The unfixed tree under the entry-delay instrument** (`audit/base/`):
  - every call's first poll delayed 200 ms and 50 ms, two module runs each;
  - keys 1 and 2 delayed 300 ms, one module run;
  - the pipeline-error test with keys 1 and 2 delayed 2 s, three runs.
- **The unfixed tree under contention:** 40 module runs (`audit/contention-aea75a79-module/`).
- **At `87b29387`:** the same instrument (`proof/instr/`) and 80 contended module runs
  (`proof/contention-87b29387-module/`).

| test | row | what it uses | exposed at `aea75a79`? | unfixed evidence | at `87b29387` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `a_shutdown_cancels_every_live_pipeline_and_ends_the_command_resumably` | R-AB | scripted, injector (shutdown), runner endings | **yes**: the CI failure | fails every instrumented module run (4 of 4, and at keys 1, 2); contended 36 of 40 | passes every run |
| `a_halt_inside_verification_cancels_it_and_leaves_the_transaction_for_closure` | R-E/R-V, R-AC | scripted, runner endings (a `Sequence` invocation cancelled) | **yes**: the halt's cancellation can land before the verification's granted invocation starts | fails 4 of 4 at 200 and 50 ms ("the verification's invocation was cancelled, not completed"); contended 40 of 40 pass | passes every run |
| `a_pipeline_error_cancels_the_others_and_ends_the_command_resumably` | R-AB | scripted, runner endings (workers 1 and 2 cancelled) | **yes**: workers 1 and 2 are granted at the start and the error arrives later, a wide window but not a closed one | fails with keys 1 and 2 at 300 ms, and 3 of 3 at 2 s (`left: []`); uniform delays and contention pass | passes every run, 2 s keys 1 and 2 included (3 of 3) |
| `the_coordinator_settles_promotes_and_dispatches_while_a_verification_is_open` | R-E/R-V | scripted, `durable_at_spawn` (`task_merged` counts) | **no, for what it asserts**: a process's log was read whenever its thread reached the double, but the counts it asserts are fenced by the snapshot gate. Delta's gate starts before the second `task_merged` can be appended (`verify` returns only when no attempt snapshot is live), and its review pass only after it | passes all 5 instrumented runs and 40 of 40 contended | passes every run |
| `stale_duplicate_and_mismatched_completions_are_discarded_with_a_warning_and_counted` | ST-01, 02, 06 | scripted, injector | no: injected messages are refused or discarded on coordinator state alone, and the script reads the coordinator's own `live` and `invoking` lists | passes | passes |
| `two_independent_tasks_overlap_merge_through_one_queue_and_the_dependent_starts_on_both` | acceptance item 2 | scripted (records `view.invoking`), `head_at_spawn` | no: the list is the coordinator's, and `head_at_spawn` reads the worktree's own HEAD | passes | passes |
| `disjoint_hints_dispatch_together_overlapping_and_absent_hints_serialize` | acceptance item 2 | `first`, `widest` | no: `widest` counts the coordinator's grants | passes | passes |
| `out_of_order_completions_bind_to_their_own_identities_under_seeded_permutations` | ST-03, ST-08 | seeded, 16 seeds | no: it asserts the durable log only, written in the coordinator's own message order | passes | passes |
| `independent_tasks_dispatch_together_and_keep_per_key_projections_under_every_seed` | ST-08 | seeded, 8 seeds | no, as above | passes | passes |
| `concurrent_attempts_at_one_generation_and_attempt_use_distinct_snapshot_slots` | ST-04 | seeded, `widest`, `ran()` workspaces | no: names and counts, not order or timing | passes | passes |
| `live_state_equals_replay_after_every_append_at_width_three` | ST-10 | seeded | no | passes | passes |
| `a_retained_retry_is_admitted_beside_other_pipelines_and_regates_on_fresh_snapshots` | ST-15 | scripted, `ran()` workspaces of one key | no: one key's gates start in that key's own order | passes | passes |
| `adversarial_orders_with_one_slot_per_agent_and_pool_always_reach_run_finished` | deadlock-freedom | seeded, 12 seeds; `widest_slotted`, `points` | no: it counts the coordinator's grants; the grants a release frees pass through `started` like any other | passes | passes |
| `a_chain_plan_projects_identically_at_widths_three_and_one` | ST-08 | no observer | not applicable: production intake, asserts the log | passes | passes |
| `a_panicking_pipeline_ends_the_command_with_a_defined_error` | R-AB | no observer | not applicable: asserts the balance, which holds in every interleaving | passes | passes |
| `run::tests::scaffold_runner` (five tests) | the double | hold, complete, cancel | no: each drives the double directly with its own handshake (`await_waiting`) | not run in the audit | six pass, the new one included (§8, §9) |

The `Scheduler` itself was not exposed where it chose. Its choices read the coordinator's lists,
and `release` already waited for the invocation it chose to be held. It was exposed wherever it did
not wait: an injection, and every cancellation the coordinator made on its own.

**The proof** (at `87b29387`; `proof/`).

- **Red at `aea75a79`, green after.** The same entry-delay instrument was applied to a scratch copy
  of `87b29387` (`proof/c1-instr.diff`; the binaries' sha256 are in `proof/binaries.sha256`). With
  it the shutdown test passes 10 of 10 at 200 ms, and the pipeline-error test 3 of 3 with keys 1
  and 2 delayed 2 s. The whole module passes 17 of 17 in each of five runs: 200 ms twice, 50 ms
  twice, keys 1 and 2 at 300 ms once (`proof/instr/`). The barrier instrument does not apply to the
  repaired tree. Its wait-until-cancelled is the interleaving the repair forbids, since the
  coordinator now waits for the call to reach the runner, and `bar` is that same interleaving
  constructed deliberately.
- **Under load.** On the committed binary, the shutdown test passed 200 of 200, as 8 loops of 25 on
  two CPUs. The whole module passed 80 of 80 runs, as 4 loops of 20, each of its 17 tests passing
  every time (`proof/contention-87b29387-*/summary.txt`). The contention matched the unfixed runs':
  the shutdown test's median run took 0.16 s unfixed and 0.17 s repaired, against 0.05 s alone
  (`proof/contention-durations.txt`, `repro/base/single-1.log`).
- **Mutation witnesses** from `87b29387` (`proof/c1-nosettle.diff`, `proof/c1-norecord.diff`,
  `proof/mutants/`).
  - *Without the call in `started`:* the module's 15 scheduler-driven tests fail in each of three
    runs, 45 failures, every one at the scheduler's "is granted and not inside the runner at a
    quiescent point". Only the two observer-free tests pass.
  - *Without the double's record of a pre-start cancellation:*
    `a_shutdown_releases_each_invocation_once_whether_pending_unstarted_running_or_finished` and the
    double's two cancellation tests fail. The shutdown test passes, correctly: under the repair
    nothing it cancels is unstarted.
- **The rest.** §8 holds the module diff proof, the census, the notes checks, the development run
  and the two targets' type-check; §9 holds the gates.

### Early review round R1

Written by `pr11_repair_r1` (`claude-opus-5-5`, `max`), a fresh session `orch_pr11` spawned on phase 4's
pushed head `898e7338` to repair the early review's five findings. Every file named here is under
`~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_repair_r1/` unless another directory is named.

**The review.** Two `gpt-6-astra` lenses at `max`, concurrency and regression, ran at
`83a387bd4f68025a6309e4fc8c5ccf2e42652c4e` (phases 0–3 and round C1) through the codex transport with a
`REVIEW_ROOT` detached worktree at that head (`-s read-only`); both returned **CHANGES_REQUIRED**. The texts,
their logs and the orchestrator's triage are under `~/orch-pr11/reviews/`
(`review-327-early-concurrency-83a387bd.review.md`, `review-327-early-regression-83a387bd.review.md`, the two
`.log` files, `early-review-R1-triage.md`; sha256 in `SHA256SUMS-early`, all four verified). Five findings:
`R1-REG-1` (P2, executed by the reviewer), `R1-CONC-1`, `R1-CONC-2` and `R1-CONC-4` (P1, reasoned) and
`R1-CONC-3` (P2, reasoned). Phase 4 was built on top afterwards and told of them for awareness; the round starts
from its head, and checks what phase 4 added for the same classes.

**The method** (round C1's: reproduce first, contend, mutate). Every witness is a test that fails at the start
head and passes after the repair. The red runs use a tree built by `tools/witness-scratch.py`: a `git archive`
of `898e7338` carrying only the witnesses — the new tests, copied by name from the repaired tree, the test
double's two additions (`RecordingRunner::panic_when_released`, `WidePlans::panic_verifying`) and the observer
seam `Release::Append`, which is a pure addition — so a run against it is a run against the start head's
production code (`repro/final-dc3136fb/`: the build log naming the tree, the binary's sha256, one log per
test, `SUMMARY.txt`, `FAILURES.txt`). The green runs are the repaired head's binary (`proof/green-dc3136fb/`),
and each repair has a mutation that revives its defect in a copy of the repaired head (`proof/mutants/`,
`tools/run-mutants-r1.sh`). The repaired code is `dc3136fb`.

#### `R1-REG-1` — a removal's hooks deadlock on the registry lock

**The finding** (regression lens, verbatim in part): "`remove_worktree_proving` holds the new registry mutex
across `funnel`, including its `Before` and `After` callbacks. A callback calling `worktree_records()` attempts to
acquire that same non-reentrant mutex. … At head `83a387bd`, execution stopped inside the `Before` callback until
the three-second watchdog terminated it. … It also contradicts R-X's assertion that the lock is never nested and
its holder waits only for Git or filesystem operations."

**The witness**, `workspace_manager::tests::an_observer_lists_the_worktrees_from_both_hooks_of_a_removal`: a task
worktree removed through `remove_worktree` with an observer whose hook lists the worktrees at `Before` and
`After`; the removal runs on its own thread and is waited for 60 s, so the regression fails rather than hangs the
suite. **Red at `898e7338`**: "the removal did not return within 60s" (`repro/final-dc3136fb/an_observer_lists_the_worktrees_from_both_hooks_of_a_removal.log`).
**Green at `dc3136fb`**: the `Before` hook lists two worktrees and the `After` hook one, as the reviewer saw at the
base.

**The root cause.** Phase 3's lock (R-X) wrapped the whole removal, and `funnel` calls the observer inside it; the
holder therefore waited for whatever the observer did, and a registry reader in the observer took the same lock
on the same thread. Phase 4 adds no holder; its closure removes worktrees through this funnel, so the deadlock was
reachable from its reclaim too.

**The repair: the lock is released around the hooks, not bypassed.** The scan runs under the lock before any
hook and binds the registration the removal will act through; the removal of the checkout, the administrative
directory and the prune take the lock again inside the funnel, after the `Before` hook, and release it before the
`After` hook (`remove_worktree_proving`, the new private `remove_bound`). A lock-free inventory path for observers
would serve an observer that reads on the holder's own thread and nothing else: a hook that waits on another
thread's registry access would still deadlock, and the rule that a holder calls out to nothing would stay broken.
The add/prune race stays closed: every add, list, scan and prune runs under the lock, so none sees another half
done; what the split admits is a *complete* add of another slot between the scan and the removal, which the prune
under the lock leaves alone (its checkout exists), and a change to the bound registration itself is what
`registration_still_names` already handles. **A first version re-scanned inside the funnel** and bound after the
`Before` hook; three existing tests that exchange or rebind the registration at that hook and expect the
acted-through check to refuse it failed (`dev/fix5-coord-wm.log`:
`a_registration_admin_directory_exchanged_at_the_before_hook_refuses_the_worktree_removal`,
`a_registration_rebound_after_validation_keeps_its_admin_state`,
`every_path_a_primitive_acts_through_refuses_a_link_planted_at_the_before_hook`), so the binding stays the
pre-hook scan's.

**"Never nested", re-checked against every holder** (R-X corrected in §3): the add's Git child (inside the funnel,
after `Before`, released before `After`); the removal's scan; the removal's mutation (filesystem calls,
`sync_checkout_removed` into a `DurabilityLedger` handle — a plain shared list — and `git worktree prune`); the list
in `worktree_records`; the torn plan's scan (its caller removes only after releasing it). None calls a hook or a
registry reader; the witness and the rest of the workspace-manager module pass (§9).

**The class — every callback made under a lock.** Every lock PR11 adds to production code
(`measure/class-search-locks-production.txt`): the synchronous registrar's `Mutex` guards register and settle
only, never the Runner call between them; `Cancellation` drains its wakers under the lock and wakes them after it;
the `REGISTRY_LOCKS` table is held for the lookup alone; the host and container runners' `hooks` locks are held
across a *runner-level* observer's callbacks for its invocation, by design since phase 1 (and taken always before
PR11) — only tests install one, and it is how such an observer serializes invocations; they are listed, not
changed. **The mutation** `r1-reg-1-lock-across-hooks` (the lock held from the scan across the funnel, phase 3's
shape): the witness fails at its watchdog and the module's other 229 tests pass.

#### `R1-CONC-1` — an unresolved process releases its slots and lets another start

**The finding** (concurrency lens): "A's container supervision fails, and neither stop nor removal establishes
termination, so the Runner returns `ProcessFate::Unresolved`. A sends `Ended { completed: false }`, then is
descheduled before sending its error completion. The coordinator cancels A's registration, releases its pair and
immediately grants B. B starts while A's container can still be running. … `Ended` discards the process fate …
Balanced ledger counts therefore do not establish the claimed release-after-termination property."

**The witnesses.** `an_unresolved_end_keeps_its_pair_and_no_waiting_invocation_starts_on_it`: width two, one slot
for the agent (`SlotLimitsOf::Exactly(1, 1)`), alpha's worker holding the pair and beta's waiting; the double
answers alpha's worker with an unresolved Runner error. **Red at `898e7338`**: beta's worker was granted and
started (`Ending::Cancelled` after it had entered the runner). And `an_unreported_end_keeps_its_pair_and_no_waiting_invocation_starts_on_it`,
the same position reached through `retire`: the double panics on alpha's thread as its worker ends
(`panic_when_released`), so alpha never reports the end. **Red at `898e7338`** the same way. **Phase 4's claim,
verified:** its guard (a halt does not close over an unresolved cancelled process, R-AF) holds, and
`a_halt_whose_cancelled_process_is_unresolved_ends_the_command_without_closing` passes at both heads; but `Ended`'s
release and `retire`'s were unchanged, as its §11 said, and both witnesses show them.

**The root cause.** `Ended { completed: bool }` carried no fate; `end_invocation` cancelled the registration,
which released the pair and granted the head of the queue; `retire` withdrew an invocation its pipeline never
reported ended, with the same grant.

**The repair.** `Ended` carries `InvocationEnd` — completed, or failed with the Runner's `ProcessFate` and its
account — and so does `Registrar::ended`, from `Judge::execute_typed` and `Registering::settle`.
`InvocationLedger::end` completes, cancels when the fate says the process is gone, and **keeps** an unresolved
invocation: its registration stays running and its pair held, granting nothing. The coordinator's
`hold_unresolved` records it and is itself an interrupt: admission stops, every live pipeline is cancelled and
every pending request withdrawn and refused — so no request waits for ever on the pair that is never released, the
wait the hold alone would create — and the command ends under R-AB's error path with the Runner's account in its
error. `retire` holds an unreported running invocation the same way. **What happens instead** of the release:
the invocation stays held for the rest of the process; the next process's census reclaims the container and its
recovery settles the identity.

**The ledgers still balance, in the sense R3 and R4 give it.** `settled_but` (in the coordinator's tests) asserts
the end state: every registration settled exactly once but the held ones, which keep their pairs; nothing
pending; no duplicate. R3 and R4 at `NoRunFinished`: "released (process death; empty at restart)" — the next
process's ledgers start empty (`PermitBroker::for_run`;
`a_shutdown_with_pipelines_in_flight_is_settled_by_the_next_resume_whose_ledgers_start_empty`).
`TopologyRun::invocations_balance` is false while one is held, deliberately, and `limit_slots` then refuses a
second coordinator on the same run. The synchronous registrar holds the same way; nothing waits on its ledger
(R-O) and the unresolved error ends the command, so the balance is all that changes at width 1. Two phase-3 tests
now say so with `settled_but`: `a_pipeline_error_cancels_the_others_and_ends_the_command_resumably` (its gate
returns an unresolved error) and `a_panicking_pipeline_ends_the_command_with_a_defined_error` (the panic unwinds
through a gate).

**The class — every place an end is settled without its fate, every release of a granted invocation**
(`measure/class-search-settlements.txt`): `end_invocation` and `retire` (repaired); `execute_typed`,
`Registering::settle` and the synchronous registrar (carry the fate); `abandoned` (a grant withdrawn from a
pipeline that stopped waiting: no process started), `stop`, `cancel_all`, `withdraw_pending` and
`register_at_once`'s withdrawal (pending requests only); `InvocationLedger::cancel_all_running`, the append-error
protocol's obligation (3), which releases running registrations before their processes end (R-AI) — listed, not
changed: the pending requests go first, the fold is poisoned and every later `Admit` refused, so nothing can be
granted after it, and each pipeline's later end, unresolved included, is a counted duplicate the coordinator
still records; `AttemptContext::cancel_in_flight`, whose one caller is a test, "between synchronous invocations,
after their children exit" — listed. **The mutations:** `r1-conc-1-ended-releases` (the ledger cancels an
unresolved end) turns the first witness and the pipeline-error test red; `r1-conc-1-retire-releases` (`retire`
withdraws and grants) the second witness and the panic test.

#### `R1-CONC-2` — a fatal verification completion waits for the snapshots

**The finding**: "Verification V panics; its panic is caught and delivered as `JudgeError::Other`. The coordinator
accepts V's completion but merely stores the error in `arrived`. The verification loop continues admission and
waits for `gate.live() == 0`. … A receives no cancellation and continues until completion or timeout; other work
can also be dispatched meanwhile. … Fatal verification results must trigger cancellation when received."

**The witness**, `a_fatal_verification_completion_interrupts_on_receipt_without_waiting_for_the_snapshots`: gamma
is granted a review snapshot inside the verification, and the verification's body panics
(`WidePlans::panic_verifying`); the scheduler records every quiescent point at which the transaction is open and
no verification pipeline is live. **Red at `898e7338`**: the coordinator waited there with gamma's review held,
released it, and gamma settled first. **Green**: never asked in that state; gamma's review cancelled; nothing
appended after `merge_verification_started`; the command ends with the panic. **Phase 4's claim, verified:** every
interrupt (halt, shutdown, append error) cancels as it is recorded, inside `verify` too (R-AH); the fatal
completion's wait was unchanged, as its record said.

**The root cause.** `verified_arrived` stored the raw outcome; `verified()` — the mapping that tells a fatal
error from an unavailable verification — ran only after the snapshots drained.

**The repair.** `verified_arrived` maps at receipt with the width-1 mapping; an error is the interrupt there and
then (every pipeline cancelled, admission stopped) and `verify` returns without the drain; only an accepted
result waits for it. `judged` classifies before it retires, so nothing the failing pipeline held is granted on
before the interrupt. **The class — every receipt that could defer a fatal classification:** `judged` (at receipt
already; its order fixed), `verified_arrived` (repaired), `Ended` with an unresolved fate (`R1-CONC-1`'s
interrupt), a cancelled pipeline's completion (discarded; its fate came with its ends). **The mutation**
`r1-conc-2-late-classification` (the error kept until the drain, then failed) turns the witness red.

#### `R1-CONC-3` — a non-halting decline leaves `verify` waiting for ever

**The finding**: "While verification V remains running, the coordinator ingests a decline with
`decline_halts_run = false`. The fold closes that lineage and removes V's transaction. The coordinator neither
cancels V nor unwinds its verification wait. When V eventually reports, `check` correctly detects the closed
identity, retires V and discards its result. It never sets `arrived`. … the verification loop still calls
`blocking_recv`. … DESIGN §26 explicitly requires a concurrent driver ingesting declines to stop affected work."

**Constructibility.** The live engine of this build cannot produce the sequence. It runs at most one member of a
lineage at a time: a repair is spawned only inside a `merge_rejected` (the only `task_spawned` producer is the
tests' `scaffold::Run::spawn_repair`), a repair is dispatched only from the rejection that names it
(`run.rs`, `rejected_source`), a root awaits repair while its repair is live, and a lineage question blocks the
dispatch, attempt start and integration of every member (`lineage_has_question` in `check_dispatched`,
`check_attempt_started`, and queue eligibility through `task_is_awaiting_input`). So neither a decline nor a lineage
member's failed settlement finds another live member to close. The fold admits the state all the same (its
`declining_an_embedded_question_cancels_its_lineages_unprepared_verification` and
`declining_an_embedded_question_closes_a_running_sibling_and_refuses_its_late_result`), the design requires it
handled, and PR12's writer may reach it; so it is constructed, with the observer seam `Release::Append` — the
test's scheduler plants a recorded event at a quiescent point, fold-checked, appended by the coordinator — planting
a sibling repair's spawn, dispatch, start and park. Production passes no observer.

**The witnesses.** `a_declined_embedded_question_stops_the_verification_its_lineage_had_started`: beta's candidate
under verification, its gate held; the sibling parks; gamma's worker ending opens the pass that ingests the
decline. **Red at `898e7338`**: with gamma's candidate ready, the coordinator selected gamma's integration inside
`verify` and ended the command ("selection returned an integration of task 2 while a verification is open"); with
nothing else ready — the witness's first form, at the first witness tree — it was stuck with no pipeline live
(`repro/red-conc3-try2.log`: "0 pipeline(s) live"), which without an observer is the reviewer's `blocking_recv`
for ever. And `a_declined_embedded_question_stops_a_running_sibling_attempt`: beta's worker held when the decline
closes its generation. **Red at `898e7338`**: beta ran on — its gate was registered and run for a closed
generation. **Green**: the verification's gate and beta's worker cancelled, nothing appended for the cancelled
sequence, beta's late result never settled, the run completing.

**The root cause.** Nothing re-read the fold for the live pipelines after an append, and `verify`'s loop ended
only on an arrival or an interrupt.

**The repair.** `reconcile`, first in every admission pass (after the poison check, before the ending check —
answers are ingested and settlements applied between passes, inside `verify` as outside), stops each live
pipeline whose identity the fold no longer holds open (`stop`: its token cancelled, its pending request withdrawn
and refused, its snapshot request refused, a warning). `verify` watches its own transaction: gone, it waits for its
pipeline to end and returns an error that abandons the integration; `integrate()` appends nothing further and the
coordinator goes on (`abandoned`), where an error would have ended the command. The staging worktree, pin and
snapshots of the abandoned sequence are left to the terminal finalization and the next resume's
`reclaim_stale_residue`, which remove any staging and pin no open transaction owns.

**The class — every wait whose producer can vanish** (`measure/class-search-waits.txt`). The production intake's
`blocking_recv`: in `drive` only while a pipeline is live, in `finish` after every live pipeline was cancelled (each
sends its completion), in `verify` now bounded by its pipeline's liveness; `Client::ask`: always answered, refused
or dropped — and a held unresolved pair cannot strand a request, because the hold interrupts; `join`: each
pipeline ends after its completion. **Found in the deterministic intake:** `cancel_all` (phase 3) and the new
`stop` marked a pipeline `Running` although its request was still in the intake's buffer, unapplied, and the intake
then waited on the inbox for a pipeline that was waiting on it. Witnessed by
`a_halt_answers_a_request_its_intake_still_buffers` — **red at `898e7338`: it hung, and its 120 s watchdog fired**
— and `a_stop_answers_a_request_its_intake_still_buffers` (red at `898e7338` as a stuck point, there being no
reconcile there); repaired by the transition (an invocation's pipeline is `Running` once cancelled; a waiting one
only once its reply is refused). Production has no buffer. **The mutations:** `r1-conc-3-no-reconcile` (both
decline witnesses red), `r1-conc-3-no-unwind` (the verification witness and the stop witness red),
`r1-busy-cancel-all` and `r1-busy-stop` (each its witness, at the watchdog).

**Corrected in round R2 (below).** The repair was not complete as built. The admission pass that stopped the
verification went on selecting: with a candidate queued before the decline, selection found its integration
eligible — the transaction gone, `verify` not yet unwound — and the arm's refusal ended the command, the failure
this subsection's first witness saw red. That witness passed only because gamma's candidate became eligible after
`verify` had unwound (`R2-DECLINE-QUEUED`). And stopping one closed pipeline could grant another closed pipeline
the pair its withdrawal freed before that one was stopped (`R2-RECONCILE-GRANT`). Round R2 repairs both. And
"`reconcile`, first in every admission pass" is no longer the order: round R3 (below) acts on a halt the fold records
before `reconcile` runs, because under a halt reconciliation's withdrawals granted open pipelines (`R3-HALT-GRANT`).

#### `R1-CONC-4` — a completed verification's spend is invisible to admissions

**The finding**: "V completes with $20 of review charges; the coordinator accepts that completion but stores the
charges without updating `Spend`. … The next `admit()` still sees spend below $10 and dispatches an independent
task C. … Charging occurs only after A's snapshot drains. This contradicts R-V's explicit claim."

**Phase 4 repaired it, and round R1 verified it rather than redo it.** Phase 4 charges a verification's review
spend when its completion is accepted, before the next admission pass (R-AH), with
`a_verifications_review_spend_is_charged_before_the_next_selection`. That test is **green at `898e7338`**
(`repro/final-dc3136fb/a_verifications_review_spend_is_charged_before_the_next_selection.log`) and, ported to
`83a387bd` with the `started_under` fixture it needs (`repro/conc4-reviewed-port.diff`), **red there**: "t4 was never
dispatched" failed, t4 dispatched after the verification's completion had arrived and before its charge
(`repro/conc4-at-reviewed-83a387bd.log`). So the reviewer's sequence ran at the reviewed head and cannot run at the
start head; round R1's restructure of `verified_arrived` keeps the charge first. **The class — any pipeline's
reported spend:** an attempt's completion is settled, and its spend recorded, as it is received; a verification's
accepted completion is charged as it is received; a discarded completion — cancelled or stale — is never settled,
and its spend is unknown, as an interrupted attempt's is (R-AB's overshoot bound; `Spend::replay` agrees). **The
mutation** `r1-conc-4-late-charge` (the charge moved back to `verify`'s return) turns the test red.

#### Contention, the rest of the proof

**Under contention** (round C1's method: every test process pinned by `taskset` to CPUs 30 and 31, four
concurrent loops of twenty, one test thread each, a fresh `TMPDIR` per process; `tools/contend-module.sh`,
`tools/contend.sh`). The whole coordinator module at `dc3136fb` (binary `bins/dc3136fb-lib` under the private
target, sha256 `57b226b4…` in `proof/green-dc3136fb/binary-rebuilt.sha256`, the build whose log names this
worktree): **80 of 80 runs exit 0 and each of the module's 45 tests passes in all 80**, the kill child ignored as in
every run (`proof/contention-dc3136fb-module/summary.txt`); libtest's "finished in" has median 37.41 s, min 28.09 s
and max 45.25 s (`durations.txt` beside it). The registry lock's five tests — the new witness, phase 3's concurrent
add-and-remove witness and the three that pin the pre-hook binding — pass in 80 of 80 runs each
(`proof/contention-dc3136fb-registry/summary.txt`). **No test round R1 adds sleeps or waits on a clock for an
ordering**: the scheduler, the observer seam and the double's door order every interleaving (R-AD). Its three
bounded waits are the watchdogs of the registry-lock witness (60 s) and of the two intake witnesses (120 s), and they
decide nothing but a regression's failure.

§8 (round R1) holds the diff, the constraints, the census, the module diff proof, the full runs, the two targets'
type-check and the notes checks; §9 the gates.

### Review round 2 (round R2)

Written by `pr11_repair_r2` (`claude-opus-5-5`, `max`), a fresh session `orch_pr11` spawned on round R1's pushed head
`e6ecc64d` to repair review round 2's findings. Every file named here is under `~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_repair_r2/`
unless another directory is named.

**The review.** Three `gpt-6-astra` lenses at `max` — a delta lens over round R1's repairs, a regression lens over
phases 0–4 and a concurrency lens over phase 4 — ran at `e6ecc64d0508a9f9ec9d28896eee0ac6761e41ff` on the
`cameron-codex` account, through `~/orch-pr11/reviews/run-lens.sh` (the codex transport, `-s read-only`, a
`REVIEW_ROOT` detached worktree at that head per lens), launched at 2026-09-30T14:18:32Z; all three returned
**CHANGES_REQUIRED**. The texts, their logs and the orchestrator's triage are under `~/orch-pr11/reviews/`
(`review-327-r1-{delta,regression,concurrency4}-e6ecc64d.{log,review.md}`, `review-round2-triage.md`; sha256 in
`SHA256SUMS-r2`), and the reviewers' witness patches and logs, tests only, in `r2-witnesses/{delta,conc4}/`
(`SHA256SUMS-r2-witnesses`); all thirteen hashes verify (`measure/review-sha256-check.txt`). Five findings:
`R2-CONC4-1` (P1, executed), `R2-DECLINE-QUEUED` (P2, executed by two lenses), `R2-RECONCILE-GRANT` (P2, the grant
executed and the process start reasoned), `R2-REG-1` and `R2-REG-2` (P3, notes).

**The method** (round R1's: reproduce first, contend, mutate). Before any code changed, the reviewers' own witness
patches were applied to a `git archive` of `e6ecc64d` and run: all four tests red, as the reviewers saw them
(`repro/reviewers-e6ecc64d/`: the build log naming the scratch tree, the binary's sha256, one log per test). Then
this round's own witnesses were written. Each is a test red at the start head and green after the repair. The red
runs use a tree built by `tools/witness-scratch.py`: a `git archive` of `e6ecc64d` with round R2's block of test
items appended to the coordinator's test module and nothing else; the script checks that the module above the block
is the start head's unchanged, so the tree runs the start head's production code (`repro/final-2495300c/`: the build
log, the binary's sha256, one log per test, `SUMMARY.txt`). The green runs are the repaired head's binary
(`proof/green-2495300c/`). Each repair has a mutation that revives its defect in a copy of the repaired head
(`proof/mutants/`, `tools/run-mutants-r2.sh`). The repaired code is `2495300c`; the tests that pin the corrected notes
are `c0771a8c`.

#### `R2-CONC4-1` — a halt publishes a verification whose result had arrived

**The finding** (concurrency lens, verbatim in part): "Gamma is parked on a question; alpha has merged; beta's
verification is the only remaining pipeline. Beta sends `Verified`; the coordinator retires it and retains its
judgement in `arrived`. The next admission pass receives Gamma's halting decline. Because `live` is now empty, the
guard skips `halt()`. `verify_concurrently` returns the retained judgement, and integration prepares and publishes
it. … Closure step (2) requires interrupting the still-open `VerificationStarted` transaction."

**The witness**, `a_halt_after_the_verifications_result_arrived_interrupts_it_and_publishes_nothing`: gamma parks
on a question and alpha merges; beta's verification is then the only live pipeline (asserted when its last process,
a review, is released). The answer source, `DecliningAtPoll`, is armed at that release to decline at the second poll.
The deterministic intake applies the review's end and then the verification's completion, each followed by one
admission pass, so the decline is ingested in the pass after the completion was accepted. `discarded() == 0`
confirms the completion was accepted, not discarded as a cancelled pipeline's. **Red at `e6ecc64d`**: after
`question_answered` the log reads `merge_prepared`, `task_merged`, `run_finished`
(`repro/final-2495300c/a_halt_after_the_verifications_result_arrived_interrupts_it_and_publishes_nothing.log`; the
reviewer's witness gives the same sequence, `repro/reviewers-e6ecc64d/`). **Green at `2495300c`**:
`question_answered`, `merge_verification_interrupted`, `run_finished`. Nothing was prepared or merged for the
sequence, every process of the verification ended `Completed`, the pin was deleted expected-old, and the staging and
the sequence's snapshots were reclaimed before `run_finished`. The ledger balances with no duplicate, and the replay
equals the live fold.

**The root cause.** The admission pass decided whether a halt had anything to interrupt by the live table
(`halted && !self.live.is_empty()`). A verification whose completion has been accepted has no pipeline, but its
transaction stays `VerificationStarted` until `integrate()` appends a terminal, so the fold still shows it in
flight. Nothing else stood between the accepted result and the prepare: `verify`'s loop checks for an interrupt
before it looks at the arrival, and none was recorded.

**The repair.** `any_in_flight`: a halt is acted on when a pipeline is live or the fold shows in-flight work
(`closure::in_flight`). In the arrived window that is the verification. `halt()` records the interrupt, `verify` ends
`Interrupted` and records the sequence as cancelled (as it does for every interrupt; R-AF's vouching), `integrate()`
appends nothing further, and the closure appends `merge_verification_interrupted` and reclaims the pin, the staging
and the sequence's snapshots. The pipeline had ended, and each of its invocations had reported an end that
established its process gone — an unresolved one would have ended the command first. The terminal's detail text
said "the coordinator cancelled its pipeline … nothing was judged", false in this case; it now says the pipeline had
ended, cancelled or with a result the halt discards unprepared (`closure.rs`, `InFlight::interrupted`). A budget stop
is not a halt: it drains, and an arrived verification reaches its natural terminal as before
(`a_verifications_review_spend_is_charged_before_the_next_selection` still asserts its `merge_prepared` after
`budget_exceeded`).

**The class — every place that takes "no pipeline is live" for "nothing is in flight"** (`measure/class-search-in-flight.txt`,
the production region's every read of the live table, the arrival and `in_verify`, at both heads):
- the halt check in `admit` — repaired;
- `drive`'s idle arm (`live.is_empty()`, then `idle()`): `verify` is never on the stack there, so every in-flight
  identity this coordinator started has a live pipeline; the idle closure vouches for nothing, and anything else in
  flight is refused by `closure::settleable` before any append;
- `cancel_all` records the cancelled identities from the live table, and `finish` drains it: the arrived
  verification is recorded by `verify`'s `Interrupted` arm, for every interrupt;
- the budget stop's drain decision (ending and not halted: nothing cancelled): a drain, correct as it stands; a budget
  stop converted to a halt goes through the repaired check;
- a shutdown and an append error: each records its interrupt unconditionally, and `verify` checks the interrupt
  before the arrival, so no arrived result is returned after either; each ends the command appending nothing, and the
  next process's recovery settles the transaction (R-AJ, R-AI);
- `verify`'s abandonment (`!live.contains_key(&pipeline)` once the transaction is gone): it asks the fold first.

**The mutation** `r2-conc4-1-live-only-halt` (the check back to the live table alone): the witness fails with left
`["question_answered", "merge_prepared", "task_merged", "run_finished"]`; the module's other 48 tests pass
(`proof/mutants/SUMMARY.txt`).

#### `R2-DECLINE-QUEUED` — a decline aborts the command when another candidate is queued

**The finding** (delta lens, verbatim in part; the concurrency lens found the same): "Start beta's verification,
park its sibling on an embedded question, and let gamma finish into the candidate queue **before** accepting the
non-halting decline. The decline removes beta's transaction. `admit()` reconciles its pipeline but continues
selecting: gamma is now eligible, while `in_verify` remains set. The command fails with 'selection returned an
integration of task 2 while a verification is open,' before reaching the new abandonment check."

**The witness**, `a_decline_that_cancels_the_open_verification_goes_on_to_integrate_the_queued_candidate`: round
R1's verification witness with a fourth task, delta, whose worker ending opens the pass that ingests the decline. So
gamma's candidate is queued before the decline (asserted: its `task_candidate_created` precedes `question_answered`)
while the verification's gate is held (asserted when the decline is armed). **Red at `e6ecc64d`**: the command ended
with the reviewer's refusal. **Green**: the verification's gate was cancelled and nothing was appended for its
sequence after the decline. Gamma's queued candidate was integrated next (its `merge_prepared` and `task_merged` after
the decline), and the abandonment warning was recorded. Alpha, gamma and delta merged; the ledger balances, and the
replay equals the live fold.

**The root cause.** Round R1's `reconcile` stops the verification in the pass that ingests the decline, and `verify`
unwinds when its transaction disappears — but the pass itself went on. With the transaction gone,
`eligible_integration_candidate` returned gamma's, and the integration arm's refusal (one transaction at a time)
ended the command. Round R1's witness had gamma's candidate become eligible only after `verify` had unwound, so it
never met the refusal.

**The repair.** `abandoning`: while `verify` is on the stack and the fold no longer holds its transaction open, the
admission pass ends — after the reconcile that stops the pipeline, and after the ending check, so a halt in the same
append still cancels at once — before ingestion or selection. `verify` then waits for its pipeline to end and
unwinds; `integrate()` appends nothing; the coordinator's `integrate` reads `abandoned` and returns to the outer pass,
which selects gamma. `in_verify` now carries the verification's identity, so the pass can ask the fold.

**The class — every append that can remove `verify`'s transaction, and every selection made under `verify` after it
is gone.** The appends are an ingested decline (in the pass), a lineage member's failed settlement (in `handle`,
before the next pass) and a planted event (the test seam), and each is followed by an admission pass that now ends
at `abandoning` before anything is selected. The selections are the integration (the finding), a retry, a dispatch,
a budget stop and an answer's ingestion, all after the check. Round R1's claim that `R1-CONC-3` was repaired is
corrected in its subsection above and in R-V (§3).

**The mutation** `r2-decline-queued-no-return` (the early return removed): the witness fails with the refusal; the
other 48 pass.

#### `R2-RECONCILE-GRANT` — reconciliation grants another closed pipeline while withdrawing the first

**The finding** (delta lens, verbatim in part): "With agent and pool limits of one, an unrelated invocation holds
agent X. Pipeline A waits for `{X, P}`; pipeline B waits for `{Y, P}`, blocked by A's reservation of pool P. A
non-halting lineage decline closes both identities. `reconcile()` stops A first. Cancelling A's pending registration
pumps the queue and immediately sends B a successful grant. B's cancellation token remains unset until the
subsequent `stop(B)`, allowing its process to start after its identity closed."

**Constructibility.** As for `R1-CONC-3`, the live engine never runs two members of one lineage, so the state is
built — here on a coordinator the test assembles itself (`with_two_closed_siblings`). The fold is real: the root
and an unrelated holder dispatched and started through `begin_dispatch`, a sibling repair planted running, a second
sibling parked, and the decline ingested through `ingest_answers`. So are the broker's registrations, at limits
`(1, 1)`. The live table holds three entries standing for the three pipelines, and the two requests wait on their
replies.

**The witnesses.** `stopping_two_closed_pipelines_grants_neither_the_pair_the_first_one_frees` calls `reconcile`.
**Red at `e6ecc64d`**: the sibling's reply was `Ok`, and the observer was handed the sibling's worker as granted.
**Green**: both replies refused, nothing handed out, both requests settled cancelled, the holder's pair kept, and a
warning names the withdrawn grant. The class witness, `a_pipeline_whose_identity_closed_is_granted_nothing_before_it_is_stopped`,
reaches the other delivery sites in the step between the closing append and the next admission pass: the closed
sibling's gate registration, its snapshot request, a snapshot it waits for when the gate opens, and the pair the
holder's end frees. **Red at `e6ecc64d` at every site**: `(Some(true), Some(true), Some(true), 2, (Some(true), None))`
— registered, granted, granted, two snapshots held, the freed pair granted to the closed root — with the sibling's
gate and the root's worker handed out as granted. **Green**: every one refused, no snapshot held, nothing pending or
running, the gate never registered.

**The root cause.** The broker grants by its queue alone and knows nothing of the fold, and the coordinator handed
every grant a settlement returned to the pipeline waiting for it without asking whether that pipeline still served
an open identity. A slotted request's standing is checked at registration, not at the grant; a gate registers with
no standing at all.

**The repair.** `receives` — live, not cancelled, identity open in the fold, no interrupt recorded — is asked at
every place a grant is delivered. In `reply_granted`, which hands on a pair freed by a stop, an end or a withdrawn
grant, a grant for a pipeline that does not receive it is withdrawn undelivered (the registration cancelled with no
process started, R-AE's position without a Runner call) and its request refused; whatever that frees is handed on
the same way. `admit_invocation` refuses a registration from a pipeline whose identity closed as cancelled, and
`begin_snapshot` and `grant_snapshots` refuse its snapshot. Which of these the production intake reaches with a
closed identity: `reply_granted` from `stop`, whenever one append closes two identities (the finding). The others are
reached only between a closing append and the next admission pass. The production intake never leaves that gap —
every message applied and every answer ingested is followed by a pass that reconciles first — and the observer's
`Release::Append` seam does.

**The class — every place a grant is delivered** (`measure/class-search-grants.txt`, every `send(Ok` and every
hand-on of a broker grant in the production region, at both heads): the four above (repaired); `cancel_all` and
`stop`, which only refuse; `abandoned`, a withdrawal that hands on through `reply_granted`. Nothing else answers a
request with a grant.

**The mutations**, one per site (`proof/mutants/SUMMARY.txt`): `r2-reconcile-grant-delivered` (`reply_granted`
delivers without asking) turns both witnesses red, the class witness at its freed pair alone;
`r2-admit-closed-identity`, `r2-snapshot-begin-closed` (the phase-4 condition) and `r2-grant-snapshots-closed` each
turn the class witness red at exactly its own site's element, and the other 48 tests pass under each.

**Corrected in round R3 (below).** The class was grants to a closed or cancelled pipeline, and `receives`' "no
interrupt recorded" left out a halt the fold had recorded and the pass had not yet acted on — the pass reconciled
first. A withdrawal's grant could reach an open pipeline after the halt was durable (`R3-HALT-GRANT`). Round R3 adds
the halt in the fold to `receives` and `admit_invocation` (`interrupted`) and acts on the halt before reconciling.

#### `R2-REG-1` and `R2-REG-2` — notes promising what the code no longer does

**The findings** (regression lens, verbatim in part): "`run.md:275` says concurrent cancellation, draining,
promotion and publication remain refused by `closure::refuse_unclosable`. … Phase 4 deliberately implements these
operations in `close_run`"; and "`preflight.md:176` promises every registration is cancelled, every pair released
and the ledger balanced after refusal. `attempt.md:1229` likewise says both Runner outcomes settle the registration.
… R1 intentionally introduced this behavior. These contract paragraphs must describe retention until process exit."

**The repair.** `run.md`'s `Self::Closure` arm now says what phase 4 does in `close_run` and what is still refused:
in-flight work nothing vouches for, in the sentence `refuse_unclosable` keeps for the frozen recovery tests; the
section's heading, which `run/tests.rs` does not pin, is unchanged. `preflight.md`'s refusal and `attempt.md`'s
registration protocol now say that an unresolved invocation keeps its registration and pair until the process exits,
and `attempt.md`'s subheading says the end is reported on every path out, not that it is settled. **The class —
every note promising release, settlement or balance after a failure, or naming `refuse_unclosable` as the concurrent
closure's refusal** (`measure/class-search-notes.txt`, the same grep at both heads): `identity.md` and `permits.md`
already state the retention (round R1); `preflight/tests.md`'s "both ledgers balance … on the refusal path" holds for
its tests, none of which leaves a probe unresolved, and now says so; `closure.md`'s `refuse_unclosable` section
describes a caller that vouches for nothing, which is true. `closure.md`'s sections on the interrupted terminal and on
`Cancelled` are updated for `R2-CONC4-1`.

**The witnesses** (`c0771a8c`), in the form `run/tests.rs` already uses to pin its notes: each reads its section with
its whitespace collapsed, asserts what the section now states and that the retired sentence does not come back —
`the_closure_notes_say_what_closure_does_under_concurrency_and_what_it_still_refuses` (`run.md`, `R2-REG-1`),
`the_refusal_notes_keep_a_probe_whose_process_is_unresolved` (`preflight.md`) and
`the_registration_notes_keep_an_invocation_whose_process_is_unresolved` (`attempt.md`, both `R2-REG-2`). **Red against
`e6ecc64d`'s notes**, each at its first pin (a `git archive` of `e6ecc64d` with only the three tests applied,
`repro/notes-pins.patch`; `repro/notes-pins-e6ecc64d/test.log`, 3 failed). **Green at `c0771a8c`**
(`proof/notes-pins-green/test.log`). No mutation is added for a notes finding: the start head's notes are the defect

#### `R3-HALT-GRANT` — a grant delivered after a halt is durable

**The finding** (concurrency lens, verbatim in part; the delta lens found the same): "`admit()` calls `reconcile()`
before acting on the recorded halt. Reconciliation withdraws closed pipelines’ requests and immediately delivers newly
available grants. The delivery guard checks the recipient’s identity and `self.interrupt`, but the latter has not yet
been set. … H holds agent X. A waits for `{X, P}`, reserving P against later requests. Unrelated B waits for `{Y, P}`.
… A sibling repair’s halting decline closes A’s identity and records the halt. B remains open. … `reply_granted()`
sends B success … B’s thread can receive that grant and start its process before the coordinator finishes
reconciliation and calls `halt()`. This violates the claimed withdrawal of pending invocations on halt and can start
additional paid work after the halt." The delta lens: "The witness observed a successful grant after the durable halt,
with cancellation still `false`. … Activate the halt before reconciliation can deliver grants." Both graded it P2
because their witnesses plant a fold-valid sibling-lineage state the live engine does not generate.

**Constructibility.** As for round R2's grant witnesses: a halting decline must close the identity of a live pipeline
whose withdrawn request frees another's pair, and in this build's live engine a root whose repair exists awaits that
repair and is never in flight beside it (`R1-CONC-3`'s constructibility, above). So the state is built on a coordinator
the test assembles (`with_a_waiter_behind_a_decline`). Four unrelated attempts — the root, a holder, a waiter and an
asker — are dispatched and started through `begin_dispatch`. At slot limits `(1, 1)` the holder's worker holds agent
`a`, the root's worker waits for `{a, p}` at the head of the queue, and the waiter's for `{b, p}` behind that
reservation. A sibling repair of the root is planted parked on an embedded question, and its decline is ingested
through `ingest_answers` — the halting carrier the live engine does produce — failing the root's lineage and, with
`decline_halts_run`, recording the halt. The fold is real, and so are the broker's registrations; four live-table
entries stand for the four pipelines.

**The witnesses.** `a_recorded_halt_withdraws_every_waiting_request_before_reconciliation_frees_a_pair` runs the
admission pass that follows the halting decline. **Red at `8eae559a`**: `(true, [], (1, 2), Some(false), Some(true),
[k2 worker])` — the halt was acted on and every pipeline cancelled, but only after reconciliation: the broker granted
the waiter the pair the root's withdrawal freed (its grant count went from 1 to 2), the waiter's reply was `Ok`, and
the observer was handed its worker as granted. **Green**: `(true, [], (1, 1), Some(false), Some(false), [])` — both
waiting requests withdrawn while still pending and refused, the broker granting nothing, nothing handed out, no warning
of a withdrawn grant (none was computed), the holder's, the waiter's and the asker's in-flight attempts vouched for the
closure, and only the holder's invocation running, to be released by its own end. The class witness,
`no_grant_reaches_an_open_pipeline_between_a_recorded_halt_and_the_pass_that_acts_on_it`, reaches every other delivery
site in the step between the halting append and the pass (`grant_sites`): the asker's slotted registration (`{c}`:
free, and clear of the head's reservation), its snapshot request, its snapshot queued while the gate was closed when
the gate opens, and the pair the holder's end frees — to the closed root at the head of the queue, then, that grant
withdrawn, to the open waiter. **Red at `8eae559a` at every site**: `(Some(true), Some(true), Some(true), 2,
(Some(false), Some(true)), [k3 worker, k2 worker])` — registered and granted, granted, granted, two snapshots held,
and the freed pair granted to the waiter. **Green**: every one refused, no snapshot held, nothing handed out, nothing
pending or running, the asker never registered. The reviewers' witnesses pass at `60fb5913` too, the delta lens's
non-halting control still granting (`repro/reviewers-60fb5913/`).

**The root cause.** The coordinator had two notions of "the command is halting": the fold's `halted_at`, durable from
the halting append, and its own `interrupt`, set only when an admission pass acted on the halt. The pass reconciled
before it acted, and every grant delivery asked only the second (`receives`, `admit_invocation`). So in the window the
pass itself opened — between the append and its own `halt()` — a withdrawal that freed a pair handed it to an open
pipeline as if no halt had been recorded.

**The repair** (`60fb5913`, `coordinator.rs`'s production region). (1) The admission pass acts on a halt the fold
records, with anything in flight, **before** it reconciles: `halt()` cancels every live pipeline and withdraws every
pending request while it is still pending — `withdraw_pending` takes the whole queue without pumping it, so no
withdrawal frees a pair or moves the head for another — and refuses every owed reply; reconciliation then finds every
pipeline cancelled. (2) `interrupted` — an interrupt recorded, or a halt in the fold — replaces the bare `interrupt`
check in `receives`, which every delivery of a pair or a snapshot asks, and in `admit_invocation`, so no grant is
delivered between a halting append and the pass that acts on it, whatever the order of reconciliation, withdrawal and
activation; the three refusal texts name the new reason. A pipeline whose identity the halting append itself closed is
now cancelled by the halt with the others and recorded in `cancelled_work`; that settles nothing, because the closure
settles only what the fold shows in flight (`closure::settleable`).

**A budget stop keeps draining, and its pending requests are granted.** The packet's closure procedure step (2) drains
a BudgetExceeded run "to natural settlements"; step (1) and `precedence_consequences` stop admission ("after a halting
settlement or budget_exceeded no admission occurs"). Admission is selection — a dispatch, a retry, an integration —
which the pass stops at its ending check, as before. A pending request of a live pipeline is not admission: it is an
invocation of an attempt or a verification already admitted, which that pipeline must run to reach its natural
settlement, and refused, the pipeline would return an error and end the command — neither a natural settlement nor a
halt's cancellation. So **a pending request of a live pipeline whose identity is open is still granted under a budget
stop**, by an end, a withdrawal or a reconciliation, as R-AH already reads ("slot and snapshot grants go on so live
pipelines can finish"). `interrupted` therefore reads `halted_at`, never `run_is_ending`, and the pass's cancellation
keys on the halt, never on the ending. A halting settlement drained under the stop is a halt from its append on. The
controls: `a_budget_stop_drains_so_the_pair_reconciliation_frees_reaches_an_open_waiter` — the same pass under a planted
`budget_exceeded` after a decline that does not halt (the fold refuses a decline while a budget stop is current): no
interrupt, only the root stopped, the waiter granted the freed pair — and
`under_a_budget_stop_every_grant_site_still_grants_an_open_pipeline` — every site delivers, only the closed root is
refused. Both pass at both heads.

**The class — every path that delivers a grant, and every place an interrupt is activated after an append that could
have made it durable** (`measure/class-search-r3.txt`, over the raw listings at both heads,
`measure/class-search-r3-raw.txt`, `tools/class-search-r3.py`). *Grants:* the four delivery sites round R2 guarded —
`grant_snapshots` (from `open_gate` and from `verify`'s entry), `admit_invocation`, `reply_granted` (from `abandoned`,
`end_invocation` and `stop`: a withdrawn grant, a completion or a cancellation, and reconciliation's withdrawal, the head
recomputation included) and `begin_snapshot` — now all ask `interrupted`; `cancel_all` and `stop` only refuse. The
broker's grant producers are unchanged and byte-identical at both heads: `SlotTable::enqueue`, `release` and `withdraw`
pump the queue, and their grants reach a pipeline only through `reply_granted` (a registration's own pump grants at most
the registering invocation); `withdraw_all` — `withdraw_pending`, from an interrupt's `cancel_all` and a shutdown — does
not pump; `cancel_all_running`, the append-error protocol's obligation (3), withdraws every pending request before it
releases the running ones, so those releases pump an empty queue. *Interrupts:* a halt becomes durable only by an append
that sets `halted_at` — an ingested decline (in the pass, whose next iteration now acts on it first), a settlement (in
`handle`, after which the next pass acts on it first, nothing running between) and the tests' `Release::Append` (one
buffered message is applied before the next pass, and any grant it would deliver asks `interrupted`). An append error
poisons the fold inside the emitting call and returns to `fail` through `?`, nothing running between but the returns;
a poisoned fold holds no identity open (`Standing::of` reads `Holds::Nothing`), so `receives` and `admit_invocation`
refuse through the identity check whatever the order, and the next pass refuses at `is_poisoned`. A shutdown and every
other error are recorded in `interrupt` by the call that decides them, followed at once by `cancel_all`.
`budget_exceeded` is not an interrupt, and drains.

**The mutations** (`proof/mutants/SUMMARY.txt`; each a `git archive` copy of `60fb5913` with one change, built on a
private slot pool, its Compiling line naming the copy, and the whole coordinator module — 61 tests, 2 ignored — run
against it). Every mutant is killed, and its red tests are exactly these:

| mutant | the change | red |
|---|---|---|
| `r3-m1-reconcile-before-the-halt` | the pass reconciles before it acts on the halt (round R2's order); the guard kept | the admission-pass witness alone, at the broker's grant count `(1, 2)`: the waiter granted by the broker and withdrawn, nothing delivered |
| `r3-m2-guard-ignores-a-halt-in-the-fold` | `interrupted` reads `interrupt` alone (round R2's guard); the order kept | the class witness alone, at every site, as at `8eae559a` |
| `r3-m3-both-reverted` | both: round R2's coordinator, the defect revived | both halt witnesses, the waiter answered `Ok` and handed out |
| `r3-m4-guard-refuses-a-budget-drain` | `interrupted` reads `run_is_ending` | both budget controls and the six budget-stop tests below |
| `r3-m5-a-budget-stop-cancels` | the pass cancels on any ending run | the admission-pass control and the same six |

The six earlier tests the two over-broad mutants turn red: `a_budget_stop_drains_live_pipelines_to_their_settlements_and_ends_budget_exceeded`,
`a_verifications_review_spend_is_charged_before_the_next_selection`,
`over_budget_prefix_without_budget_exceeded_is_not_ending_at_width_three`,
`retained_generation_closed_at_run_end_at_width_three`,
`run_finished_halted_and_budget_exceeded_accepted_with_deferred_items_at_width_three` and
`the_broker_ledgers_balance_at_every_end_and_start_empty_at_every_next_start_at_width_three`.

#### `R3-BODY` — the body's qualification covered a reachable P1

**The finding** (delta lens, verbatim in part): "The blanket claim covers all three code findings. However,
`a_halt_after_the_verifications_result_arrived_interrupts_it_and_publishes_nothing` uses three independent tasks through
`halting_on_gamma`: gamma parks, alpha merges, beta’s verification reports, and ordinary answer ingestion records the
halt. It plants no sibling events and assembles no coordinator manually. That sequence is reachable through the
implemented concurrent driver. Restrict the sibling-state qualification to `R2-DECLINE-QUEUED` and
`R2-RECONCILE-GRANT`; otherwise the body understates the repaired P1’s reachability."

**Checked, and repaired in the body.** The body's round R2 paragraph said: "The code findings' states — two members of
one lineage live at once — are unreachable in this build's live engine, as round R1's were; the witnesses plant them
through the observer seam or assemble the coordinator." `R2-CONC4-1`'s witness plants nothing: it drives three
independent tasks through `run_concurrently` under the scheduler, and the halt is an ordinary declined answer. **The
repaired P1 was reachable** in the implemented concurrent driver, which production does not call yet (R-G). The body now
restricts the qualification to `R2-DECLINE-QUEUED` and `R2-RECONCILE-GRANT` and says so of `R2-CONC4-1`. This record's
own round R2 statements were already scoped — §11 names "the decline-with-a-queued-candidate state and the
two-closed-siblings state", and §13's constructibility paragraph sits under `R2-RECONCILE-GRANT` — and the notes carry
no such sentence (`measure/class-search-body-r3.txt`, one grep over the body, the record and the notes). Round R3's own
witnesses plant their sibling as round R2's grant witnesses did; the body and §11 say that of them, restricted to them.
