#### `R4-REG-1` — a reader thread that cannot be started leaks the linked child

**The finding** (regression lens, verbatim): "Reader-thread creation failure leaks the child during fixture construction.
[src/workspace_manager/fixture.rs:3038] Executed on Linux; Windows/macOS consequence reasoned. Sequence: `Command::spawn`
succeeds → creating the reader thread fails → `std::thread::spawn` panics → construction unwinds before `LinkedChild`
exists. Dropping the raw `std::process::Child` neither kills nor reaps it, so `LinkedChild::drop` never performs cleanup.
I compiled the exact helper source in an isolated witness and forced `pthread_create` to return `EAGAIN`. The child
survived the failed constructor; the control killed and reaped it normally. … Establish a cleanup guard immediately after
spawning the child, before creating the reader. This violates §6's cleanup requirement and the fixture's claim that a
failing test leaves no process behind." P2, executed; triaged *fixed* (an executed witness: `MAINTAINING.md` step 5).

**The witness, first, red at the start head.** A test can refuse a thread for real: a `std::thread::Builder` asked for a
stack no address space can map (`readiness::unstartable_reader`, 2^(`usize::BITS` − 2) bytes) fails in `pthread_create`,
which answers `EAGAIN` — the reviewer's fault, without a shim. So the constructors take their reader's builder from the
caller (`LinkedChild::spawn_with`, `Producer::adopt_with`; `spawn` and `adopt` pass `thread::Builder::new()`), and the
witness hands over the unmappable one and learns the child's pid when the constructor asks for the reader.
`workspace_manager::tests::a_linked_child_whose_reader_cannot_be_started_is_killed_and_reaped` asserts that the failed
construction leaves nothing under that pid, not even a zombie (`fixture::process_exists` counts one); the child is told
to run a test no test has the name of, so it leaves nothing of its own. The red run's tree is a `git archive` of
`1fe988cd` with exactly this added (`tools/r4-witness-scratch.py`, `repro/r4-scratch-build.txt`): the two constructors
split into `spawn`/`spawn_with` and `adopt`/`adopt_with` **in the start head's order**, their one changed line std's own
definition of `thread::spawn` — `Builder::new().spawn(f).expect("failed to spawn thread")` — with the caller's builder;
`unstartable_reader`; and the two witnesses. **Red at `1fe988cd`** (`repro/r4-red-1fe988cd/test.log`, its binary's
sha256 `b975f0a2…` beside it): "the linked child 2276739 outlived its failed constructor, neither killed nor reaped (its
state: R); the constructor failed with: failed to spawn thread: Os { code: 11, kind: WouldBlock, message: \"Resource
temporarily unavailable\" }" — the child was *running*, as the reviewer saw it. Both children were gone once the test
binary had exited (`repro/r4-red-1fe988cd/leftover-check.txt`). **Green at the repair** (`proof/r4-green/`, binary sha256
`8ec7cf53…`): both pass. A first green attempt ran the scratch tree's binary from the shared slot — no Compiling line named
the worktree — and is kept as `proof/r4-green-INVALID-stale-slot/`, not counted.

**The root cause.** Both constructors built their reader before the value that owns the child: `LinkedChild::spawn` took
the child's pipes and called `std::thread::spawn` with a raw `Child` in a local, building `Self` only after; a panic
there — the thread refused, or the stdout `expect` — unwound past the `Child`, whose drop neither kills nor waits. The
fixture's own claim ("a test that fails midway leaves no process and no thread behind") held from the moment the
constructor returned, not from the spawn.

**The repair** (`48ea79d3`). `LinkedChild::spawn_with` builds `Self` — the child, its stdin, no reader yet — on the
statement after the spawn, before anything else is asked of the system; the reader is started after it and installed in
the owner (`Mutex::get_mut`); a thread that cannot be started unwinds through `LinkedChild::drop`, which closes stdin,
kills and reaps the child and finds no reader to join. The panic now names the child: "start the reader of the linked
child {pid}: …". `fixture.rs`'s doc comments say the order and why, citing the finding.

**The class — every fixture constructor that creates a process and then does run-time-fallible work before its owner
exists** (`measure/r4-class-search.txt`; the 71 process-creating call sites under `src/`, `-raw.txt`). In it, and fixed
the same way, with a witness each: `LinkedChild::spawn` (the finding) and `readiness::Producer::adopt`, whose reader thread
was started before `Self` existed. `Producer::adopt` is reached by every caller that hands it a freshly spawned child —
`fixture::spawn_ready_helper` (PR11's reaper test, and the frozen `recover/tests.rs`, which needs no edit), the readiness
tests, four `rundir` tests and one in `workspace.rs` — and its witness,
`agent::proc::tests::a_producer_whose_reader_cannot_be_started_is_killed_and_reaped`, is red at the start head the same
way ("the producer 2276738 outlived its failed adoption, neither killed nor reaped") and green after; its section in
`readiness.md` says the order. Outside the class, each with its reason in the file: `ParkedFork::holding`, `ReapedChild`,
`spawn_signal_helper`, `hold_inherited_descriptors` and `Served::spawn` (the owner exists before anything fallible);
`rundir::tests::ScenarioChild::spawn` (an `expect` on a stream the same command pipes, so nothing between can fail at run
time); `KillableGitChild` and `SampledChild`, which own a child and are not guards (their samplers kill and wait them);
helpers that return a raw `Child`; and test bodies and synchronous helpers that hold one for their whole life, where a
panic can leave the child as a raw `Child` always can — pre-existing test code, unchanged; and the Process funnel's own
production spawns.

**Two corrections the repair needed, each found by a gate this box runs.** (1) `unstartable_reader`'s two callers are the
`#[cfg(unix)]` witnesses, so ungated it was dead code on Windows, where `-D warnings` refuses it; the Windows-target clippy
here found it (`240141d1` gates it, as `fixture::spawn_ready_helper` is gated for the same reason). (2) The full suite at
`240141d1` failed `runner::container::tests::the_readiness_allowance_names_the_paths_it_is_written_against`
(`dev/suite-1-240141d1/`): that census counts the denied primitives `readiness.rs` reaches by their call forms, and the
repair's `builder.spawn(..)` matched `.spawn(`, one of `std::process::Command::spawn`'s, though a thread is not a process.
`f81e7bd8` writes the thread's start in its path form, `thread::Builder::spawn(builder, ..)`, so the file still reaches
exactly the five primitives its allowlist row names; the census, an instrument, is unchanged, and `readiness.md` says
why the call is spelled so.

**The mutations** (`tools/run-mutants-r4.sh`, `tools/mutate.py`; `proof/mutants/SUMMARY-r4.txt`): each a `git archive`
copy of the repaired head with one constructor put back in the start head's order — the reader started before the owner —
built on a private slot pool, its Compiling line naming the copy, and run against 33 tests: both witnesses, the
`agent::proc::tests` whose names begin `a_` or `the_bound` (the readiness producer's among them) and the two two-process
coordinator tests named `a_foreign_census…`, which spawn through `LinkedChild`. `r4-m1-linked-child-reader-first` turns the
`LinkedChild` witness red and nothing else; `r4-m2-producer-reader-first` the `Producer` witness and nothing else; 32 of
the 33 pass under each. Run at `48ea79d3` and again at `f81e7bd8`, with the same result.

**Under contention** (round C1's method; §8): the two witnesses, 200 of 200 runs on two CPUs, each passing in all 200
(`proof/contention-18577661-r4-witnesses/summary.txt`). Neither waits on a clock: the refusal is the OS's at once, and the
observation follows the constructor's return.

§8 (phase 7) holds the diff, the constraints, the census, the module diff proof, the full runs, the two targets'
type-check and the notes checks; §9 the gates.

### The full review (round R5)

Written by `pr11_repair_r5` (`claude-opus-5-5`, `max`), a fresh session `orch_pr11` spawned on phase 7's pushed head
`a9e88039` to repair the full review's six findings. Every file named here is under `~/orch-pr11/logs/pr11_repair_r5/`
unless another directory is named.

**The review.** The full review of record: three `gpt-6-astra` lenses at `max` — scope conformance and fix-check,
regression, and concurrency — ran at phase 7's pushed head `a9e88039e7b789a426224fb9de3920774313fc37` on the
`cameron-codex` account, through `~/orch-pr11/reviews/run-lens.sh` (the codex transport, `-s read-only`, a `REVIEW_ROOT`
detached worktree at that head per lens), launched at 2026-10-01T01:35Z; all three returned **CHANGES_REQUIRED** (the
regression lens at 01:48Z, the concurrency lens at 01:57Z, the scope lens at 02:01Z). The texts, their logs and the
orchestrator's triage are under `~/orch-pr11/reviews/` (`review-327-full-{scope,regression,concurrency}-a9e88039.{log,review.md}`,
`review-full-triage.md`; sha256 in `SHA256SUMS-full`), and the reviewers' witness and mutation patches and logs, tests
only, in `full-witnesses/{scope,conc-append,conc-identity}/` (`SHA256SUMS-full-witnesses`); all twenty-eight hashes verify
(`measure/review-sha256-check.txt`). Six findings: `FULL-SC-1` (P1), `FULL-SC-2` = `FULL-CONC-2` (P1, an executed
mutation by two lenses), `FULL-SC-3` (P1, an executed mutation), `FULL-CONC-1` (P2, executed), `FULL-REG-1` (P2,
executed with `sh -n`, the test's failure reasoned) and `FULL-SC-4` (P3, executed). The orchestrator triaged all six
*fixed*; this round repairs the five contained ones first, each with its witness, and `FULL-SC-1` after them.

**The method** (rounds R1 to R4's: reproduce first, contend, mutate). The witnesses were written into the worktree's test
modules before any repair, and the tracked files at that moment were frozen as one copy on the private target
(`snap/start`, its identity beside it: `a9e88039` plus `repro/start-head/witnesses.patch`, sha256 `94916591…`, 23,769
bytes, every hunk inside `coordinator.rs`'s test module — the lowest at line 3,017, the module opening at 1,739:
`repro/start-head/patch-confinement.txt`), so a run against it is a run against the start head's production code. The red
runs are that tree's binary (`repro/start-head/`, `repro/start-head-reg1/`: the build log naming the worktree, the
binary's sha256 `7c1c9275…`, one log per test, `SUMMARY.txt`; `tools/run-each.sh` runs each test in its own process under
a fresh `TMPDIR`). Every mutation is a substitution in a scratch copy of a frozen tree, built on its own slot pool, its
`Compiling` line naming the copy, with the whole coordinator module run against it (`tools/mutate-tree.py`): first
copies of the start tree (`proof/mutants-start/`, `tools/run-mutants-start.sh`), then of the repaired tree
(`snap/r5-fixed`, `proof/r5-fixed.patch` sha256 `508d852c…`; `proof/mutants-r5/`, `tools/run-mutants-r5.sh`), the
identity module too, and both trees run unmutated as controls: the start tree fails exactly `FULL-CONC-1`'s witness and
`FULL-SC-4`'s pin (`control-start`), the repaired tree nothing (`control-fixed`: 105 of 105). The green runs are the
repaired binary, whose `src/` is `218476b6`'s byte for byte (`proof/green-218476b6/`, `provenance.txt`). The code is
`218476b6`; `114067f3` moves one Windows seed (below).

#### `FULL-SC-2` = `FULL-CONC-2` — the identity-binding tests cannot fail

**The finding** (scope lens, verbatim in part): "`coordinator.rs:1312` unconditionally rejects `Origin::Injected`. The
stale/mismatched completion tests use that origin, so rejection does not establish that genuine pipeline completions are
checked against their registered identity and the fold. In an exact-head archive, I disabled both checks—`live.identity
!= *identity` and `!identity.open_in(self.run)`. **All 78 coordinator tests still passed** … This is a missing required
proof, not an observed failure of the current identity checks." The concurrency lens, the same with the binding alone
removed: "**All 78 existing coordinator tests passed**, including the mismatch and seeded-injection tests. An added
witness using `Origin::Pipeline` passed against the reviewed code and failed against the mutant: `real alpha completion
carrying beta's live identity was accepted`." Each lens's suite run on its mutant is in `full-witnesses/`
(`scope/identity-checks-disabled.log`, `conc-identity/all-original-coordinator-tests-mutant.log`: 78 passed, 3 ignored).

**The witnesses** take a completion down the path a pipeline's takes. `completion_on_the_pipeline_channel` assembles a
coordinator over a width-three run whose alpha and beta attempts have started, their pipelines live with their jobs, and
sends one completion through the named pipeline's own `Client` into the coordinator's inbox; `receive_one` takes it with
no observer — the production intake — so it arrives as `Origin::Pipeline` and meets `accepts` as a real completion
does, and what the coordinator did is returned whole (interrupt, live and cancelled pipelines, discards, appends).
`a_pipelines_completion_naming_another_attempt_of_its_task_is_discarded_and_its_pipeline_kept` (attempt 2 of alpha's
own task), `a_pipelines_completion_carrying_another_live_pipelines_identity_is_discarded_and_settles_nothing` (the
concurrency lens's case: beta's identity, which the fold holds open, from alpha's pipeline) and
`a_pipelines_completion_for_an_identity_the_fold_closed_is_discarded_and_settles_nothing` (beta's attempt settled first,
then beta's own completion before any pass stops the pipeline). Each asserts the discard, its count, the check's own
warning, nothing appended, and which pipelines remain. **Green at the start head** (`repro/start-head/SUMMARY.txt`):
the guard was correct; its proof was missing.

**The mutations, the reviewers' own.** `sc2-m-bind` (the binding removed, the concurrency lens's) turns red the wrong
attempt's witness and the crossed identity's, and the two injection tests as this round strengthens them (below);
`sc2-m-open` (the fold check removed) the closed identity's alone; `sc2-m-both` (both, the scope lens's) all five. The
same at the start head (`proof/mutants-start/`) and at the repaired head (`proof/mutants-r5/`), where nothing else fails.
With the binding removed, the wrong attempt is refused by the fold check instead and the live pipeline retired — its
own completion would then be stale; the crossed identity is accepted, alpha's pipeline retired and its job settled with a
completion that was not its own.

**The root cause.** The injector's refusal is the last check in `accepts`, and every forged completion a test sent came
through the injector, so the binding and the fold check were shadowed: whatever they did, an injected completion ended
discarded and counted, with only a different warning. On the production intake both are defence in depth — a pipeline's
identity is fixed when it is spawned, and an identity closes in a step after which the admission pass stops the pipeline
before its completion is applied — so only a completion forged on the pipeline channel reaches them, which is what the
witnesses send.

**The class — every check an injected message meets before the injector refuses it, and every test leaning on the
injection path for a property it cannot fail on.** In `accepts`: the poisoned fold, the live table, the binding, the
cancelled flag, the fold check, then the injector. `admit_invocation` refuses an injected request at its first arm, so its
binding and fold checks were already proved on the pipeline channel (`a_pipeline_whose_identity_closed_is_granted_nothing_before_it_is_stopped`);
`end_invocation` holds an injected end as not current, and a duplicate end reaches the ledger as a pipeline's would — ST-02's
duplicate `Ended` and the stale duplicates (the live-table check) are origin-blind; `SnapshotEnd` refuses an injected end.
The two tests that lean on the injection path — `stale_duplicate_and_mismatched_completions_are_discarded_with_a_warning_and_counted`
and the seeded `interleaving::injected_duplicates_at_seeded_points_release_nothing_twice_and_change_nothing_durable` (ST-05) —
counted discards, which the injector alone satisfies; each now asserts which check refused each injected completion: the
binding refuses alpha's two (another generation, another attempt) and the injector only beta's own; in ST-05 every
completion injected under another live pipeline's identity (`duplicating`'s `crossed`) must carry the binding's warning,
seed by seed, with at least one injected over the seeds run. Both are red under `sc2-m-bind` and `sc2-m-both`. **The audit
mutations** of the other checks — the first two in copies of the start tree (`proof/mutants-start/`), the third in a copy
of the repaired one (`proof/mutants-r5/`): the cancelled check removed —
`a_shutdown_cancels_every_live_pipeline_and_ends_the_command_resumably`,
`a_shutdown_releases_each_invocation_once_whether_pending_unstarted_running_or_finished` and
`a_shutdown_with_pipelines_in_flight_is_settled_by_the_next_resume_whose_ledgers_start_empty` fail, real pipelines'
completions after their cancellation (`audit-m-cancelled`); the injector's own refusal removed — the stale test never ends,
beta's live pipeline retired by the forged completion carrying its own identity and its real worker stranded in the
scheduler's runner (`audit-m-injector`, the binary killed at 302 s of its life, `summary.txt` says so); the poisoned check
removed — nothing fails (`audit-m-poisoned`: 105 of 105), because at the coordinator it is doubly redundant: the append
error's interrupt cancels every live pipeline in the step the error returns to, and a poisoned fold holds no identity open
(`Standing::of` reads `Holds::Nothing`), so the cancelled and fold checks each refuse what it would, and the property, no
completion applied to a poisoned fold, holds with any one of the three removed. Noted, not a missing proof.

**On Windows** (R-AY) ST-05 ran seed 0 alone, whose nine injections include no crossed completion (each seed's counts:
`proof/windows-subset/st05-crossed-per-seed/test.log` — seeds 1, 2, 3, 4 and 7 inject one or two). A claim made of some
seed runs there with the seed that makes it on Linux (R-AY's rule), so `114067f3` gives ST-05 seed 1 (nine injections, two
crossed). With `scheduled_here` forced to the Windows subsets the test passes, and fails under `sc2-m-bind` (0 refused by
the binding against 2 crossed) (`proof/windows-subset/st05-on-windows-schedules-seed1{,-m-bind}/`); with seed 0 it failed
its "some seed" assertion (`st05-on-windows-schedules/`, before the change).

#### `FULL-SC-3` — the halting-budget-drain witness never tests cancellation of remaining live work

**The finding** (scope lens, verbatim in part): "`coordinator.rs:4796` shuts down all pipelines before planting
`budget_exceeded` and the halting settlement. It then restarts the coordinator to close the already-settled prefix. This
proves outcome precedence, but not the contract's conversion back to closure step (2), which must cancel remaining
attempts and verifications. I changed the halt branch to run only when `budget_stop().is_none()`, disabling cancellation
specifically during a budget drain. **All 78 coordinator tests still passed** … A replacement witness plants the halting
settlement after the budget stop while another worker remains live."

**The witness**, `a_halting_settlement_drained_after_a_budget_stop_cancels_the_live_worker_and_ends_halted_at_width_three`
(the lens's, kept and sharpened): four tasks at width three under the tight ceiling; alpha crosses it and
`budget_exceeded` is recorded with beta's and gamma's workers inside the Runner; the scheduler then drains a halting
settlement of beta's (`Release::Append` — R-AH says why a halting drain settlement can only be planted in this build) while
gamma's worker is still live. It asserts that gamma's worker was live at the drain, that it is cancelled through the
Runner, that the closure settles gamma's attempt `attempt_interrupted` and nothing else, that nothing is published, that
the run finishes Halted at beta, that the ledger balances and that the live fold equals its log's replay. **Green at the
start head**; **the mutation** `sc3-m-halt-not-in-drain` (the lens's) turns it red, and nothing else, at both heads: gamma's
worker runs on (`Completed`) and the command fails on gamma's next snapshot request. The planted-prefix test keeps its
packet name (T-FINISH's `run_finished_budget_exceeded_refused_after_halting_drain_settlement`), and its notes now say it
proves outcome precedence only.

**The root cause.** A test-shape gap, not a code defect: phase 4 planted the drained prefix on a shut-down run, where the
halt it planted had nothing left to cancel, and R-AH pointed the conversion at `halt_cancels_in_flight_attempt_at_width_three`,
which has no budget stop — so the one condition that distinguishes a drain was never under a test.

**The class — every coordinator test of a closure or drain sentence, and whether the work its sentence speaks of is live
when the step it names runs** (`measure/sc3-class-search.txt`): one member, the planted prefix; the halt, verification,
retained-generation, deferred-item, budget-drain and closure-fault tests run on live work, the constructed publication
and promotion tests claim completion rather than cancellation (R-AG), and the T-FINISH refusals are the fold's.

#### `FULL-CONC-1` — an append error releases running invocations before their termination is established

**The finding** (concurrency lens, verbatim in part): "at width three, beta and gamma hold their worker slots. Alpha
finishes; the coordinator's `candidate_prepared` append fails. The append-error discharge immediately marks both running
registrations cancelled and releases their pairs. Only afterward does the coordinator cancel their pipelines. Beta reports
`CancelledUnresolved`; `InvocationLedger::keep` treats that report as a duplicate because its registration was already
cancelled. A test-only witness against unchanged production code reproduced: `running=[], holders=[], balanced=true,
cancelled=2, duplicates=2`, despite confirming beta's unresolved termination. … R-AI explicitly acknowledges early
release; its justification that only the duplicate count changes does not hold for this case."

**The witness**, `an_append_error_releases_a_running_invocation_only_when_its_end_establishes_its_process_gone` (the
lens's, sharpened): the phase-4 append error at `candidate_prepared` (a flush error after the full line), with beta's
cancelled worker reported unresolved by the double. It asserts that the Runner established gamma's termination and not
beta's, that the command's end names beta, and that the ledger holds `(running [beta], pair holders [beta], balances
false, gamma settled, duplicates 0)`. **Red at the start head** (`repro/start-head/`): `([], [], true, true, 2)` — the
lens's numbers.

**The root cause.** Obligation (3)'s ledger half, `InvocationLedger::cancel_all_running`, settled every running
registration at the protocol, and the coordinator's broker ran the same discharge as the synchronous substrate's. At
width 1 that is sound: the owner runs every invocation itself, so when it appends nothing it holds is running a process.
At the coordinator the processes run on pipelines, and only each pipeline's end report establishes whether its process is
gone.

**The repair.** The ledger knows how it learns an end (`Ends`): `Inline` for an owner that runs every invocation itself
(the synchronous substrate, the pre-flight, recovery — every ledger built before), `Reported` for one the coordinator's
pipelines report into. `InvocationLedger::for_pipelines` and `PermitBroker::for_pipelines` build the latter, and
`TopologyRun::limit_slots` — the coordinator's entry, before anything is admitted — installs it. The discharge
(`UncancelledAppend::cancelling`) calls `cancel_after_append_error`: an inline ledger settles everything as before
(`cancel_all_running`); a pipelines' ledger withdraws the waiting requests — no process of them ran — and settles no running
one. The coordinator cancels the pipelines in the same step (`fail`), each running registration is then settled once by
its own end report, and an unresolved one is kept with its pair (round R1's rule): the ledger reports no release and no
balance it has not observed. Nothing can be granted meanwhile: the waiting requests are gone, the fold is poisoned, and
the interrupt refuses every request (R3). No call site of the protocol changes — the ledger the caller hands over decides
— so R-AI's alternative, "a second discharge path through all of them", was not needed. The phase-4 append-error test now
asserts no duplicate settlement where it asserted two, and a ledger test holds both kinds side by side
(`identity::tests::a_pipelines_ledger_leaves_each_running_invocation_to_the_end_that_establishes_its_process_gone`).

**The mutations** (`proof/mutants-r5/`): `conc1-m-pipelines-ledger-settles-running` (a pipelines' ledger discharging like
an inline one) turns red the witness, the phase-4 append-error test and the ledger test; `conc1-m-coordinator-broker-inline`
(the coordinator's broker built inline) the witness and the phase-4 test. Nothing else fails under either.

**The class — every settlement of a running registration not made by the end report of its own process**
(`measure/conc1-class-search.txt`): the discharge (repaired), reached from every emitting call site; `cancel_in_flight`
(between synchronous Runner calls, its one caller a test — an inline settlement by construction, listed); the synchronous
registrar's and `end_invocation`'s ends; `abandoned` (a grant never delivered: no process started); `stop`, `cancel_all`
and `shut_down` (waiting requests only); `register_at_once`'s withdrawal (a request it has just made). After the repair
`cancel_all_running` is reached only through an inline ledger.

#### `FULL-REG-1` — an apostrophe in `TMPDIR` breaks the new reaper test

**The finding** (regression lens, verbatim in part): "On Linux or macOS, set `TMPDIR` to an existing writable directory
such as `/tmp/reviewer's-temp` … `HostHeld` interpolates the resulting PID-file path into: `echo $$ > '<path>'; exec sleep
600`. The apostrophe breaks the shell quoting. No PID markers are written, so the parent waits until its 120-second bound
and fails before exercising the reaper guarantee. **Executed:** passing the exact source template to `sh -n -c` succeeds
with `/tmp/review` and exits 2 with `/tmp/reviewer's-temp` … The resulting Rust-test failure is **reasoned**, not
executed. … this also violates standards §9's prohibition on concatenating values into shell text."

**The witness** is the test itself, given a pid directory whose own name carries an apostrophe and a space (`the reaper's
pids`), so the condition holds on every run whatever `TMPDIR` is. **Red at the start head** — executed, where the lens
reasoned it: "three pipeline processes started within 120s", after 120.02 s (`repro/start-head-reg1/`). **The repair:**
`HostHeld` hands the shell the path as `$1` (`sh -c 'echo $$ > "$1"; exec sleep 600' held <path>`), never inside its
program text. Green (`proof/green-218476b6/`). **The mutation** `reg1-m-path-in-program-text` (the start head's
`format!` restored) turns the test red alone, at its 120 s bound (`proof/mutants-r5/`).

**The class.** Statically, every line PR11 adds under `src/` that builds text a shell or another interpreter parses, or a
command line (`measure/reg1-class-search.txt`, 21,854 added lines): one value spliced into shell text — this one; the
probe's `sh -c "exit 0"` is constant; everything else passes values as argv elements or environment, or writes data
files. Executed, PR11's test modules under `TMPDIR=…/reviewer's-temp` at the start head and after the repair
(`measure/apostrophe-tmpdir/SUMMARY.txt`): at the start head the reaper test fails with the two start-red witnesses and two
real-Docker tests; after it only those two real-Docker tests fail, and neither is PR11's. One,
`runner::container::exec::tests::real_docker_confines_a_gate_to_its_mount`, splices paths into its gate's shell script the
same way — lines from `e80564d8` (2026-09-02), an ancestor of PR11's base — and is filed as
`PR11-R5-REAL-DOCKER-GATE-SCRIPT-SPLICES-PATHS` (P3, §6); the other is a Unix socket path past `sun_path`'s 108 bytes under
this run's long `TMPDIR` (112 bytes; 69 under the lens's own), not quoting.

#### `FULL-SC-4` — the overshoot finding understates the bound

**The finding** (scope lens, verbatim in part): "The finding:19 says the two remaining pipelines overshoot by 'up to two
invocations' spend.' The body's ledger similarly describes a bound of `max_parallel` live invocations. Concrete
counterexample: width three, two reviewers per task; alpha crosses the ceiling while beta and gamma's workers remain
running. After durable `budget_exceeded`, beta and gamma each start both review passes: **four additional agent
invocations** … Describe the exposure as the unknown remaining spend of admitted pipelines, including subsequent reviews
and reasks." The lens's run recorded the four: `k1.g0.a1.review_pass0.o0`, `k1.g0.a1.review_pass1.o0`,
`k2.g0.a1.review_pass0.o0`, `k2.g0.a1.review_pass1.o0` (`full-witnesses/scope/scope-witnesses-at-head.log`).

**The repair.** `PR11-OVERSHOOT-BOUND-IN-NO-OUTPUT`'s failure sequence and remedy now state the exposure as the remaining
spend of every pipeline admitted before the stop, its later review passes and re-asks included, which no count of
invocations bounds, and say what it said before (`c564273b`). **The class — every statement of the overshoot's size**
(`measure/sc4-class-search.txt`): the coordinator's notes ("the unknown spend of at most `max_parallel` live pipelines,
each running one invocation at a time"), R-AB here, §11, and the body's ledger row ("bounded by max_parallel live
invocations"). The notes' `Budget overshoot` section is rewritten (`218476b6`) and pinned by
`the_overshoot_notes_bound_the_remaining_spend_of_every_pipeline_live_at_the_stop`, **red against the notes at the start
head** at its first pin (`repro/start-head/`) and green after; as for round R2's notes findings, the start head's notes
are the defect revived and no further mutation is added. R-AB and §11 are corrected above with this round's mark, and the
body's row with the body. **The shape, executed:**
`a_budget_stop_drains_live_pipelines_to_their_settlements_and_ends_budget_exceeded` now asserts that beta and gamma, each
running its worker when the stop is recorded, start their review passes after `budget_exceeded` (`Ran::durable_at_spawn`);
it is green at the start head too — the behaviour was always so, and only the sentences understated it.

#### `FULL-SC-1` — the Unix reaper's container half is not wired at the coordinator

**The finding** (scope lens, verbatim in part): "Record R-AR substitutes next-command census cleanup for the packet's Unix
requirement: the surviving reaper kills the dead coordinator's labeled containers, closing the orphan window. ST-16(d),
included in PR11's concurrent proof obligations, requires that behavior. Concrete sequence: start three container
pipelines → kill the coordinator on Unix → issue no further write command. Nothing registers its container scope or keeps
a supervisor alive across those invocations. The containers can continue editing and spending indefinitely. The executed
width-three test explicitly asserts the contrary of the required cleanup: `coordinator.rs:8681`, 'its three containers
outlive it'." The triage: pre-existing (its first bad commit is PR7's `TopologyRun`, which the census notes named as the
scope's registrar), and PR11's contract under concurrency per the review of record.

**Measured, before any change** (`~/orch-pr11/questions/pr11_repair_r5-1.md` has it whole). Registering the scope needs no
instrument: `set_container_reclaim_scope` is an existing reachable fn, classified `effect_free`. Keeping a reaper alive
across a container invocation does: reapers are forked only by the Process funnel's private `termination::Supervisor::begin`,
once per host process launch, and a `Supervisor` cannot be held for a run's life — `begin` takes the process-wide launch
claim (`claim_launch`: no other launch proceeds while `spawning` is held) until its group registers. A run-lifetime reaper is
therefore a new reachable fn in `src/agent/proc.rs`, a `CLASSIFIED_MODULES` file, with its `effects/wrappers.toml` rows — the
brief's stop condition. The round asked the orchestrator (`questions/pr11_repair_r5-1.md`) with the design, the two
instrument edits it needs, and the options; this subsection is completed with the answer.

#### Contention, the rest of the proof

**Under contention** (round C1's method: every test process pinned by `taskset` to CPUs 30 and 31, one test thread, a
fresh `TMPDIR` per process; `tools/contend.sh`, `tools/contend-module.sh`, `tools/contend-r5.sh`) on the binary whose `src/`
is `218476b6`'s (`bins/r5-fixed-lib`, sha256 `c2dcb747…` in each `run.txt`; `proof/green-218476b6/provenance.txt`). The
round's seven new tests with the two strengthened scripted ones — the three channel witnesses, the halting drain, the append
error, the notes pin, the ledger test, the stale test and the budget drain — four loops of fifty: **200 of 200 runs exit 0
and each of the nine passes in all 200** (`proof/contention-218476b6-witnesses/summary.txt`; libtest's "finished in" median
1.39 s, min 1.26 s, max 1.53 s, `durations.txt`). The heavier touched tests — the seeded ST-05, the phase-4 append error, the
broker ledgers at every end, the reaper test — four loops of ten: **40 of 40 runs exit 0 and each passes in all 40**
(`proof/contention-218476b6-heavier/summary.txt`; median 17.94 s, min 17.49 s, max 18.49 s). The whole coordinator module, four loops,
stopped once twenty runs had completed — each run is 84 tests at one thread, about three minutes with four processes on
two CPUs — and the four in flight then not counted: **21 of 21 completed runs exit 0 and each of the module's 84 tests
passes in all 21**, its three ignored children spawned only by their tests (`proof/contention-218476b6-module/summary-completed.txt`;
median 169.65 s, min 126.22 s, max 197.72 s).
None of round R5's seven new tests sleeps or waits on a clock: the channel witnesses drive an assembled coordinator one
message at a time, the ledger test and the pin are pure, and the two scripted ones run under the deterministic scheduler,
whose waits are bounded handshakes.
